![обложка|156](<../attachments/vi%E1%B9%A3%E1%B9%87u-sahasra-n%C4%81ma-stotra-bs--%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B6%D0%BA%D0%B0--4ea9e8a2015dc47b.jpeg>)

Viṣṇu Sahasra Nāma Stotra is famous, and many commentaries have been written to explain the meaning of the names of Viṣṇu. Baladeva Vidyābhūṣana’s commentary is unique for explaining the names in terms of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. This follows from the proposition that Kṛṣṇa includes all other forms of the Lord. Thus all names of the Lord are Kṛṣṇa's names.

# Viṣṇu Sahasra Nāma Stotra (BS)

### Verse 1

*śrī vaiśaṁpāyana uvāca*  
*śrutvā dharmān aśeṣeṇa pāvanāni ca sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*yudhiṣṭhiraḥ śānta-navaṁ punar evābhyabhyāṣata \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Śrī Vaiśampāyana said: After hearing plentifully about dharma and those objects that purify, Yudhiṣṭhira again asked Bhīṣma, the abode of everlasting peace.**

**Комментарий:**

I offer respects to Śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-candra.

May the cause of the universe, the supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, endowed with many forms and his three śaktis, the one ocean of unlimited auspicious qualities, all pervading person, full of knowledge and bliss, dear to the worshipper, bestow auspiciousness upon us.

I praise Vyāsa, the son of Satyavatī, the guru of the sages, who is directly Nārāyaṇa. I surrender to Vaiśampāyana, who took pleasure in the nectar of pronouncing the name Acyuta. I offer respects to Bhīṣma, son of Gaṅgā, dear to Kṛṣṇa, who is the best of those who know all persons requesting fulfilment of desires. I offer respect to all present in the assembly, who relished the topics.

May Murāri, the essence of consciousness, reside at all times in our hearts! By his mercy the king of the elephants became sinless and ended material existence.

The names of Kṛṣṇa, which fulfill the desires of the reciter, which Śeṣa utters out of ever fresh bliss in all his mouths simultaneously, remain ever victorious.

I offer respects to the devotees of the Lord, who take the Lord’s name as their only goal. By their mercy, there will arise nectar in the meanings of names.

*śampāṁśa-tad-rudām āhur ayanaṁ patanaṁ tataḥ*  
*vigataṁ śabdo ‘to yasmāt tad vaiśampāyanaḥ smṛtaḥ*

**Перевод:**

***Śampā*** **refers to the sound of thunder.** ***Ayanam*** **means to fall. Thus Vaiśampāyaṇa is so called because he was born with the appearance (*****vigata*****) of the sound of thunder.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way Vaiśamāyana is glorified in the Purāṇas. He was the dear disciple of Vyāsa and a sage. Though not requested to recite these verses of praise, he speaks to King Janamejaya out of friendship and mercy, starting with this verse, because the verses were most excellent.

Yudhiṣṭhira heard from Bhīṣma completely (*aśeṣataḥ*) about dharmas-- a king’s dharma as well as others’ dharmas. He heard about tīrthas (*pāvanāni*) and other purifying objects completely. He again inquired from Bhīṣma (*śānta-navam*), an abode of peace.

### Verse 2

*śrī yudhiṣṭhira uvāca*  
*kim ekaṁ daivataṁ loke kiṁ vāpy ekaṁ parāyaṇaṁ \|*  
*stuvantaḥ kaṁ kam arcantaḥ prāpnuyur mānavāḥ śubham \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira said:**  
**According to the scriptures, who is the one object of worship and who is the one object of auspiciousness? Men will attain auspiciousness by praising whom and by worshipping whom?**

**Комментарий:**

“Our grandfather, Bhīṣma, lying on a bed of arrows, is endowed with all dharmas such as brahmacārya and has studied all Upaniṣads and Vedas along with their limbs. By the blessing of the Lord, he is free of all pain from weapons. He is omniscient, having knowledge of all material and spiritual topics by his all-seeing ability. Having being asked already generally about the highest truth that should be known, Bhīṣma will speak.” Thinking in this way, Yudhiṣṭhira inquires.

In asking these questions, the phrase “according to you” and “by scriptures” (*loke*) should be attached to each question, changed suitably according to the grammar. *Loke* means that by which the truth is seen—by the Vedas and other scriptures.

According to you, who is the chief object of worship, as described in the scriptures?

According to you, who is the chief (*ekam*) object endowed with all fortune (*parāyanam*)? That is the second question.

Praising and worshipping which deity will mankind attain auspiciousness? According to you, man will attain the highest good by worshipping and glorifying the actions and qualities of which person? These are the third and fourth questions.

### Verse 3

*ko dharmaḥ sarva-dharmāṇāṁ bhavataḥ paramo mataḥ \|*  
*kiṁ japan mucyate jantur janma-saṁsāra-bandhanāt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**What is the highest dharma among all dharmas, according to scripture and according to you? By which type of chanting will the jīva become free from the bondage of birth and saṁsāra?**

**Комментарий:**

Among all the dharmas spoken by you, which is the highest dharma, which, without effort, gives results, according to you, who are omniscient? This is the fifth question.

By what type of chanting will the jīva (*jantuḥ*) become free from the bondage of birth and saṁsāra? Birth indicates being born and falling into hell. These are effects. Saṁsāra is the ancient karmas, which are the cause. By these two, one becomes bound up. This obstructs attaining the highest goal. What frees one from this bondage, according to you? This is the sixth question.

### Verse 4

*śrī bhīṣma uvāca*  
*jagat-prabhuṁ devadevam anantaṁ puruṣottamam \|*  
*stuvan nāma-sahasreṇa puruṣaḥ satatotthitaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Bhīṣma said:**

**The person who is constantly absorbed in praising the most excellent, infinite person, who is worshipped by all devatās and is the master of all living entities in the universe, (surpasses all suffering.)**

**Комментарий:**

Having being asked generally about six topics, Bhīṣma answers the first question among them in detail in one line. The chief object of worship (*daivatam*) is the master of the universe which is composed of moving and non-moving entities, who is worshiped by all the devatās (*deva-devam*), who is unlimited (*anantam*) in qualities and powers as well as his svarūpa, and who is most excellent among all persons either in bondage or liberation (*puruṣottamam*). Skipping the order, out of enthusiasm for that present topic, he proceeds to the third question in the second line.The person desiring the highest goal, being constantly engaged (*satata utthitaḥ*), praises the highest person by the thousand names which will be described. He glorifies the highest person’s qualities and activities. This answers the sixth question.This verse is connected with the statement “He thus surpasses all suffering” in verse 6.

### Verse 5

*tam eva cārcayan nityaṁ bhaktyā puruṣam avyayam \|*  
*dhyāyan stuvan namasyaṁś ca yajamānas tam eva ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Worshipping the eternal Lord constantly with faith, meditating on him, praising him and offering respects, the worshipper (surpasses all suffering.)**

**Комментарий:**

He answers the fourth question.The worshiper (*yajamānaḥ*), praising Kṛṣṇa (*puruṣam*) and worshipping him with faith (*bhaktyā*) constantly, surpasses all suffering (verse 6). Bhakti means service or a figurative meaning, as well as faith, division, and a roundabout way of speaking, according to Haima-koṣa. How does one praise the Lord? One praises him by worshipping him—by offering him foot water and other items. He concentrates his mind upon the Lord’s form (*dhyāyan*) and offers respects with his eight limbs (*namasyan*). The Lord is described. He has eternal form, qualities, powers and pastimes (*avyayam*). It is not a fault to repeat the word *tam* in the verse, since it is used in different statements.

### Verse 6

*anādi nidhanaṁ viṣṇuṁ sarvaloka-maheśvaram \|*  
*lokādhyakṣaṁ stuvan nityaṁ sarva-duḥkhātigo bhavet \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Praising the all-pervading Lord who is the eternal shelter, who is the great lord of all planets and their leaders, and who is the witness of all beings, one surpasses all suffering for all time.**

**Комментарий:**

While two verses speak of the practical, favorable result, they describe the nature of the Lord. Praising the Lord, one surpasses all suffering, beyond birth and death (*nityam*). That is because the Lord is all pervading (*viṣṇum*) and is thus the great lord (*maheśvaram*) of all planets and their controllers (*sarva-loka*), and he is the witness (*adhyakṣam*) of all the planets and their controllers. The word *anādī* indicates that he is beyond limitations of time. The word *viṣṇum* (all pervading) indicates that he is beyond limitations of space. Being beyond limitations of time and space, he can easily control all planets and their leaders. Praising this person who thus suitable to be praised and worshipped, since he knows everything, and can thus fulfil all the desires of those who utter praises, a person can surpass the three miseries. This means he attains Kṛṣṇa, the ocean of bliss, and becomes blissful. This means he becomes liberated.

The first verse mentioned uttering the names of the Lord while performing worship. However, the last verse mentions just uttering the names.

*sakṛd uccāritaṁ yena harir ity akṣara-dvayam*  
*baddha-parikaras tena mokṣāya gamanaṁ prati*

**By once chanting the holy name of the Lord, which consists of the two syllables ha-ri, a person in bondage comes liberated. Skanda Purāṇa**

*sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā*  
*bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma*

**O best of the Bhārgavas! If any person chants the name offenselessly even once, be it with śraddhā, or with derisive indifference, he will be delivered. Mahābhārata**

Though liberation is hard to attain by visiting tīrthas, austerities, samādhi and other means, which must be performed constantly for a long time, the scriptures glorify the name by saying that just by uttering the name this is achieved. The knower of the Vedas who does not know the power of the name is merely an animal. To think otherwise would contradict the smṛti which says *tathārthavādo harināmni kalpanam*: it is an offense to interpret the name or think its glories are exaggeration.

### Verse 7

*brahmaṇyaṁ sarva-dharma-jñaṁ lokānāṁ kīrti-vardhanam \|*  
*loka-nāthaṁ mahad-bhūtaṁ sarva-bhūta-bhavodbhavam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One should praise the Lord, who protects the brāhmaṇas and devatās, who knows all dharmas, who increases the fame of those who worship him, who is the lord of all planets, who is worshipped because he is first to exist, and who is the cause of Brahmā and Śiva.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord is the protector of the brāhmaṇas, devatās and religious practice (*brahmaṇyam*). He knows all dharmas mentioned in the Vedas, such as fire sacrifices and darśa-paurṇamāsa, as well as worship of the Lord, meditation, offering respects and verses of praise. This means that he gives the results for these actions. He spreads the fame (*kīrti-vardhanam*) of persons who undertake these dharmas mentioned in the Vedas by giving wealth, knowledge, detachment etc. appropriate to the actions. For whatever is desired, he should be requested by all people, including Brahmā and Śiva. He rules over all people (*loka-nātham*). Or he is respected by all people since *nāth* means to respect, to request, to give pain, to rule over and to bless, according to Dhātu-paṭha.

*yuga-koṭi-sahasrāṇi viṣnum āraḍhya padmabhūḥ*  
*punas trailokya-dhātṛtvaṁ prāptavān iti śuśruma*  
*mahā-devaḥ sarvam edhe mahātmā hutyātmānaṁ deva devo babhuva*  
*viśvāl lokān vyāpya viṣṭabhya kīrtyā virājate dyutimān kṛtti-vāsā*

**We have heard that Brahmā attained control of the three worlds by worshipping Viṣṇu for millions of yugas. Offering oblations to the Lord, the great soul Śiva became strong, and became lord of the devatās. Spreading over the worlds, fixing himself there, he remains shining with fame. Mahābhārata**

*etau tau vibudha-śṛeṣṭau prasāda-krodha-jāv ubhau*  
*tadā darśita-panthānau sṛṣti-saṁhāra-kārau*

**Brahmā and Śiva, born by mercy and anger, the best of the devatās, having been shown the method, became the creator and destroyer of the universe. Mahābhārata**

This is explained elsewhere. The Lord is worshipped by all persons because he existed previous to them (*mahad-bhūtam*). This is then explained. He is the source (*udbhavam*) of Brahmā and Śiva, who give birth to all beings (*sarva-bhūta-bhava*).

*prajāpatiṁ ca rudraṁ cāpy aham eva sṛjāmi vai*  
*tau hi māṁ na vijānīto mama māyāvimohitau*

**I create Brahmā and Śiva. Bewildered by my māyā, they do not know me. Mahābhārata.**

### Verse 8

*eṣa me sarva-dharmāṇāṁ dharmo ‘dhikatamo mataḥ \|*  
*yad bhaktyā puṇḍarīkākṣaṁ stavair arcen naraḥ sadā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**I consider the highest among all dharmas to be that by which mankind will devotedly worship Kṛṣṇa by praising him with verses.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse answers the fifth question. Among all dharmas mentioned by me, that dharma is considered by me (Bhīṣma) to be the best. What is that dharma? It is that dharma in which men worship Kṛṣṇa (p*uṇḍarīkāksam*) with devotion at all times, by praises, uttering his names. Bhīṣma considers worship consisting of chanting Kṛṣṇa’s names with faith to be the highest dharma. Bhakti indicates that worship of devatās is distasteful, like drinking bitter medicine.

*Puṇḍarīkāṣa* (Kṛṣṇa with beautiful eyes) indicates that praise of devatās includes describing their fearful and distorted forms. “Praising” indicates that this action is superior to jyotiṣṭoma sacrifices, performed with great difficulty. The word person (*naraḥ*) in the singular indicates that this method is not limited to twice born individuals and that many prerequisites are not necessary. This method can be performed at all times (*sadā*), without dependence on place or time. Some say that *bhaktyā* means “with many types,” since the names are unlimited. Bhīṣma indicates that the names of the Lord are most excellent because they indicate the form of the Lord.

*oṁ āsya jānanto nāma cid vivaktan mahas te viṣṇo su-matiṁ bhajāmahe oṁ tat sat*

**You, who know the Lord’s name oṁ is spiritual, should chant it*****. Ṛg Veda*** **1.156.3**

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ \|*  
*pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto ’bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ \|\|*

**The name of the Lord is cintāmaṇi. It is Kṛṣṇa himself, a form of consciousness and rasa. It is complete, pure, and eternally liberated since there is no difference between the name and Kṛṣṇa himself. Padma Purāṇa**

Thus the śakti of the Lord is in his names.

*avaśenāpi yan-nāmni kīrtite sarva-pātakaiḥ*  
*pumān vimucyate sadyaḥ siṁha-trastair mṛgair iva*

**If one chants the holy name of the Lord, even in a helpless condition or without desiring to do so, one is delivered from all hellish conditions, just as when a lion roars, all the small animals flee in fear. Viṣṇu Purāna**

*ayaṁ hi kṛta-nirveśo janma-koṭy-aṁhasām api*  
*yad vyājahāra vivaśo nāma svasty-ayanaṁ hareḥ*

**This person has performed atonement for the sins of ten million births since he has spontaneously chanted the auspicious name of the Lord. SB** [**6.2.7**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/2.7)

The scriptures speak of attaining liberation by going on pilgrimage etc. which is difficult to perform. But liberation attained by a person chanting, who does not even know the power of the name.

*japas tu sarva-dharmebhaḥ paramo dharma ucyate*  
*ahiṁsayā ca bhūtānāṁ japa-yajñaḥ pravartate*

**Japa is the supreme dharma among all dharmas. One undertakes the sacrifice of japa with non-violence towards all beings. Mahābhārata**

Violence is against dharma. By chanting the name of the Lord, non-violence is accomplished. This shows the greatness of the name. This is not an example of chanting in ignorance (previous quotation).

### Verse 9

*paramaṁ yo mahattejaḥ paramaṁ yo mahat-tapaḥ \|*  
*paramaṁ yo mahad-brahma paramaṁ yaḥ parāyaṇam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**He is the object of worship by his supreme position (*****paramam*****), is the greatest power, giving power to all powerful objects like the sun (*****mahat tejaḥ*****), and is the excellent goal of such powerful persons (*****mahat-tapaḥ*****).**

**Комментарий:**

*bhīṣāsmād vātaḥ pavate bhīsodeti sūryaḥ bhīṣāsmād*  
*agniś cendraś ca mṛtyur dhāvati pañcamaḥ*

**Out of fear of him the wind blows. Out of fear of him the sun rises. Out of fear of him fire, Indra and death, the fifth, run. Kaṭha Upaniṣad** [**2.3.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.3.3)

He who is most powerful (*paramam*) and who is the supreme brahman is the object endowed with all auspiciousness (*parāyanam*).

### Verse 10

*pavitrāṇāṁ pavitraṁ yo maṁgalānāṁ ca maṅgalam \|*  
*daivataṁ devatānāṁ ca bhūtānāṁ yo ‘vyayaḥ pitā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**He is the purest of the pure and most auspicious among auspicious items. He is the most worthy of worship among worshippable beings. He is the indestructible father of all beings.**

**Комментарий:**

As if glancing backward like a lion, the first question is again answered. (Who is the supreme object of worship?) He is the purest among pure items which destroy sin, such as the Gaṅgā. He gives these items the power to destroy sin. He is the most auspicious among persons who destroy obstacles, such as Ganeśa. He gives them the power to destroy obstacles. He is the supreme object of worship (*daivatam*) among all worshippable beings such as Brahmā. They have their positions because of a relationship with him and because they worship him. He is the origin and protector (*pitā*) of all beings. He is indestructible (*avyayaḥ*). This distinguishes him from recent fathers.

### Verse 11

*yataḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni bhavanty ādi-yugāgame \|*  
*yasmiṁśca pralayaṁ yānti punar eva yuga-kṣaye \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**From him arise all beings at the beginning of the first yuga, and into him they enter at the end of at final destruction, with destruction of the yugas.**

**Комментарий:**

His unique nature is shown. All beings arise from him at the first yuga and enter him at the destruction of yugas, at the time of final destruction. The word *ca* indicates the middle period. This means that all beings are protected by the Lord. Being the cause of the universe indicates his nature as the supreme Lord. Vyāsa says *janmādy asya yataḥ*: from him comes the creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe. (Brahma-sūtra [1.1.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/1.1.2))

### Verse 12

*tasya loka-pradhānasya jagannāthasya bhūpate \|*  
*viṣṇor nāma-sahasraṁ me śrṇu papa-bhayāpaham \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O king! Hear from me the thousand names of Viṣṇu, who is the Lord mentioned in scriptures and the person to be approached by all beings in the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

O Yudhiṣṭhira (*bhūpate*)! Hear from me the thousand names of Viṣṇu, who has been described above (*tasya*). Those names are described: they destroy (*apaham*) sin and fear. *Apaham* comes from the verb *han.* Viṣṇu is described. He is the lord of all beings (*pradhānasya*), as mentioned in the scriptures (*loka*). He should thus be requested (*nāthasya*) by the beings within the universe (*jagat*) such as devatās and humans, since he can bestow the goal of life.

### Verse 13

*yāni nāmāni gauṇāni vikhyātāni mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*ṛṣibhiḥ parigītāni tāni vakṣyāmi bhūtaye \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**I will narrate the names sung of the Lord by the sages, which describe the Lord’s qualities and are famous, in order that one can attain the human goals.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse explains that all the names of Viṣṇu cannot be recited (since they are many). The names arise because of the Lord’s qualities (*gauṇāni*). This also indicates his activities. Thus they are not just proper names like Ḍittha. Among the names the famous names (*vikhyātāni*) of Viṣṇu (*mahātmanaḥ*) will be recited. These names are recited by sages, who realized the names through mantras, because the names are spiritual, for attaining the four human goals (*bhūtaye*).

The following should be understood. Like a vaidurya gem, Kṛṣṇa, also called parabrahman, shines as eternal Nārāyaṇa and Vāsudeva.

*eko vaśī sarvagaḥ kṛṣṇa īḍya*  
*eko’pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti*

**Kṛṣṇa is supreme, all-pervading, and most worthy of praise. Though he is one, he is also many. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

*Kṛṣṇas tu bhagavan svayam*

**Kṛṣṇa is the final form of Bhagavān. SB 1.3.28**

He is the final form (*svayam rūpa*) since he manifests all śaktis. Generally the vilāsa form Nārāyaṇa is the same. However, his svāṁśas and kalās do not manifest all the śaktis. Vāsudeva does not contact prakṛti. Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna and Aniruddha manifest as the puruṣa avatāras Mahāviṣṇu, Garbhodaśāyī and Kṣīrodaśāyi. They give blessings to prakṛti. The līlā avatāras such as Matsya and Kūrma arise from Garbhodaśāyī. The manvantara avatāras and yuga avatāras are famous in the Purāṇas. But Kṛṣṇa is the ātmā of all these. That is the conclusion. Because of Kṛṣṇa’s blessings, learned Bhīṣma taught the names of Kṛṣṇa, endowed with his associates and abode.

Names indicating public actions he spoke clearly and names indicating confidential actions he spoke with a hidden meaning. The mukhya-vṛtti (chief meaning) of words like Brahmā signifying four headed Brahmā in usage become parama mukhya-vṛtti or the highest usage, when referring to bhagavān. Sometimes words with conventional meanings are used to indicate the Lord by a higher convention used by sages.

The derivation of names by etymology is of two types: grammatical and according to sages. As well, the derivation of some names are explained in Mahābhārata and other works. These are accepted but for fear of enlarging the work they are not mentioned. The names of Viṣṇu and others are repeated, but this is not a fault, sinces the meaning is different. Names like Śrīdhara and Mādhava, though having one meaning, are repeated because the forms are different. There is no fault in repetition since all the names are actually indicating one Lord who is the object of inquiry. This is the introduction.

### Verse 14

*viśvaṁ viṣṇur vaṣaṭkāro bhūta-bhavya-bhavat prabhuḥ \|*  
*bhūta-kṛd bhūta-bhṛd bhāvo bhūtātmā bhūta-bhāvanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**1.Viśvam: he enters everything.**  
**2. Viṣṇuḥ: he pervades everything.**  
**3. Vaṣaṭkāraḥ: he is worshipped by sacrifices.**  
**4. Bhūta-bhavya-bhavat-prabhuḥ: he is the master of all beings that have been created and that will be created.**  
**5. Bhūta-kṛt: he produces the elements.**  
**6. Bhūtabhṛt: he supports the elements.**  
**7. Bhāvaḥ: he is the world.**  
**8. Bhūtātmā: he is the cause of everything spiritual and material.**  
**9. Bhūta-bhāvanaḥ: he brings into existence all living beings.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to increase thirst for the qualities of the Lord embodying completeness and the highest powers, Bhīṣma first recites some names which are easily understood. The word *oṁ,* none other than the Lord, is uttered at the beginning and the end of the work, as in uttering mantras, since this stotra is like a mantra. Each name in the dative case followed by *namaḥ* can be uttered while offering tulasī leaves to the Lord along with pranāmas, as a puraścaraṇa (preparatory rite).

Words like *viśva* are not used as sarva-nāmas, but conjugated as viśvāya etc. Masculine, feminine and neuter words are recited, but they should be understood to modify the Lord.

*Viśvam* means he enters (*viś*) into all things other than himself. Viṣṇu means that he pervades all things.

*Vasātkāraḥ* means he is worshipped in all sacrifices. All oblations are dedicated to him.

He is the master of all beings who have been born (*bhavat*) or will be born (*bhavya*). He is the lord of all living entities in the three worlds. Some explain this as “he is the controller (*prabhuḥ*) of the three aspects of time, past, present and future.”

He produces (*kṛt*) all the elements such as ether (*bhūta*).

He maintains (*bhṛt*) the elements (*bhūta*) by his will.

Possessing the cit and acit śaktis, he becomes the world (*bhāvaḥ*). Śruti says *so ‘kāmayata bahusyāṁ prajāyeya*: he desired to become many. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad)

Because he causes everything in the spiritual and material realms by his expansions, he is called *bhūtātmā*.

He brings into being all entities and then nourishes them by his power and wealth (*bhūta-bhāvanaḥ*).

### Verse 15

*pūtātmā paramātmā ca muktānāṁ paramā gatiḥ \|*  
*avyayaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣī kṣetrajño ‘kṣara eva ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**10. Pūtātmā: he remains pure in all conditions.**  
**11. Paramātmā: he is endowed with the highest beauty and prosperity.**  
**12. Muktānāṁ paramā gatiḥ: he is the highest goal of the liberated souls.**  
**13. Avyayaḥ: he does not disappear.**  
**14. Puruṣaḥ: he gives results to the liberated souls.**  
**15. Sākṣī: he witnesses everything.**  
**16. Kṣetrajñaḥ: he knows all bodies.**  
**17. Akṣara: he is never deprived of his form and qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

He is the person devoid of material contamination, though he creates the world (*pūtātmā).*

He is the ātmā endowed with the highest, eternal beauty and prosperity (*paramātmā*). He is the supreme attainment of the jīvas who are eternally free of ignorance or who have become free of ignorance by sādhana. The word *parama* indicates that he is naturally endowed with the greatest beauty in his form, because of his powers. Because the Lord and the liberated souls are defined as separate entities (being their goal), the philosophy of complete oneness is rejected.

He does not disappear, because he has affection for the liberated souls (*avyayaḥ*). The root *vey* belongs to the *pacādi* class. (*vye* becomes *vyay + a =vyaya*, decrease).

He gives (*sanoti*) results (*puru*) to the liberated souls (*puruṣaḥ*). Other beings cannot do this.

He sees everything as if personally present (*sākṣī*).

He knows simultaneously the bodies of all the jīvas (*kṣetra-jñāḥ*).

*kṣetrāṇi hi śarīrāṇi bījaṁ cāpi śubhāśubhe*  
*tāni vetti sa yogātmā tataḥ kṣetra-jña ucyate*

**The Supreme Lord knows both the bodies, the ātmās within the bodies and their good and bad karmas. Therefore he is called the knower of all fields. Mahābhārata**

He is never deprived of his svarūpa and his svabhāva (*akṣaraḥ*).

### Verse 16

*yogo yoga-vidāṁ netā pradhāna-puruṣeśvaraḥ \|*  
*nārasiṁha-vapuḥ śrīmān keśavaḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**18. Yogaḥ: he is the shelter of samādhi**  
**19. Yogavidāṁ netā: he awards samādhī to those who practice yoga.**  
**20. Pradhāna-puruṣeśvaraḥ: he is the regulator of prakṛti and the jīvas.**  
**21. Nāra-siṁha-vapuḥ: he has the bodies of a man and a lion combined.**  
**22. Śrimān: he is endowed with great beauty.**  
**23. Keśavaḥ: he produces Brahmā and Śiva.**  
**24. Puruṣottamaḥ: he is most excellent in comparison to the jīvas.**

**Комментарий:**

He is the auspicious shelter of samādhī (*yogaḥ*). *Yoga* means the mind is concentrated (*yujyate*) on him. *Yoga* means destruction of the functions of material consciousness (*citta-vṛtti-nirodha*).He allows (*netā*) those who practice yoga (*yoga-vidām*) to attain that state.

He regulates (*īśvaraḥ*) the jīvas (*puruṣa*) and prakṛti (*pradhāna*).

He has the form of Narasiṁha in order to deliver his devotee (*nārasiṁha-vapuḥ*).

He has great beauty (*śrīmān*) though he also has the astonishing, fearful form of Narasiṁha. According to Viśva, *śrī* indicates beauty and ornamentation.

He is called Keśava because he produces Brahmā (*ka)* and Śiva (*īśa*). Śiva says:

*ka iti brahmaṇo nāma īśo ‘haṁ sarva-dehinām*  
*āvāṁ tavāṅga-sambhūtau tasmat keśava-nāma-bhāk*

**Kameans Brahmā and īśa refers to myself, Śiva, present in all beings. We two arose from your limbs. Therefore you are called Keśava. Hari-vaṁśa**

He is the most excellent (*uttamaḥ*) among all the jīvas liberated or in bondage (*puruṣa*).2

*yasmāt kṣaram atīto ’ham akṣarād api cottamaḥ \|*  
*ato ’smi loke vede ca prathitaḥ puruṣottamaḥ \|\|*

**Because I am superior to the conditioned and liberated** ***jīvas*****, I am celebrated in the** ***Vedas*** **and the** ***smṛtis*** **as the Supreme Person. BG** [**15.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.18)

### Verse 17

*sarvaḥ śarvaḥ śivaḥ sthāṇur bhūtādir nidhir avyayaḥ \|*  
*saṁbhavo bhāvano bhartā prabhavaḥ prabhur īśvaraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**25. Sarvaḥ: he goes everywhere.**  
**26. Śarvaḥ: he destroys the inauspicious.**  
**27. Śivaḥ: he is most auspicious.**  
**28. Sthāṇuḥ: he is fixed in compassion.**  
**29. Bhūtādiḥ: he is accepted as the giver of auspiciousness by all beings.**  
**30. Nidhir avyayaḥ: he is the precious jewel placed in the heart at all times.**  
**31. Sambhavaḥ: he manifests forms for his devotees.**  
**32. Bhāvanaḥ: he contemplates how to deliver his devotees.**  
**33. Bhartā: he nourishes his devotees with bliss.**  
**34. Prabhavaḥ: he gives spiritual bodies to the devotees in a remarkable way.**  
**35. Prabhuḥ: he performs feats impossible for others.**  
**36. Iśvaraḥ: he controls even Brahmā, though appearing like a human.**

**Комментарий:**

*Sarva* means he who goes or spreads (*sarati*). Arjuna says

sarvaṁ samāpnoṣi tato ‘si sarvam: since you extend over everything, you are everything. (BG [11.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/11.40))

He destroy (*śṛnosi*) everything inauspicious (*śarvaḥ*).

He possesses (*va*) all auspiciousness (*śi*) since he removes deficiency and lack of qualities (*śivaḥ*). He is most the auspicious among auspicious beings.

He is fixed in showing compassion (*sthānuḥ*).

By all beings (*bhūta*), he is accepted (*ādīyate*) as the giver of auspiciousness (*bhūtādiḥ*). *Ādi* is formed from the root *ādā* according to the rule *upasarge ghoḥ kiḥ* (Pāṇini 3.3.92)

He is placed (*nidhīyate*) in the heart like a priceless gem (*nidhi*).

Though all beings’ lives depend on him at all times, he remains complete with his parts (*avyaya*). This modifies *nidhiḥ* (*nidhir avyayaḥ*).

He manifests in forms like Matsya by his unfailing svabhāva and svarūpa, to deliver the devotees (*sambhavaḥ*).

*paritrāṇāya sādhūnāṁ vināśāya ca duṣkṛtām \|*  
*dharma-saṁsthāpanārthāya saṁbhavāmi yuge yuge \|\|*

**In every age, I appear in order to protect the devotees, to protect the devotees, destroy the demons and establish dharma. BG** [**4.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.8)**.**

He thinks (*bhāvayati*) “I will protect these devotees” (*bhāvanaḥ*).

He killed Hiraṇyakaśipu, who had a boon of not being killed, after the Lord found some loophole. He increased the glory of the Pāṇḍavas by various means. He thus nourishes the devotees by giving them bliss *(bhartā*).

He manifests bodies for the devotees in a remarkable way (*prabhavaḥ*).

*evam evaiṣa samprasādo 'smāt śarīrāt samutthāya paraṁ jyotir upasampadya svena rūpeṇābhiniṣpadyate*

**Receiving mercy, rising from his body, the jīva attains the supreme light (the Lord) and manifests his own form. Chāndogya Upaniṣad** [**8.12.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/8.12.3)

Kṛṣṇa performs actions impossible for devatās, though he appears like a human with human actions when he comes as an avatāra (*prabhuḥ*). For instance, he brought to life the sons of his guru and a brāhmaṇa. He brought to life Parīkṣit who had been burned by the brahmāstra. He bestowed a heavenly abode to Ghaṇṭakarṇa and Jara (the hunter who shot Kṛṣṇa). He gave liberation to those who died in battle.

Though dressing as a human he controls Brahmā and others (*īśvaraḥ*). Śiva says:

*īśas tvaṁ sarva-bhutānāṁ īśvaro ‘si sadā smṛtaḥ*

**You are the lord of all beings. You are always the supreme controller. Hari-vaṁśa**

### Verse 18

*svayaṁbhūḥ śaṁbhur ādityaḥ puṣkarākṣo mahāsvanaḥ \|*  
*anādi-nidhano dhātā vidhātā dhātur uttamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**37. Svayambhūḥ: he manifests by his own desire.**  
**38. Śaṁbhuḥ: he produces happiness by his qualities.**  
**39. Ādityaḥ: he resides in the sun.**  
**40. Puṣkarākṣaḥ: he spreads in the sky.**  
**41. Mahā-svanaḥ: he is the worshippable sound of the Vedas.**  
**42. Anāndi-nidhanaḥ: he is the beginingless abode.**  
**43. Dhātā: he is the support of Brahmā.**  
**44. Vidhātā: he manifests the universe.**  
**45. Dhātur uttamaḥ: he is superior to Brahmā and others.**

**Комментарий:**

He manifests by his own desire (*svayambhūḥ*). This word is found in *kavir manīṣī paribhūḥ svayambhūḥ:* he is omniscient, controlling the minds of all, the best and independent. (Iśopaniṣad 8)

He produces (*bhāvayati*) happiness (*śaṁ*) by manifesting auspicious qualities (*śambhuḥ*).

He lives in the sun, the son of Aditi (*ādityaḥ*). Āditya means the son of Aditi according to dityadityādityapatyuttara-padāṇṇyaḥ (Pāṇini 4.1.85)

*ya eṣo ‘ntarāditye hiraṇmayaḥ puruṣo dṛśyate*

**He who is within the sun is visible as the golden person. Chāndogya Upaniṣad** [**1.6.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/1.6.6)

In the sky (*puṣkara*) he spreads (*akṣnoti*) out (*puṣkarāksaḥ*). *Puṣkara* means sky according to Amara-koṣa. Śruti says *ākāśātmā*: the Lord spreads in the sky. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [3.14.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/3.14.1))

He possesses the great, worshippable (*mahān*) sound of the Vedas (*svanaḥ*).

*tasya vā etasya mahato bhūtasya niśvasitam etad yaḍ rgvedo yajurveda*

**The ṛg and yajur Vedas are the breathing of the great Lord. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad**

He is the eternal abode (*anādi-nidhanam*), since he is devoid of birth and destruction.

He is distinguished from Brahmā and others as the creator. He maintains (*dhātā*) Brahmā, the total of all jīvas in the totality of all matter.

Transforming the womb of matter, he manifests the universe (*vidhātā*).

*yo brahmāṇaṁ vidadhāti, pūrvaṁ yo vidyās tasmai jñāpayati sma kṛṣṇaḥ*

**Kṛṣṇa created Brahmā and preserved knowledge of the eighteen syllable mantra for him. Gopāla Tāpanī Upaniṣad 1.44**

*mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbhaṁ dadhāmy aham*  
*sambhavaḥ sarva-bhḷutānāṁ tato bhavati bhārata*

**The great expanse of prakṛti is my womb. In that womb, I place the seed of all jīvas. The birth of all beings comes from that, O descendent of Bharata. BG** [**14.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3)

He is superior to Brahmā and others, because he produces them (*dhātuḥ uttamaḥ*).

### Verse 19

*aprameyo hṛṣīkeśaḥ padmanābho ‘mara-prabhuḥ \|*  
*viśvakarmā manus tvaṣṭā sthaviṣṭhas sthaviro dhruvaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**46. Aprameyaḥ: he cannot be measured by anyone.**  
**47. Hṛṣīkeśaḥ: he controls everyone’s senses.**  
**48. Padmanābhaḥ: he produces Brahmā from the lotus in his navel.**  
**49. Amara-prabhuḥ: he gives devatās their abilities.**  
**50. Viśva-karmā: he resides in and assists the creators of the universe.**  
**51. Manuḥ: he knows everything.**  
**52. Tvaṣṭā: he makes living entities visible by giving them bodies.**  
**53. Sthaviṣṭaḥ: he creates many universes.**  
**54. Sthaviraḥ dhruvaḥ: he is fixed in his śaktis though he seems to be transformed.**

**Комментарий:**

Brahmā and others cannot measure him with their senses (*aprameyaḥ*). Brahmā says:

*na hy ādi-madhyāntam ajasya yasya vidmo vayaṁ sarva-mayasya dhātuḥ*

**We do not know the beginning, middle or end of the unborn Lord who is everything. Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**4.1.83**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/4.1.83)

That is because he controls (*īṣaḥ*) the senses (*hṛṣika*) of everyone. If he makes them conscious, how can they know him?

He produces Brahmā from the lotus in his navel (*padma-nābhaḥ)*

He gives the ability of Brahmā and others (*amara*) to create the world (*amara-prabhuḥ*).

He resides within the creators such as Brahmā and helps them (*viśva-karmā*).

*sa evedaṁ sarvam asṛjat*

**He alone created everything. Taittirīya Upaniṣad** [**2.6.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.6.1)

He knows everything about everything (*manuḥ*). This is derived from *man* which means to know.

He combines names and forms and thus makes the living beings visible or splendorous (*tvaṣṭā*). *Tviṣa* means splendor. This is formed according Pāṇini 6.4.12.

*sarvāṇī rūpāṇi vicitya dhīro nāmāni kṛtvābhivadan yad āste*

**I know the Lord who exists, who made all forms, and having named them, uttered those names. Taittirīya Āraṇyaka**

By his śakti, he creates many universes (*sthaviṣṭḥaḥ*).

*vistāraḥ sarva-bhūtasya viṣṇor viśvam idaṁ jagat*

**This universe belongs to Viṣṇu, the origin of all things. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

He is different from causes like earth or other elements. He remains at all time in possession of his three śaktis for all time (*sthaviraḥ*).

He is fixed in his purpose (*dhruvaḥ*). The formation of *sthavira* is similar to *ajira* or *śiśira.*

*vyatiriktaṁ na yasyāsti vyatirikto ‘khilasya ya*

**Nothing exists except the Lord, who is separate from everything. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

He has three śaktis:

*parasya śatkir vividhaiva śruyate*  
*svābhāvikī-jñana-bala-kriyā ca*  
*pradhāna-kṣetrajña-patir guṇeśaḥ*

**The Lord has various śaktis: jñāna, bala and kriyā which are natural to him. He is the lord of prakṛti and the jīvas. Śvetāśvatāra Upaniṣad**

### Verse 20

*agrāhyaḥ śāśvataḥ kṛṣṇo lohitākṣaḥ pratardanaḥ \|*  
*prabhūtas tri-kakub-dhāma pavitraṁ maṅgalam param \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**55. Agrāhyaḥ: he is not controlled by the material elements.**  
**56. Śāśvataḥ: he is eternally in charge of the universe.**  
**57. Kṛṣṇaḥ: he is the form of existence, knowledge and bliss.**  
**58. Lohitākṣaḥ: he has reddish eyes.**  
**59. Pratardanaḥ: he removes the melancholy of the devotee.**  
**60. Prabhūtaḥ: he is the lord of the spiritual sky.**  
**61. Tri-kakub-dhāma: he has an abode consisting of three parts.**  
**62. Pavitram: he purifies those who remember him.**  
**63. Maṅgalam param: he is the highest auspiciousness.**

**Комментарий:**

Though he is the material source of the universe, he is not controlled by earth or other elements (*agrāhyaḥ*). The formation of *agrāhyaḥ* is according to the rule *ṛhalor ṇyat* (Pāṇini 3.1.124)

He remains eternally the cause of the workings of the material universe (*śāśvataḥ*).

*anādir bhagavān kālo nānto’sya dvija vidyate \|*  
*avyucchinnās tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-saṁyamau \|\|*

**There is no end for the Lord and time which have no beginning. The two are continuous, and control creation, maintenance and destruction. Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**1.2.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.2.26)

He is called Kṛṣṇa because he has a form of knowledge and bliss and is colored blue like the atasī flower.

*kṛṣir bhū-vācakaḥ śabdo ṇaś ca nirvṛti-vācakaḥ \|*  
*tayor aikyaṁ paraṁ brahma kṛṣṇa ity abhidhīyate \|\|*

***Kṛṣ*** **means “existence” and** ***ṇa*** **means “bliss.” Combined together, the two roots indicate that Kṛṣṇa is the supreme form of God.**

*kṛṣṇo varṇaś ca me yasmāt tasmāt kṛṣṇo 'ham arjuna*

**O Arjuna! Because I am black I am called Kṛṣna. Mahābhārata 12.330.14**

*nāmnāṁ mukhyatamaṁ nāma kṛṣṇākhyaṁ me parantapa*

**O conqueror of foes! The name Kṛṣṇa is the chief among names. Skanda Purāṇa**

Shining full of bliss, he has eyes like red lotus petals (*lohitākṣaḥ*).

*Samāvṛṣabho lohitākṣaḥ*

**The excellent Lord has reddish eyes. Mantra-varṇa**

*mada-vighūrṇita-locana īṣan mānadaḥ sva-suhṛdām vanamālī*

**Kṛṣṇa with a forest garland, giving respect to his friends, rolls his eyes with intoxication. SB** [**10.35.24**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.24)

He removes the melancholy of the devotee (*pratardanaḥ*). *Tard* means to injure.

He is the lord of the spiritual sky. He is served by the highest wealth of the fourteen worlds (*prabhūtaḥ*).

This is because he is *tri-kakub dhāma.* He has a place in the spiritual sky called *tri-kakub*.

*pādo ‘sya viśvā bhūtāni tripādasyāmṛtam divi*

**The material realm is one foot and the spiritual realm consists of three feet. The Lord has tripād- vibhūti. Puruṣa-sūkta**

He purifies those who remember him (*pavitram*). He purifies sages and devatās. The root *pu* means to purify. This is expressed in the causative form. This is according to the rule *kartari carṣi-devatayoḥ* (Pāṇini 3.2.186).

He is the highest auspiciousness (*maṅgalam param*). *Maṅg* means to go.

*aśubhāni niracaṣṭe tanoti śubha-santatim*  
*smṛti-matreṇa yat puṁsāṁ brahma tan maṅgalaṁ viduḥ*

**The Lord in human form removes inauspiciousness and bestows auspiciousness abundantly just by remembering him. Thus he is known as maṅgalam. Mahābhārata**

In using *namaḥ* this name becomes maṅgalāya parasmai namah.

### Verse 21

*īśānaḥ prāṇa-daḥ prāṇo jyeṣṭhaḥ śreṣṭhaḥ prajā-patiḥ \|*  
*hiraṇya-garbho bhū-garbho mādhavo madhusūdanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**64. Īśānaḥ: he establishes all things.**  
**65. Prāṇa-daḥ: he bestows sense to all beings.**  
**66. Prāṇaḥ: he gives all beings life.**  
**67. Jyeṣṭhaḥ: he is the eldest of all.**  
**68. Śreṣṭhaḥ: he is most excellent.**  
**69. Prajā-patiḥ: he is the master of the nitya-muktas.**  
**70. Hiraṇyagarbhaḥ: he lives in a golden abode.**  
**71. Bhū-garbhaḥ: he closely embraces Bhū-devī.**  
**72. Mādhavaḥ: he is the consort of Lakṣmī.**  
**73. Madhusūdanaḥ: he destroys saṁsāra for the devotee.**

**Комментарий:**

He establishes all things (*īśānaḥ*).

As the first puruṣa he supplies senses (*prāṇa*) to the living entities so that they can attain their goals (*prāṇa-daḥ*).

*buddhīndriya-manaḥ-prāṇāṇ janānām asṛjat prabhuḥ*

**The Lord created the intelligence, senses, mind, and life airs of the living entities. SB** [**10.87.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/87.2)

He brings to life the living beings as the second puruṣa (*prāṇaḥ*).

*ko hy evānyāt kaḥ prāṇād ākāśa ānando na syāt*

**Who could breathe if ākāśa (brahman) were not bliss? Taittirīya Upaniṣad** [**2.7**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.7)

He is the oldest, existing before all beings (j*yeṣṭhaḥ*). The root *jya* means to become old.

He is most excellent because of great qualities (*śreṣṭhaḥ*). *Śra* means to be excellent.

He is the master (*patiḥ*) of the nitya-muktas like Garuḍa (*prajā*). *Prajā* means persons who are distinct (*pra*) from the sādhana-siddha jīvas. He is eternally worshipped by them.

*yad akṣare parame prajāḥ*

**The nitya-muktas live in the spiritual sky. Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad**

He lives in the golden spiritual sky (*hiraṇya-garbhaḥ*). It is called golden, because it is desirable like gold.

*hiraṇmaye pure kośe virajaṁ brahma niṣkalam*

**The pure, absolute Lord resides in a golden city. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad**

He is eternally served by his queen. He holds Bhu-devī like an embryo (*bhū-garbhaḥ*).

He is the husband (*davaḥ*) of Lakṣmī (*mā*):

*mādhava.śrīś ca te lakṣmīs ca patnyau*

**Śrī and Lakṣmī are his wives. Puruṣa-sūkta**

He is the destroyer (*sūdanaḥ*) of saṁsāra (*madhu*) for those who remember him. Śiva says:

*madhuḥ saṁsāra-nāmeti tato madhusūdanaḥ*

**Madhu means saṁsāra. Thus the Lord is called Madhusūdana.**

### Verse 22

*īśvaro vikramī dhanvī medhāvī vikramaḥ kramaḥ \|*  
*anuttamo durādharṣaḥ kṛta-jñaḥ kṛtir ātmavān \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**74. Īśvaraḥ: he is not obstructed in performing his pastimes.**  
**75. Vikramī: he is bold in his desires, knowledge and logic.**  
**76. Dhanvī: he possesses a bow.**  
**77. Medhāvī: he has fixed intelligence for performing his pastimes.**  
**78. Vikramaḥ: he can go anywhere unaided.**  
**79. Kramaḥ: he has the power to enjoy everything.**  
**80. Anuttamaḥ: he is supreme.**  
**81. Durādharṣaḥ: he cannot be defeated.**  
**82. Kṛta-jñaḥ: he acknowledges offerings.**  
**83. Kṛtiḥ: he engages jīvas in action.**  
**84. Ātmavān: he owns all the jīvas.**

**Комментарий:**

His desires are never obstructed in performing pastimes (*īśvaraḥ*).

*Satya-kāmaḥ satya-saṅkalpaḥ:*

**his desires are absolute and are all fulfilled. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad)**

He is bold by nature in his desires, knowledge, logic and destroying demons (*vikramī*).

He has an attractive bow suitable to his boldness (*dhanvī*).

He has fixed intelligence suitable to his pastimes (*medhavī*).

He can go to his destination even without Garuḍa (*vikramaḥ*).

He has the power to enjoy all collected items such as food, perfumes, and liquids (kramaḥ). He possesses this power at all times. This is formed according to the rulearśādibhyo c (Pāṇini 5.2.127)

*Krama* means power and arrangement according to Viśva.

He is incomparable, supreme (*anuttamah*).

Na tat samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate: there is no one equal or superior to him. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

*Mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat*: there is nothing superior to me. (BG [7.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.7))

He cannot be attacked or known (*durādharṣaḥ*). *Dur* indicates a negative, just as *durbhāgya* indicates poverty.

He acknowledges offerings of flowers and leaves made to him (*kṛta-jñaḥ*).

*patraṁ puḷspaṁ phalaṁ toyam yo me bhakhyā prayaccatitad ahaṁ bhakty upāhṛtam aśnāmi prayatātmanaḥ*

**I accept what is offered with devotion by the unmotivated devotee—a leaf, flower, fruit or water. BG** [**9.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.26)

He makes the jīvas engage in actions (*kṛtiḥ*). *Karoti* becomes *kṛti* just as *prakaroti* becomes *prakṛti.*

Eṣa hy eva sadhu karma kārayati: the Lord makes the jīva perform good actions. (Kauṣītaki Upaniṣad 3.8)

He is in charge of the jīvas, who should be engaged in actions (*ātmavān*).

### Verse 23

*sureśaḥ śaraṇaṁ śarma viśva-retāḥ prajā-bhavaḥ \|*  
*ahaḥ saṁvatsaro vyālaḥ pratyayas sarva-darśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**85. Sureśaḥ: he is the lord of those who give.**  
**86. Śaraṇam: he removes the sorrows of those in distress.**  
**87. Śarma: he is bliss. 88. Viśva-retāḥ: he possesses all the jīvas as his potency.**  
**89. Prajā-bhavaḥ: he produces all beings.**  
**90. Ahaḥ: he does not give up his devotees.**  
**91. Saṁvasaraḥ: he stays with the jīvas in this world.**  
**92. Vyālaḥ: he brings the jīvas to himself.**  
**93. Pratyayaḥ: he bestows consciousness.**  
**94. Sarva-darśanaḥ: he shows himself to the devotee.**

**Комментарий:**

He is the lord (*īśaḥ*) of those person who bestow (*rāti*) excellently (*su*) to qualified persons. In other words, he is the person who inspires giving.

Sarvasya śaraṇaṁ suhṛt: he is the shelter and friend of all beings. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [3.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/3.7))

He is the form of bliss (*śarma*). *Ānando brahma*: the Lord is bliss. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad [3.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/3.2))

He possesses all the particles consciousness (*viśva*) as his potency (*retāḥ*).

Thus, from him all beings arise (*prajā-bhavaḥ*).

Mama yonir mahad brahma: the great expanse of prakṛti is my womb. (BG [14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3))

He does not give up (*na jahāti*) his nature or his devotees (*ahaḥ*). Or he, like the day (*ahaḥ*), wakes up the jīvas submerged in the sleep of beginningless ignorance (*ahaḥ*). This word is formed according to *nañi jahāter* (Uṇādi-sūtra 159).

*tvatto jñānaṁ hi jīvānāṁ pramoṣas te 'tra śaktitaḥ*  
*tvam eva hy ātma-māyāyā gatiṁ vettha na cāparaḥ*

**From you alone the knowledge of the living beings arises, and by your potency that knowledge is stolen away. Indeed, no one but yourself can understand the real nature of your illusory potency. SB** [**11.22.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/22.28)

He stays (*saṁvasati*) with the jīvas who have woken up, in order to deliver them (*samvatsaraḥ*).

*teśām ahaṁ samuddhartā mṛtyu-saṁsāra-sāgarāt*  
*bhavāmi mā cirāt pārtha mayy āveśita-cetasām*

**O son of Pṛthā, for the devotees who have fixed their minds upon me, I am the swift deliverer from the ocean of birth and death. BG** [**2.7**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.7)

He accepts (*lāti*) the jīvas in a special way (*vi*). He brings the jivas close to him (*vyālaḥ*).

Because of him, the jīvas can perceive (*pratyayaḥ*).

*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te*

**I give them the intelligence by which they come to me. BG** [**10.10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.10)

When the jīvas acknowledge the Lord through scriptures and guru, the Lord shows his form and qualities to them (*sarva-darśinaḥ*).

*paśyanti te me rucirāṇy amba santaḥ*  
*prasanna-vaktrāruṇa-locanāni*  
*rūpāṇi divyāni vara-pradāni*  
*sākaṁ vācaṁ spṛhanīyaṁ vadanti*

**O mother, my devotees always see the smiling face of my form, with eyes like the rising morning sun. They like to see my various transcendent forms, which are all benevolent, and they also talk favorably with me. SB.** [**3.25.35**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/25.35)

### Verse 24

*ajaḥ sarveśvaraḥ siddhaḥ siddhiḥ sarvādir acyutaḥ \|*  
*vṛṣākapir ameyātmā sarva-yMga-viniḥsṛtaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**95. Ajah: he drives away obstacles to attaining him.**  
**96. Sarveśvaraḥ: he spreads to the senses of the devotees.**  
**97. Siddhaḥ: he manifests his perfect form to the devotees.**  
**98. Siddhiḥ: he becomes visible through bhakti.**  
**99. Sarvādiḥ: he is the root of all the devotees’ desires.**  
**100. Acyutaḥ: he never fails his devotee.**  
**101. Vṛṣākapiḥ: he showers all objects of desire and makes the non-devotees tremble.**  
**102. Ameyātmā: his intelligence cannot be limited.**  
**103. Sarva-yoga-viniḥsṛtaḥ: he is not contaminated by contact with matter.**

**Комментарий:**

He drives away all obstacles to attaining him for the devotees *(ajaḥ*). *Āj* means to drive away. The agument *a* is added.

He spreads to all the senses of the devotees (*sarveśvaraḥ*).

He manifests his perfect form to the devotees (*siddhaḥ*).

He is the very form of unlimited results or he becomes visible through certain methods (*siddhiḥ*).

bhaktyāham ekayā gryāhyaḥ: I am achieved only by bhakti. (SB [11.14.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.21))

He is the root of all the devotees’ desires (*sarvādiḥ*).

*svargāpavargayoḥ puṁsāṁ rasāyāṁ bhuvi sampadām sarvāsām api siddhānāṁ mūlaṁ tac-caraṇārcaṇam*

**By worshiping Lord Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet, one attains both liberation and heavenly happiness within this world. That worship is the cause of attaining all mystic powers, and all the opulences of the earthly and pātāla planets. SB** [**10.81.19**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/81.19)

He never fails his devotee (*acyutaḥ*).

*tasyāhaṁ na praṇaśyāmi sa ca me na praṇaśyati*

**For one who sees me everywhere and sees everything in me, I am never lost, nor is he ever lost to me. BG** [**6.30**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.30)

He showers whatever the devotee desires (*vṛṣa*) and makes the non-devotees tremble (*ākapiḥ*).

He has intelligence (*ātmā*) which is impossible to limit (*ameyaḥ*).

He is not contaminated by contact with matter (*sarva-yoga-viniḥsṛtaḥ*).

### Verse 25

*vasur vasumanāḥ satyaḥ samātmā sammitaḥ samaḥ \|*  
*amoghaḥ puṇḍarīkākṣo vṛṣa-karmā vṛṣākṛtiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**104. Vasuḥ: he lives in the devotee.**  
**105. Vasu-manāḥ: his mind is fixed in the treasures which are his devotees.**  
**106. Satyaḥ: he acts properly with his devotees.**  
**107. Samātmā: he thinks equally of devotees though they may have differing qualities.**  
**108. Sammitaḥ: he is measurable, held in the hand.**  
**109. Samaḥ: he manifests himself with beauty to the devotees according to their qualification.**  
**110. Amoghaḥ: he gives to all what they desire without fail.**  
**111. Puṇḍarīkākṣaḥ: he spreads in the lotus of the heart.**  
**112. Vṛṣakarmā: he showers the devotee with whatever the devotee desires.**  
**113. Vṛṣākṛtiḥ: his form is pleasing to the eye because of its showers of beauty.**

**Комментарий:**

He lives in the devotees (*vasuḥ*).

*mad-bhaktā yatra gāyanti tatra tiṣṭhāmi nārada*

**O Nārada, I stay where my devotees engage in glorifying my activities. Viṣṇu-dharma**

His mind is fixed (*manāh*) in the treasures (*vasu*) which are his devotees, who are without material wealth. According to Halayudha the word *vasu* means fire, wealth, rays, a jewel and the Vasu devatā.

namo 'kiñcana-vittāya: I offer respects to the wealth who is the penniless devotee. (SB [1.8.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.27))

He acts properly with his devotees (*satyaḥ*).

He thinks equally of devotees though they may have differing qualities such as education etc. (*samātmā*).

He is measurable, held in the hand, because of being limited by his devotee (*sammitaḥ*).

*kṛtāgasaṁ taṁ prarudantam akṣiṇī*  
*kaṣantam añjan-maṣiṇī sva-pāṇinā*  
*udvīkṣamāṇaṁ bhaya-vihvalekṣaṇaṁ*  
*haste gṛhītvā bhiṣayanty avāgurat*

**When caught by mother Yaśodā, Kṛṣṇa became more and more afraid and admitted to being an offender. As she looked upon him, she saw that he was crying, his tears mixing with the black ointment around his eyes, and as he rubbed his eyes with his hands, he smeared the ointment all over his face. Mother Yaśodā, catching her beautiful son by the hand, mildly began to chastise him. SB** [**10.9.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.11)

He manifests himself with beauty to the devotees according to their qualification (*samaḥ*).

He gives to all what they desire without fail (*amoghaḥ*).

*amoghā bhagavad-bhaktir netareti matir mama*

**Any method other than devotional service, which is unfailing in giving results, is useless. That is my opinion. SB** [**8.16.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/16.21)

He spreads (*akṣnoti*) in the lotus of the heart (*puṇḍarīka*) of the devotee (*pundarīkākṣaḥ*).

He acts to shower the devotee with whatever the devotee desires (*vṛṣa-karmā*). *Vṛṣ* means to sprinkle.

His form is pleasing to the eye because of its showers of beauty (*vṛṣākṛt*i).

*bibhrad vapuḥ sakala-sundara sanniveśaṁ*

**The Lord, who bore his body as the amalgamation of everything beautiful, dutifully executed the most auspicious activities while on the earth, although he was, in fact, without any endeavor already satisfied in all desires. SB** [**11.10.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/10.1)

### Verse 26

*rudro bahuśirā babhrur viśva-yMniḥ śuci-śravāḥ \|*  
*amṛtaḥ śāśvataḥ sthāṇur varāroho mahā-tapāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**114. Rudraḥ: he removes sickness from living entities**  
**115. Bahu-śirāḥ: he has innumerable heads.**  
**116. Babhruḥ: he supports the world.**  
**117. Viśva-yoniḥ: he associates with the devotees.**  
**118. Śuci-śravāḥ: he hears the words of his devotees.**  
**119. Amṛtaḥ: he prevents old age and death and gives satisfaction.**  
**120. Śāśvataḥ sthāṇuḥ: he is eternal by his nature and is continually enjoyed.**  
**121. Varārohaḥ: he gives the most excellent elevation.**  
**122. Mahā-tapāḥ: he tolerates the material creation.**

**Комментарий:**

He removes the sickness (*rujam*) from living entities (*rudraḥ*).

*rujaṁ drāvayate yasmād rudras tasmāj janārdanaḥ*

**Because Janārdana dissolves (*****rujam drāvayate*****) sickness, he is known as Rudra. Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa**

He has a thousand heads (*bahu-śirā*). *Sahasra-śīrṣā puruṣaḥ*: the Lord has a thousand heads. Purusa-sūkta

He holds up the universe (b*abhruḥ*). *Bhruḍ* means to cover.

He mixes with the devotees (*viśva-yoniḥ*).

He hears the words of the pure devotee (*śuci-śravaḥ*).

*śucīni sravaṇīyāni śṛṇomīha dhanañjaya*  
*na ca pāpāni gṛhṇāmi tato 'haṁ vai śuci-śravāḥ*

**O Dhanañjaya, I am known as** ***śuci-śravāḥ*** **because I hear the pure words of the devotee, which should be heard and I do not accept any sin. Mahābhārata 12.330.26**

He prevents old age and death and produces satisfaction (*amṛtaḥ*).

He is further described to distinguish him from the amṛta which arose from the ocean. He is eternal by nature (*śāśvataḥ)* and is enjoyed at all times by persons who serve him (*sthāṇuḥ*). Material amṛta is not eternal and is enjoyed only once.

Thus he is excellent in elevating the devotee, so that he does not return to the material world (*varārohaḥ*).

Kāraṇodaśāyī is indicated in two names. He endures (*tapāḥ*) the agitation of prakṛti (*mahat*). His toleration of the material world is seeing it.

### Verse 27

*sarvagaḥ sarva-vid bhānur viṣvakseno janārdanaḥ \|*  
*vedo veda-vid avyaṅgo vedāṅgo veda-vit kaviḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**123. Sarva-gaḥ: he spreads through prakṛti.**  
**124. Sarva-vid: he attains all created beings.**  
**125. Bhānuḥ: he shines without contamination.**  
**126. Viṣvakśenaḥ: he has associates everywhere.**  
**127. Janārdanaḥ: he causes pain to the opposition.**  
**128. Vedaḥ: he is the Vedas. 129. Veda-vit: he is the knower of the Vedas.**  
**130. Avyaṅgaḥ: he is not devoid of the Vedic aṅgas.**  
**131. Vedāṅgaḥ: he has the Vedas as his limbs.**  
**132. Veda-vit: he propagates the Vedas.**  
**133. Kaviḥ: he sees everything.**

**Комментарий:**

He spreads in all twenty-four divisions of prakṛti (*sarva-gaḥ*).

Garbhodaśāyī is described in two names. He attains (*vit*) all the created beings (*sarva*), by the creation.

Though creating everything, he remains shining beautifully, unchanged (*bhānuḥ*).

Kṣīrodaśāyī is then described in seven names. *Sena* means persons with the Lord (*sa inaḥ*). He has associates (*sena*) everywhere (*viśvak*) for protection of the universe (*viśvaksena*).

He kills persons opposed to that protection (j*anārdanaḥ*). *Ard* means to afflict. Or he is the object of request by people (*janārdhanaḥ*). He shows friendship.to them. *Ard* means to go and to ask.

He is the Ṛg and other Vedas (*vedaḥ*).

Vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt: the Lord is directly the Vedas. (SB [6.1.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/1.40))

He knows the Vedas in terms of syllables and meaning (*veda-vit*).

Vedanta-kṛt, veda-vid eva cāham: I am the maker of Vedānta and knower of the Vedas. (BG [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15))

He is not devoid of aṅgas of Vedas like śikṣā (*avyaṅgaḥ*).

He has the Vedas as his limbs. They give knowledge of him (*vedāṅgaḥ*).

Nāveda-vin manute taṁ bṛhantam: one who does not know the Vedas cannot know the Lord. (*Śātyāyana Upaniṣad* 4)

He makes persons understand the wealth of the Vedas (*veda-vit*). Nārāyanīya describes how the Lord with his hands in the air makes others repeat the Vedas.

He sees thoroughly (*kaviḥ*).

### Verse 28

*lMkādhyakṣaḥ surādhyakṣo dharmādhyakṣaḥ kṛtākṛtaḥ \|*  
*caturātmā caturvyūhaś caturdaṁṣṭraś caturbhujaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**134. Lokādhyakṣaḥ: he oversees the brāhmaṇas and others.**  
**135. Surādhyakṣaḥ: he oversees the devatās.**  
**136. Dharmādhyakṣaḥ: he oversees acts of dharma.**  
**137. Kṛtākṛtaḥ: he is not produced from material actions.**  
**138. Catur-ātmā: he is four persons.**  
**139. Chatur-vyūhaḥ: he has his four expansions.**  
**140. Chatur-daṁṣṭraḥ: he has four teeth.**  
**141. Chatur-bhujaḥ: he has four arms.**

**Комментарий:**

Three names then describe his ability to oversee. He oversees brāhmaṇas, fixed in dharma (*lokādkyakṣaḥ*).

He oversees devatās (*surādhyakṣaḥ*) who should be worshipped by dharma.

He oversees dharma such as sacrifice (*dharmādhyakṣaḥ*). Establishing various dharmas, he gives results to performers of dharma through devatās worshipped by dharma.

Vāsudeva is indicated. He is not manifested by material actions (*krtākṛtaḥ*). This means he is untouched by matter.

He has four types of bodies such as Saṅkarṣaṇa (*caturātmā*). How can one entity be four?

He has four expansions (*catur-vyūhaḥ*). He is known (*vyūhyate*) as four (*catuḥ*) by his six powers (*aiśvarya*) which manifest totally and separately as these results. *Vyūha* is formed from the root *vyūh* according to the rule *halas ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.121).

He has four teeth (*catur daṁṣṭraḥ*). Having four teeth is a characteristic of a great person.

*caturdaśa-sama-dvandvaś catur daṁṣṭraś catur gatiḥ*

**He should possess fourteen pairs of evenly sized limbs,four large teeth and four graceful modes of walking (like an elephant, tiger, lion and bull). Rāmāyaṇa 5.35.19**

Among the three forms, Viṣṇu is described with eleven names. He has four arms (*catur-bhujaḥ*).

### Verse 29

*bhrājiṣṇur bhMjanaṁ bhoktāsahiṣṇur jagadādijaḥ \|*  
*anagho vijayo jetā viśva-yoniḥ punarvasuḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**142. Bhrājiṣṇuḥ: he is most radiant.**  
**143. Bhojanam: he gives livelihood to the jīvas.**  
**144. Bhoktā: he protects the well behaved.**  
**145. Asahiṣṇuḥ: he is intolerant of the demons.**  
**146. Jagad-ādhijaḥ: he manifests himself differently among the three deities in the universe.**  
**147. Anaghaḥ: he destroys sin.**  
**148. Vijayaḥ: he remains superior to Brahmā and Viṣṇu.**  
**149. Jetā: he establishes the two in their activities by his will.**  
**150. Viśva-yoniḥ: he is the material cause.**  
**151. Punar-vasuḥ: he repeatedly appears in the universe to assist the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

He is most radiant (*bhrājiṣṇuḥ*).

He gives livelihood to the jīvas (*bhojanam*).

He protects (*bhunakti*) the well behaved (*bhoktā*).

He is intolerant of the wicked (*asahiṣṇuḥ*).

He manifests a form completely different among the three deities in the world (*jagad-ādijaḥ*). The word is according to the rule saptamyām janer daḥ (Pāṇini 3.2.97).

*yasya prasādād aham acyutasya*  
*bhūtaḥ prajā-sṛṣṭi-karo 'ṇta-kārī*  
*krodhāc ca rudraḥ sthiti-hetu-bhūto*  
*yasmāc ca madhye puruṣaḥ purastāt*

**I appear by the mercy of Viṣṇu as the creator of living entities. Śiva is born from his anger. The puruṣa existed previously as the maintainer. Mahābhārata**

Though he appears in the material world, he destroys sin (*anaghaḥ*).

Though as a guṇāvatāra he seems similar to Brahmā and Śiva, he remains in a superior position (*vijayaḥ*).

Thus he is the conqueror (*jetā):* he establishes Brahmā and Śiva in their activities according to his will.

*etau dvau vibhuda-śreṣṭhau prasāda-krodha-jau smṛtau*

**Brahmā was born from mercy and Śiva from anger. Mahābhārata 12.328.16**

He is the material cause (upādāna) of the universe through his root form (*viśva-yoniḥ*).

He repeatedly (*punaḥ*) dwells (*vasuḥ*) in the universe for benefiting the devatās (*punar-vasuḥ*).

### Verse 30

*upendro vāmanaḥ prāṁśur amoghaḥ śucir ūrjitaḥ \|*  
*atīndraḥ saṅgrahaḥ sargo dhṛtātmā niyamo yama \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**152. Upendraḥ: he was the younger brother of Indra.**  
**153. Vāmanaḥ: he had the form of a dwarf.**  
**154. Prāṁśuḥ: he became tall.**  
**155. Amoghaḥ: his actions were not in vain.**  
**156. Śuciḥ: he was the perfect benefactor.**  
**157. Ūrjitaḥ: he showed great prowess.**  
**158 Atīndraḥ: he was superior to Indra.**  
**159. Saṅgrahaḥ: he was accepted by Indra.**  
**160. Sargaḥ: he does what should be done among the Adityas.**  
**161. Dhṛtātmā: he brought Indra’s mind under control.**  
**162. Niyamaḥ: he brought the devatās near. 163. Yamaḥ: he granted their desires.**

**Комментарий:**

Vāmana is described with twelve names. Though he was the younger brother of Indra, by qualities he was superior (*upendraḥ*).

*dvadaśaivāditeḥ putrāḥ śakra-mukhyā narādhipa*  
*teṣām avara-jo viṣṇur yatra lokāḥ pratiṣṭhitā*

**O king! Aditi had twelve sons. The chief was Indra. Among then the youngest was Viṣṇu, in whom the planets are situated. Mahābhārata**

He took the form of an attractive dwarf brahmacārī to fool Bali and help Indra (*vāmanaḥ*).

He became very tall to surpass the three worlds (*prāṁśuḥ*).

*toye tu patite haste vāmano 'bhūd avāmanaḥ*  
*sarva-deva-mayaṁ rūpaṁ darśayām āsa vai bhuvi*

**When the water fell from Bali’s hand, Vāmana became huge. He showed on earth a form composed of all the devatās. Hari-vaṁśa**

Nothing is in vain for the Lord (*amoghaḥ*).

Equally, Vāmana gave wealth to Bali and Indra. He acts as a benefactor because of his gratitude (*śuciḥ*).

He showed prowess against Bali’s troops who tried to defeat him (ūrjitaḥ).

By his powers he was situated above Indra (*atīndraḥ*).

He was completely accepted by the devotee Indra (*saṁgrahaḥ*). This word is formed according to the rule *graha-vṛ-ḍr- ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.58)

He did (*sṛjate*) what should be done among the Adityas (*sargaḥ*). The root *ṣṛj* becomes *sraja* by the rule *halas ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.121)

He brought Indra’s mind under control by giving him happiness (*dhṛtātmā*).

He brought the devatās near according to their qualification (*niyamaḥ*).

He entered into the devatās and granted their desires (*yamaḥ*).

### Verse 31

*vedyo vaidyaḥ sadā-yogī vīra-hā mādhavo madhuḥ \|*  
*atīndriyo mahā-māyo mahotsāho mahā-balaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**164. Vedyaḥ: he is known as the person who takes away suffering.**  
**165. Vaidyaḥ: he became a physician.**  
**166. Sadā-yogī: he came near at all times.**  
**167. Vīra-hā: he killed the valorous demons.**  
**168. Mādhava: he was the master of knowledge.**  
**169. Madhuḥ: he had sweet beauty.**  
**170. Atīindraḥ: he disappeared after giving the nectar.**  
**171. Mahā-māyaḥ: his form of Mohinī greatly bewildered the demons.**  
**172. Mahotsāhaḥ: with great enthusiasm he endeavored for the devatās.**  
**173. Mahā-balaḥ: he showed great prowess in bewildering the demons.**

**Комментарий:**

The appearance of the nectar and the Lord from the churning of the milk ocean is described in eighteen names. He should be known (*vedyaḥ*) as the person who takes away the poverty of persons such as the devatās (by supplying nectar).

He appeared as the physician Dhanvantari and gave the nectar (*vaidyaḥ*).

He came near at all times to benefit the devatās (*sadā-yogī*).

He killed the demons who fight with prowess for establishing evil (*vīra-hā*). *Hā* instead of *han* arises from the rule *brahma-brūṇa-vṛtreṣu* *kip* (Pāṇini 3.2.87)

This is understood to be the case of names like *kāma-hā* as well. Or he killed (*hā)* demons by the wind (*īra*) of Garuda and other birds (*vī*).

*suparṇa-pakṣābhihatā nipetur dānavarṣabhāḥ*

**The greatest of demons fell, on being struck by the wings of Garuḍa.**

He was the master of all knowledge (*mādhavaḥ*). Śiva says:

*mā vidyā ca hareḥ proktā tasyām īśo yato bhavān*  
*tasmān mādhava-nāmāsi pūrvam asīti śabditaḥMā*

**is said to indicate learning. Because you are the lord of learning you are named Mādhava. Hari-vaṁśa**

Some say that Mādhava means the husband of Lakṣmī, since she appeared from the churning of the milk ocean.

He had beauty and grace tasted by Lakṣmī and others (*madhuḥ*).

After having the devatās drink nectar, he disappeared *(atīndriyaḥ*).

Dressed as Mohinī, he caused great illusion in the demons (*mahā-māyaḥ*).

He was most enthusiastic to make the devatās successful (*mahotsahaḥ*).

He had great power to bewilder (*mahā-balaḥ*).

### Verse 32

*mahā-buddhir mahā-vīryo mahā-śaktir mahā-dyutiḥ \|*  
*anirdeśya-vapuḥ śrīmān ameyātmā mahādri-dhṛk \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**174. Mahā-buddhiḥ: he made the decision to accomplishing something great.**  
**175. Mahā-vīryaḥ: he had great power to produce the nectar.**  
**176. Mahā-śaktiḥ: he was capable of controlling the demons’ intelligence.**  
**177. Mahā-dyutiḥ: he performed the great task of churning.**  
**178. Anirdeśya-vapuḥ: his body could not be defined even by the devatās.**  
**179. Śrīmān: he possessed great beauty and mind.**  
**180. Ameyātmā: his form and mind were beyond measure.**  
**181. Mahā-dri-dhṛk: he moved or held the Mandara Mountain.**

**Комментарий:**

He made the decision to accomplish something great (*mahā-buddhiḥ*).

He has great prowess to produce the nectar to help the devatās (*mahā-vīrya*).

He has great ability to control the intelligence of the demons (*mahā-śaktiḥ*).

When both the demons and devatās became exhausted from churning the milk ocean he himself performed this task with great radiance (*mahā-dyutiḥ*).

*megha-śyāmaḥ kanaka-paridhiḥ karṇa-vidyoti-vidyun-*  
*mūrdhi-bhrājad-vilulita-kacaḥ srag-dharo rakta-netraḥ*  
*jaitrair dorbhir jagad-ubhayadair daṇḍaśūkaṁ gṛhītvā*  
*mantha-manthan prati girir ivāśobhatātho dhṛtādriḥ*

**The Lord, whose complexion was like a blackish cloud and who had reddish eyes, who wore yellow garments, a garland and earrings shining like lightning, with disheveled hair shining on his head, grabbed the snake with his victorious arms-- which award fearlessness throughout the universe-- and began churning the ocean, using Mandara Mountain as a churning rod. The Lord, on grasping that mountain, appeared like a sapphire mountain. SB** [**8.7.17**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/7.17)

He has a body which cannot be precisely defined. “What is that body?” (*anirdeśya-vapuḥ*).

He has excellence of dress or mind (*śrīmān*). According to Viśva, *śrī* indicates arranging clothing nicely, beauty, Sarasvatī, the sarala tree, Lakṣmī, wealth of the three worlds, an insignia and mentality.

His body or intelligence could not be measured or limited by any being, including devatās (ameyātmā). *Ātmā* means a man, nature, effort, mind, determination, intelligence, body and brahman according to Medini.

Thus he moved beneath (*dhṛj*) the great Mandara Mountain (*mahādr*i), as Kūrma. *Ṛj* means to move or to hold. A root can have many meanings.

### Verse 33

*maheṣvāso mahībhartā śrīnivāsaḥ satāṁ gatiḥ \|*  
*aniruddhaḥ surānando govindo govidāṁ patiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**182. Maheṣvāsaḥ: he possessed a great bow (as Rāma).**  
**183. Mahī-bhartā: he held up the earth (as Varāha).**  
**184. Śrī-nivāsaḥ: he protects the inhabitants of Ketumāla as Kāma deva.**  
**185. Satāṁ gatiḥ: He is the protector of the inhabitans of the other varṣas.**  
**186. Aniruddhaḥ: he is not controlled by non-devotees.**  
**187. Surānandaḥ: he gives joy to the devotees.**  
**188. Govindaḥ: he accepts the praises of these devotees. 189. Govidāṁ patiḥ: he removes obstacles of those who worship his rays.**

**Комментарий:**

Protecting the various varṣas is described in four names. He has a great bow (*maheṣvāsaḥ*).As Rāma he protected Kimpuruṣa-varṣa and its inhabitants worshipped him.

He is the lord of the earth (*mahī-bhārtaḥ*). Varāha protects Uttara-kuru-varṣa and is worshipped by its inhabitants.

He lives in Ketumāla-varṣa with his wife Rati (*śrī-nivāsaḥ*) as Kāmadeva. He protects that place and is worshipped by the inhabitants.

He protects the inhabitants of the other varṣas as well (*satām gatiḥ*) and is worshipped by them. The five forms with names are suggested for the other varṣas.

His lordship of Śvetadvīpa is indicated by six names. He is not brought under control by persons devoid of pure bhakti (*aniruddhaḥ*).

*Sura* means those who are illuminated (*surante*) by the power of pure bhakti. He gives joy to these devotees such as Candra, Sūrya and Agni by personally appearing before them (*surānandaḥ*). The root *sur*, meaning to shine or rule, belongs to the taudādika class of words.

He accepts the praises of these devotees (*govindaḥ*). Halayudha says the word *go* can mean earth, cow, water, thunderbolt, words, sound, heaven, light and eye. One who worships the rays of his body is called *govinda*. He drives away obstacles (*patiḥ*) of those worshippers (*govindām*).

### Verse 34

*marīcir damano haṁsaḥ suparṇo bhujagottamaḥ \|*  
*hiraṇya-nābhaḥ sutapāḥ padmanābhaḥ prajā-patiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**190. Marīciḥ: he shines.**  
**191. Damanaḥ: he breaks the meditation of the devotee.**  
**192. Haṁsaḥ: he appeared as a bird to deliver knowledge.**  
**193. Suparṇaḥ: he has Garuḍa as his carrier.**  
**194. Bhujagottamaḥ: he has Śeṣa as his bed.**  
**195. Hiraṇyanābhaḥ: he has a navel attractive like gold.**  
**196. Sutapāḥ: he performs glorious austerities.**  
**197. Padmanābhaḥ: he shines because of his devotees who know his lotus feet.**  
**198. Prajā-patiḥ: he protects Brahmā and Śiva.**

**Комментарий:**

He shines (*marīciḥ*). The ending *īci* is derived according to the rule *mṛk libhyam īciḥ*. (Uṇādi 513)

He breaks the austerities of his meditators by showing his beautiful effulgence (*damanaḥ*).

He appeared as a swan (*haṁsaḥ*) to discuss what is real and unreal when the Kumāras asked the question and did not receive an answer from Brahmā. The derivation of *haṁsa* is one who destroys (*hanti*) doubts. Others say that *haṁsa* means one who attains (*hanti*) attractiveness.

Garuḍa has wings made of the meters of the Vedas (*suparṇaḥ*). It also refers to he who has Garuḍa as his carrier eternally.

He is the master of Śeṣa (*bhujagottamaḥ*). Śeṣa acts as his bed.

He has a navel pleasant to see as gold (*hiraṇya-nābhaḥ*). Derivation of *nābha* was explained previously.

He performs glorious austerities or for him persons perform glorious austerities (*sutapāḥ*).

He shines (*ābhāti*) because of his devotees who know (*na*) his lotus feet (*padmanābhaḥ*).

He protects Brahmā, Śiva and others when they are in danger (*prajā-patiḥ*).

### Verse 35

*amṛtyuḥ sarva-dṛk siṁhaḥ sandhātā sandhimān sthiraḥ \|*  
*ajo durmarṣaṇaḥ śāstā viśrutātmā surāri-hā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**199. Amṛtyuḥ: he eliminates death for the worshiper.**  
**200. Sarva-dṛk: he acknowledges all the offerings made by the devotee.**  
**201. Siṁhaḥ: he showers his glance on the devotee.**  
**202. Sandhātā: as Rāma, he united with Sītā.**  
**203. Sandhimān: he made friends with Vibhīśaṇa.**  
**204. Sthiraḥ: he was steady in his friendship.**  
**205. Ajaḥ: he did not reject the surrendered devotee.**  
**206. Durmarṣaṇaḥ: he showed extreme tolerance to gain Sītā.**  
**207 Śasta: he punished the demons.**  
**208. Vishrutātmā: he became famous for his actions.**  
**209. Surāri-hā: he killed the enemies of the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

He eliminates death for one who worships him (*amṛtyuḥ*).

Yaḥ sarvajñaḥ sarvavit: he knows everything. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad)

He sprinkles (*siñcati*) his sweet glance on the worshippers who have come near (*siṁhaḥ*).

Rāma is indicated by eighteen names. He was united with Sītā, after being brought to Mithilā by Viśvamitra *(sandhātā*).

Going to the forest on the order of his father, he lamented for Sītā when she was stolen by Rāvaṇa. He made friends with Sugrīva (*sandhimān*).

He had steady affection for Vibhīṣaṇa, who had surrendered to him, in spite of being prevented by his followers (*sthiraḥ*).

He rejected the words of his followers to give up Vibhīṣaṇa and did not give him up because of his friendship (*ajaḥ*).

*mitra-bhāvena samprāptaṁ na tyajeyaṁ kathañcana*  
*doṣo yadyapi tasmin syāt satām etad vigarhitam*

**Even if there are serious faults in a person, and he is criticized by the devotees, I should not reject this person who has approached me in the mood of a friend. Rāmāyaṇa 6.123.234**

Rāmacandra says:

*sakṛd eva prapanno yas tavāsmīti ca yācate*  
*abhayaṁ sarvadā tasmai dadāmy etad vrataṁ mama*

**I give fearlessness to he who has once surrenders to me and says “I am yours.” That is my vow. Rāmāyaṇa,** ***5.*****18.33**

He showed impossible tolerance in building a bridge to Laṅka, killing Rāvaṇa and then taking Sītā (*durmarṣaṇaḥ*).

He punished Rāvaṇa and his followers according to his promise (*śāstā*).

He is famous for his actions (*viśrutātmā*).

He killed the enemies of the devotees such as Rāvaṇa and his followers (*surāri-hā*).

### Verse 36

*gurur gurutamo dhāmaḥ satyaḥ satya-parākramaḥ \|*  
*nimiṣo nimiṣaḥ sragvī vācaspatir udāra-dhīḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**210. Guruḥ: he gave beneficial instrutions to all.**  
**211. Gurutamaḥ: he gave instructions to Brahmā and others.**  
**212. Dhāmaḥ: he was the shelter of all beings.**  
**213. Satyaḥ: he benefitted the devotees.**  
**214. Satya-parākamaḥ: his conquests were absolute and spiritual.**  
**215. Nimiṣaḥ: he closes his eyes to sins.**  
**216. Animiṣaḥ: he is awake to see devotional actions.**  
**217. Sragvī: he wore a vaijayantī garland.**  
**218. Vācas-patiḥ: he protected Sarasvatī.**  
**219. Udāra-dhih: he was merciful to Guha and others.**

**Комментарий:**

He gave beneficial instructions to the world *(guruḥ*).

He gave instructions to even gurus of the universe such as Brahmā (*gurutamaḥ*).

He was the shelter of all beings or possessed great powers (*dhāmaḥ*).

He gave benefit to the devotees such as Viśvāmitra (satyaḥ). The suffix ya  is added according to the rule ugavadibho yat (Pāṇini 5.1.2.)

He conquered absolutely, with spiritual intent (*satya-parākramaḥ*).

He sleeps while the people act contrary to the Vedas (*nimiṣaḥ*). He closes his eyes at that time.

He does not sleep but is awake when people perform Vedic activities (*animiṣaḥ*).

He always wears a Vaijayantī garland (*sragv*ī).

He protects Sarasvatī (*vācas-patiḥ*).

He offered his rare feet even to low born people like Guha, Jaṭāyu and Śabarī (*udāra-dhiḥ*). Examples of these qualities will be found in Rāmāyaṇa, Mahābhārata and Bhāgavatam.

### Verse 37

*agraṇīr grāmaṇīḥ śrīmān nyāyM netā samīraṇaḥ \|*  
*sahasra-mūrdhā viśvātmā sahasrākṣaḥ sahasrapāt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**220. Agraṇīḥ: as Matsya, he placed himself in front of the boat and pulled it.**  
**221. Grāmaṇīḥ: he had pastimes with the people in the boat.**  
**222. Śrīmān: he had wealth in the form of the Vedas.**  
**223. Nyāyaḥ: he did the proper action.**  
**224. Netā: he was the leader.**  
**225. Samīraṇaḥ: he performed glorious actions.**  
**226. Sahasra-mūrdhā: as the universal form, he has unlimited heads.**  
**227. Viśvātmā: he pervades the universe.**  
**228. Sahasrākṣaḥ sahasrapāt: he has unlimited eyes and unlimited feet.**

**Комментарий:**

Matsya is described with six names. Tying a boat to his horn and placing Manu and others within, he led the boat by going in front (*agraṇīḥ*).

Bringing the group (*grama*) headed by Manu into the boat, he has pastimes with Manu (*grāmiṇīḥ*).

He had wealth because he protected the Vedas *(śrīmān*).

He did what was proper (*nyāyaḥ*). The word is formed from the root *nī* according to the rule

*pariṇyor nīṛnor dyūta-bhūṣebhyoḥ (Pāṇini 3.3.36)*

He led them (*netā*).

He performed most glorious actions (*samīraṇaḥ*). This is a combination of samyak (*sam*) and the root *īr* meaning to go. The suffix *ana* is added. One will find examples of these qualities in the stories about Tvaṣṭā and others.

Twelve names describe the universal form. He has a thousand heads (*sahasra-mūrdhā*).

He pervades the universe (*viśvātmā*).

He has unlimited eyes and feet (*sahasrākṣa sahasra pāt*). *Sahasra* means unlimited.

Sarvataḥ pāṇipādam tad: his hands and feet are everywhere. (BG [13.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.13))

### Verse 38

*āvartano nivṛttātmā saṁvṛtaḥ saṁpratardanaḥ \|*  
*ahaḥ saṁvartako vahnir anilo dharaṇī-dharaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**229. Āvrtanaḥ: the universal form turns the wheel of saṁsāra.**  
**230. Nivṛttātmā: he rises above the world to enjoy the spiritual world.**  
**231. Saṁvṛtaḥ: he is hidden from the demons.**  
**232. Sam-pramardanaḥ: he destroys the demons.**  
**233. Ahaḥ-saṁvartakaḥ: he devours time.**  
**234. Vahniḥ: he supports the various places in the universe.**  
**235. Anilaḥ: he needs no one to inspire his generous actions.**  
**236. Dharaṇī-dharaḥ: he nourishes the earth.**

**Комментарий:**

He rotates the wheel of saṁsāra like a waterwheel (*āvatanaḥ*).

Tenedaṁ bhrāmyate brahma-cakram: the wheel of saṁsāra is rotated by the Lord. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

Transcending the one vibhūti (material world) he is in possession of the tripāda-vibhūti (*nivṛtātmā*).

Tripād urdhva udait puruṣaḥ: the puruṣa went above, to the tripāda-vibhūti. (Puruṣa-sūkta)

He is hidden from people attached to material objects (*saṁvṛtaḥ*).

He excludes the demons opposed to his path, destroying them (*saṁpratardanaḥ*).

He devours even time, characterized by day (*ahaḥ samvartakaḥ*). *Saṁvartakaḥ* is called *kāla-kālo* in Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad.

He supports the universe by manifesting its places (*vahniḥ*). The word is derived according to vahi śriyudrugla (Uṇādi sutra 494).

Pādo ‘syehābhavat punas: the places again manifested. (Purusa sūkta)
