He does not have an instigator *(anilaḥ*). By his own impetus he acts for others’ well-being.

He supports the whole earth (*dharaṇī-dharaḥ*).

Sa dadhāra pṛthivīm utemām: he held up this earth. (Mahābhārata)

One will find other examples of names in the section on the universal form in the hundred and eight names.

### Verse 39

*suprasādaḥ prasannātmā viśva-dhṛg viśva-bhug-vibhuḥ \|*  
*satkartā satkṛtaḥ sādhur jahnur nārāyaṇo naraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**237. Suprasādaḥ: on the banyan leaf, he showed extraordinary mercy.**  
**238. Prasannātmā: his heart was pure.**  
**239. Viśva-dhṛk: he created the universes.**  
**240. Viśva-bhug-vibhuḥ: he pervaded and protected the universe.**  
**241. Satkartā: he performed beneficial acts as Badarinātha.**  
**242. Satkṛtaḥ: he was worshipped by the devatās.**  
**243. Sādhuḥ: he acted properly in performing austerities.**  
**244 Jahnuḥ: he defeated Śiva and the Apsaras.**  
**245. Nārāyaṇaḥ: he was the shelter of all jīvas.**  
**246. Naraḥ: he had an indestructible form.**

**Комментарий:**

Four names describe the Lord as he slept on a banyan leaf. He showed glorious mercy (*suprasādaḥ*). He had Mārkandeya who was suffering from the waters of devastation enter into him and delivered him from suffering.

His heart was clear of material attachment since he was complete in his desires (*prasannātmā*).

To astonish Mārkandeya who had entered him, he created all the universes (*viśva-dhṛk*).

Pervading the universes, he nourished them (*viśva-bhug-vibhuḥ*). One should see this topic in other works.

Eleven names describe the lord of Badarikāśrama. He performed beneficial acts for the devatās and Pitṛs as well as the brāhmaṇas (*sat-karṭā*).

He was worshiped by them (*sat-kṛtaḥ*).

He produced benefits for the people of Bharata and performed austerities (*sādhuḥ*).

He rejected persons who opposed (*jahāti*) what he said (*jahnuḥ*). This is formed from the root according to

**jahāte ‘rddhe ‘ntalopaḥ (Uṇādi 319).**

Thus he defeated the women sent by Indra and defeated Śiva in battle. This is described in Bhāgavatam and Nārāyaṇīya.

*narāj jātāni tattvāni narānīti vidur budhāḥ*  
*tasya tāny ayanaṁ pūrvaṁ tena nārāyānaḥ smṛtaḥ*

**The elements of creation (jīvas) that arise from the Lord (*****nara*****) are known by wise as** ***nāra*****. The shelter of these elements which exists previous to them is called** ***ayana.*** **Thus the** ***puruṣa*** **is known as Nārāyaṇa. Mahābhārata 13.124.22**

Nāra refers to the mass of jīvas. Their shelter is called Nārāyaṇa. Some say Nārāyaṇa means antaryāmī brāhmaṇa etc. This form is non-different from the source of the avatāras.

He is not destroyed (*naraḥ*). Others are destroyed (*rīyate* –root is *ra*) but he is not (*na*). The formation is explained by the rule

*anyeṣv api ḍṛśyate (Pāṇini 3.2.101).*

He has an indestructible form. He is a second form of the Lord.

### Verse 40

*asaṅkhyeyo prameyātmā viśiṣṭaḥ śiṣṭa-kṛc chuciḥ \|*  
*siddhārthaḥ siddha-saṅkalpaḥ siddhi-daḥ siddhi-sādhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**247. Asaṅkhyeyaḥ: he had unlimited qualities.**  
**248. Aprameyātmā: his determination was unbreakable.**  
**249. Viśiṣṭaḥ: he had unlimited powers.**  
**250. Śiṣṭakṛt: he inspired people to follow the Vedas.**  
**251. Suciḥ: he purified his worshippers.**  
**252. Siddhārthaḥ: his goals were achieved.**  
**253. Siddha-saṅkalpaḥ: his desires were fulfilled.**  
**254. Siddhi-daḥ: he fulfilled the desires of men and devatās.**  
**255. Siddhi-sādhanaḥ: he assisted people to complete their activities.**

**Комментарий:**

Though he wore the dress of a renunciate, he had unlimited qualities (*asaṅkhyeyaḥ*).

He had unbreakable determination (*aprameyātmā*).

He was endowed with unlimited powers (*viśiṣṭaḥ*). The servants of Indra did not know his unlimited qualities. They could not break his determination by lust, though they showed many beautiful women. He astonished them and gave them Ūrvaśī.

He made people steady to follow the Vedas and understand their meaning (*śīṣṭa-kṛt*).

From meditating on him or hearing about him, he purified others (*śuciḥ*). Or he lamented, longing for people’s auspiciousness.

Nine names describe the manvantara avatāras. He accomplished his purposes (*siddhārthaḥ*) and whatever he desired (*siddha-saṅkalpaḥ*).

He satisfied the desires of devatās and humans (*siddhi-daḥ*).

He made activities to become completed without obstacles (*siddhi-sādhanaḥ*). He assisted when people performed their activities.

### Verse 41

*vṛṣāhī vṛṣabho viṣṇur vṛṣa-parvā vṛṣodaraḥ \|*  
*vardhano vardhamānaś ca viviktaḥ śruti-sāgaraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**256. Vṛṣāhī: he assisted Indra and Śiva.**  
**257. Vṛṣābhaḥ: he shone because of introducing dharma.**  
**258. Viṣṇuḥ: he entered everything.**  
**259. Vṛṣaparva: he was a joy to Indra.**  
**260. Vṛṣodaraḥ: he protected Indra.**  
**261. Vardhanaḥ: as Hayagrīva, he manifested a form containing all the planets. 262. Vardhamānaḥ: he increased the prajāpatis.**  
**263. Viviktaḥ: he was untouched by matter.**  
**264. Śruti-sāgaraḥ: the Vedas rested within him as rivers flow into the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

He extended himself, assisting Indra and Śiva (*vṛṣāhī*). *Vṛṣa* means Śiva or Indra according to Amara-koṣa.

He shone (*bhāti*) because of dharma (*vṛṣa*), which protects the universe (*vṛṣabhaḥ*).

He entered (*viśati*) into all things, into all effects (*viṣṇuḥ*).

*vyāpya sarvān imān lokān sthitaḥ sarvatra keśavaḥ*  
*tataś ca viṣṇu-nāmāsi viśer dhātoḥ pradarṣanāt*

**Keśava spreads himself in all the worlds and situates himself there. Therefore you are called Viṣṇu, indicated by the root** ***viś,*** **to enter. Hari-vaṁśa**

He was a festival for Indra (*vṛṣa-parvā*).

He protected Indra as if Indra were in his womb (*vṛṣodaraḥ*). One can finds stories related to these names of the manvantara avatāras.

Twenty-four names describe Hayagrīva. He increased the prajāpatis (*vardhanaḥ*). Others say that he makes the small offering of the devotee great.

He manifested a form containing all the planets (*vardhamānaḥ*).

Though he spread everywhere, he remained untouched by everything (*viviktaḥ*).

He was the reservoir of the Vedas which are rivers (*śruti-sāgaraḥ*).

### Verse 42

*subhujo durdharo vāgmī mahendro vasudo vasuḥ \|*  
*naikarūpo bṛhadrūpaḥ śipiviṣṭaḥ prakāśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**265. Subhujaḥ: Hayagrīva protected the Vedas with his arms.**  
**266. Durdharaḥ: he could not be restrained by the demons.**  
**267. Vāgmi: he had pleasing speech.**  
**268. Mahendraḥ: he had unlimitedly great powers.**  
**269. Vasudaḥ: he divided the demons’ treasure.**  
**270. Vasuḥ: he displayed his greatness to the ignorant.**  
**271. Naikarūpaḥ: he had many forms.**  
**272. Bṛhad-rūpaḥ: he has a huge form.**  
**273. Śipiviṣṭaḥ: he had shining rays.**  
**274. Prakāśanaḥ: he revealed the Vedas to the brāhmaṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

He had arms which are glorious because they protected the Vedas (*subhujaḥ*).

He could not be restrained by Madhu and Kaitabha just as a great river current cannot be restrained by a sand dam made by children (*durdharaḥ*).

His speech was praiseworthy (*vāgmī*).

He had unlimitedly great powers (*mahendraḥ*).

He divided the treasure of the demons *(vasu-dah*).

He displayed (*vaste—*to wear) his powers to the ignorant (*vasuḥ*).

He had many forms (*naika-rūpaḥ*).

He has a huge form (*bṛhad-rūpaḥ*).

He was effulgent with shining rays (*śipi-viṣṭaḥ*).

*śaityāc chayana-yogāc ca śīti vārī pracakṣyate*  
*tat pānād rakṣaṇāc caiva śipiviṣṭa ihocyate*

**Because of its coolness (*****śaitya*****) and use as a bed (*****śayana*** **from** ***śī*****)** ***śīti*** **means water. Because the rays of the sun drink water (*****pā*****) and protect it (*****pī*****), the rays are called** ***śipi*** **(protecting and drinking water). Because the lord enters the rays or is surrounded by the rays, he is called śipi-viṣṭa in this world.**

Bringing the Vedas to Brahmā, he revealed the Vedas (*prakāśanaḥ*), after they had been taken by Madhu and Kaitabha.

### Verse 43

*ojas tejo dyuti-dharaḥ prakāśātmā pratāpanaḥ \|*  
*ṛddhaḥ spaṣṭākṣaro mantras candrāṁśur bhāskara-dyutiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**275. Ljas-tejM-dyuti-dharaḥ: he had strength of prāṇa, power to defeat, and intolerable brilliance.**  
**276. Prakāśātmā: he was full of knowledge.**  
**277. Pratāpanaḥ: he afflicted Madhu and Kaitabha.**  
**278. Ṛddhaḥ: he was endowed with all wealth.**  
**279. Spaṣṭākṣaraḥ: he revealed the meaning of the Vedas.**  
**280. Mantraḥ: he revealed secret knowledge.**  
**281. Candrāṁśuḥ: he shone like the moon.**  
**282. Bhāskara-dyutiḥ: he had power to defeat Madhu and Kaitabha.**

**Комментарий:**

When fearful Madhu and Kaitabha approached, he escaped from danger. He was thus full strength of prāṇa (*ojas*), capable of defeating the demons (*tejas*), and was intolerably brilliant (*dyuti-dharaḥ*).

He was full of knowledge (*prakāśātmā*).

He afflicted the two demons (*pratāpanaḥ*).

He was endowed with all wealth (*ṛddhaḥ*).

He revealed the meaning of the Vedas (*spaṣṭākṣaraḥ*).

He taught in private about his esoteric existence (*mantraḥ*).

He was as bright like the moon (*candrāṁśuḥ*).

Śvetaṁ candra-viśuddhābham: candra means white or having a pure complexion.

He had the power to overcome Madhu and Kaitabha (*bhāskara-dyutiḥ*).

### Verse 44

*amṛtāṁśūdbhavo bhānuḥ śaśa-binduḥ sureśvaraḥ \|*  
*auṣadhaṁ jagataḥ setuḥ satyadharma-parākramaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**283. Amṛtāṁśūdbhavaḥ: he arose from the moon.**  
**284. Bhānuḥ: he shone with power.**  
**285. Śaśa-vinduḥ: Dattatreya defeated persons advocating crooked paths.**  
**286. Sureśvaraḥ: he was respected by the sages.**  
**287. Auṣadham: he counteracted the poison of saṁsāra.**  
**288. Jagataḥ setuḥ: he opposed contamination of dharma.**  
**289. Satya-dharma-parākramaḥ: he defeated persons opposed to true dharma.**

**Комментарий:**

He arose from the moon (*amrṭāṁśūdbhavaḥ*). *Candramā manaso jātaḥ*: the mind arose from the moon. (*Puruṣa-sūkta*) Or he arose from the churning of the milk ocean.

He shone with power on killing Madhu and Kaitabha (*bhānuḥ*). This is described in Nārāyanīya.

Dattatreya is described with nine names. *Śaśa* means a person who move about in a crooked fashion. He knew the atheists such as Kapila (*śaśa-vinduḥ*), thus defeating them. *Vindu* is formed irregularly from *vid* (to know) according to vindur icchuḥ (Pāṇini 3.2.169).

*Sura* means those who shine because of accepting the Lord described in the Vedas. He spred himself to these people or influenced persons such as Vaśiṣṭha (*sureśvaraḥ*). This means he was respected by theistic sages.

He counteracted the poison of saṁsāra by the nectar of bhakti (*auṣadham*).

He opposed contamination of dharma (*jagataḥ setuḥ*).

He defeated persons who maintain no rules of dharma by presenting the highest dharma

*satya-dharma-parākramaḥ*

### Verse 45

*bhūta-bhavya-bhavan-nāthaḥ pavanaḥ pāvano ‘nalaḥ \|*  
*kāma-hā kāma-kṛt kāntaḥ kāmaḥ kāma-pradaḥ prabhuḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**290. Bhūta-bhavya-bhavan-nāthaḥ: he was the master at all times.**  
**291. Pavanaḥ: he moved about, purifying with his glance.**  
**292. Pāvanaḥ: he employed those who were pure.**  
**293. Analaḥ: he was not attained just by offerings.**  
**294. Kāma-hā: as Rṣabha, he destroyed desires by offering what was desired.**  
**295. Kāmakṛt: he destroyed material desire.**  
**296. Kantaḥ: he was attractive in his renunciate’s dress.**  
**297. Kāmaḥ: he was attractive to atheists.**  
**298. Kāma-pradaḥ: he destroyed material desires in those desiring liberation.**  
**299. Prabhuḥ: he was expert in all activities.**

**Комментарий:**

His glory is not temporary, but continues for all three aspects of time. He is the master in past, present and future

*bhūta-bhāvya-bhavan-nāthaḥ*

He moved about (*pavate*) with his merciful glance to deliver peopḷe (*pavana*ḥ).

He made use of those who are pure (*pāvanaḥ*). He purified people by means of the sages who are related to the Lord.

He was not attained just by plentiful offerings (*analaḥ*).

Ṛṣabha is described in six names. He destroyed people’s desires by offering what they desired (*kāma-hā*).

He cut material enjoyment since he had a cakra (*kāma-kṛt*).

As a parama-haṁsa, he was attractive in his renunciate’s dress (*kāntaḥ*).

He was attractive to the atheists such as Buddha (*kāmaḥ*).

He cut the material desire of persons desiring liberation (*kāma-pradaḥ*).

He was expert in all activities (*prabhuḥ*). One should see the life story of Ṛṣabha.

### Verse 46

*yugādi-kṛd yugāvarto naika-māyo mahāśanaḥ \|*  
*adṛśyo vyakta-rūpaś ca sahasra-jid ananta-jit \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**300. Yugādi-kṛt: the Lord starts the yugas at the beginning.**  
**301. Yugāvartaḥ: he rotates the yugas.**  
**302. Naikamāyaḥ: he has many types of knowledge.**  
**303. Mahāśanaḥ: he knows how to apply his knowledge and powers.**  
**304. Adṛśyaḥ: Kalki is not seen by the unqualified.**  
**305. Vyakta-rūpaḥ: he has a huge form.**  
**306. Sahasra-jit: he defeats many mlecchas.**  
**307. Ananta-jit: he annihilates all the mlecchas.**

**Комментарий:**

Four names indicate the yuga avatāras. The Lord starts the beginning of the four yugas by his appearance (*yugādi-kṛt*).

He causes the four yugas to rotate as previously (*yugāvartaḥ*).

He has many types of knowledge (*naika-māyaḥ*). He knows and establishes the dharma for each yuga. *Māyā* means knowledge according to Nigaṇta-koṣa.

He has great perception in applications of knowledge, renunciation and power (*mahāśanaḥ*). The forms such as Śukla with four arms in Kṛta-yuga are described above.

Kalki is described in four names. Since he has great powers, the mleccha people of his times cannot see him (*adṛśyaḥ*).

He has a huge body, filled with boldness (*vyakta-rūpaḥ*).

He destroys many mleccha armies (*sahasra-jit*).

He destroys all the mlecchas (*ananta-jit*). These names describe Kalki.

### Verse 47

*iṣṭo viśiṣṭaḥ śiṣṭeṣṭaḥ śikhaṇḍī nahuṣo vṛṣaḥ \|*  
*krodhahā krodhakṛt kartā viśva-bāhur mahī-dharaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**308. Iṣṭaḥ: Kṛṣṇa was worshipped by the devatās.**  
**309. Viśiṣṭaḥ: he was born with ornaments.**  
**310. Śiṣṭeṣṭaḥ: he was worshipped by the followers of the Vedas.**  
**311. Śikhaṇḍī: he wore a peacock feather.**  
**312. Nahuṣaḥ: he bound the people of Vraja with the rope of his beauty.**  
**313. Vṛṣaḥ: he showered them with happiness.**  
**314. Krodha-hā: he extinguished the anger of his mother.**  
**315. Krodha-kṛt: he became angry when Yaśodā left him to tend to the milk.**  
**316. Kartā: he destroyed many demons.**  
**317. Viśva-bāhuḥ: he protected the universe with his arms.**  
**318. Mahī-dharaḥ: he lifted a mountain, vowing to protect the people of Vraja.**

**Комментарий:**

Having given names for the vyūha, puruṣa, and līlā avatāras, now names of svayam-rūpa are given. Activities are listed in order several times. He was cherished by Brahmā, Śiva and other devatās as the object of their desire (*iṣṭaḥ*).

He appeared in Mathurā in the prison with brilliance, endowed with cakra, Kaustubha and other ornaments (*viśiṣṭaḥ*).

*tam adbhutaṁ bālakam ambujekṣaṇaṁ*  
*catur-bhujaṁ śaṅkha-gadādy-udāyudham*  
*śrīvatsa-lakṣmaṁ gala-śobhi-kaustubhaṁ*  
*pītāmbaraṁ sāndra-payoda-saubhagam*

**Vasudeva then saw the newborn child, who had very wonderful lotuslike eyes and who bore in his four hands the four weapons** ***śaṅkha, cakra, gadā*** **and** ***padma*****. On his chest was the mark of Śrīvatsa and on his neck the brilliant Kaustubha gem. SB** [**10.3.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.9)

He was worshipped by the followers of the Vedas (*śiṣṭīṣṭaḥ*).

Going to Vraja, he wore a peacock feather on his head (*śikhaṇḍī*). In the hundred and eight names he is described as *barhi-barhavataṁsaka*: he wears a peacock feather ornament.

He bound (*nahyati*) the minds of the people of Vraja by the rope of his beauty (*nahuṣaḥ*).

This word is formed according to the rule *pṛnatikalibhyaḥ* (Uṇādi sutra 518).

He showered them with happiness (*vṛṣaḥ*). In the hundred and eight names he is described as *nanda-vraja-janānandī*: he gave joy to the people of Vraja.

By his innocent gestures, he pacified the anger of his mother, who was upset with his fickleness as a child, when she was informed of his actions by neighbors (*krodha-hā*).

*itthaṁ strībhiḥ sa-bhaya-nayana-śrī-mukhālokinībhir*  
*vyākhyātārthā prahasita-mukhī na hy upālabdhum aicchat*

**The gopīs would look at Kṛṣṇa sitting there with fearful eyes. His mother, hearing the complaints, smiled softly but did not scold him. SB** [**10.8.31**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.31)

He became angry when Yaśodā went to tend the overflowing milk (*krodha-kṛt*).

*sañjāta-kopaḥ sphuritāruṇādharaṁ*  
*sandaśya dadbhir dadhi-mantha-bhājanam*  
*bhittvā mṛṣāśrur dṛṣad-aśmanā raho*  
*jaghāsa haiyaṅgavam antaraṁ gataḥ*

**Being intensely angry and biting his reddish lips with his teeth, Kṛṣṇa, with futile tears in his eyes, broke the container of yogurt with a piece of stone. Then he entered a room and began to eat the freshly churned butter in a solitary place. SB** [**10.9.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.6)

This anger nourished the pastimes. Anger arising from rajas does not exist in him, since he is beyond māyā. This was extraordinary behavior that did not occur often, since he by nature is full of bliss and knowledge.

He killed the demons sent by Kaṁsa *(karṭā*). The hundred and eight names describe him as *pūtanā-jīvita-haraḥ*: he took away the life of Pūtanā.

His arms protected the universe (*viṣva-bāhuḥ*).

He had a mountain which he held up (mahī-dharaḥ) to protect Vraja according to his vow. This word is formed according to the rule arśa ādobhyo c (Pāṇini 5.2.127).

The hundred and eight names describe him as govardhanācaloddharṭā: he lifted Govardhana.

### Verse 48

*acyutaḥ prathitaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇa-do vāsavānujaḥ \|*  
*apāṁ nidhir adhiṣṭhānam apramattaḥ pratiṣṭhitaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**319. Acyutaḥ: he never failed to protect Vraja.**  
**320. Prathitaḥ: he was famous for that.**  
**321. Prāṇaḥ: he gave life to everyone in Vraja by his prema.**  
**322. Prāṇadaḥ: he gave them strength.**  
**323. Vāsavānujaḥ: he manifested himself happily in front of Indra.**  
**324. Apāṁ nidhiḥ: he was the shelter of water during his coronation as king of the cows.**  
**325. Adhiṣṭhānam: he was non-different from his dhāma.**  
**326. Apramattaḥ: he was attentive to protecting the inhabitants of Vraja.**  
**327. Pratiṣṭhitaḥ: he was respected as the prince of Vraja, because of his vow to protect the people.**

**Комментарий:**

He did not deviate from protecting Vraja (*acyutaḥ*).

He was famous for that (*prathitaḥ*).

He gave life to the people of Vraja by his prema (*prāṇaḥ*).

He gave them strength (*prāṇa-daḥ*).

*prāṇo ‘smi prajñātmānaṁ mām āyur amrṭam upāsasva*

**I am prāṇa, the intelligent ātmā. Meditate on me as that prāṇa, the nectar of life. Kauśītaki-brāhmaṇa 3.2**

He manifested himself (*jaḥ*) with a smiling face before (*anu*) Indra (*vāsava*) who had committed an offense, to make him attentive (*vāsavānu-jaḥ*). The last word *ja* from the root *jan* is formed according to the rule *anau karmani* (Paninī 3.2.100).

He became the shelter of shining water, when he was installed as the lord of the cows (*apām nidhiḥ*). Indra says:

*ahaṁ kilendro devāṇāṁ tvam gavām indratāṁ gataḥ*

**I am Indra among the devatās. You are Indra among the cows. Hari-vaṁśa**

*prīyān na indro gavām*

**May indra among the cows be pleased. SB** [**10.26.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/26.25)

He is also the very form of the dhāmas such as Mathurā (*adhiṣṭānām*).

*sākṣād brahma-gopāla-purīti*

**Among them is the city of Gopāla, which is directly Brahman. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

*pañca-yojanam evāsti vanaṁ me deha-rūpakam*

**This place, measuring five** ***yojanas*****, is non-different from my body. Gautamīya Tantra**

He was attentive to protect all the inhabitants of Vraja (*apramattaḥ*).

*tasmān mac-charaṇaṁ goṣṭhaṁ man-nāthaṁ mat-parigraham*  
*gopāye svātma-yogena so 'yaṁ me vrata āhitaḥ*

**I must therefore protect the cowherd community by my potency, for I am their shelter, I am their master, and they are my own family. After all, I have taken a vow to protect my devotees. SB** [**10.25.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/25.18)

Because of those words, he was respected as the prince of Vraja (*pratiṣṭhitaḥ*).

### Verse 49

*skandaḥ skanda-dharo dhuryo varado vāyu-vāhanaḥ \|*  
*vāsudevo bṛhad-bhānur ādi-devaḥ purandaraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**328. Skandaḥ: he dried up the followers of Kaṁsa.**  
**329. Skanda-dharaḥ: he supported Skanda.**  
**330. Dhuryaḥ: he carried the burden of the universe.**  
**331. Varadaḥ: he fulfilled the garland maker’s desires.**  
**332. Vāyu-vāhanaḥ: he made the prāṇas of the universe flow.**  
**333. Vāsudevaḥ: he was the son of Vasudeva.**  
**334. Bṛhad-bhānuḥ: he shone with bright rays.**  
**335. Ādi-devaḥ: he is the cause of everything among the devatās.**  
**336. Purandaraḥ: he destroyed the cities of demons.**

**Комментарий:**

He dried up (*skandhati*) Kaṁsa’ṣ supporters (*skandaḥ*).

He supported the leader of the devatās’ army by his own śakti (*skanda-dharaḥ*).

He carried the burden of the universe (*dhuryaḥ*). This word is formed according the *rule dhuro yaṭ ḍhakau* (Pāṇini 4.4.77)He gave the garland maker whatever he desired (*varadaḥ*).

He enabled the prāṇa of the universe to flow (*vāyu-vāhanaḥ*). Some say the names means that his chariot (*vāhana*) went at the speed of the wind (*vāyu*).

He was the son of Vasudeva (*vāsudevaḥ*).

He had great rays of light (*bṛhad-bhānuḥ*).

He was the deva who causes everything (*ādi-devaḥ*).

*aham ādir hi devānām*

**I am the source among the devatās. BG** [**10.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.2)

He destroyed the cities of the demons (*purandaraḥ*). *Purandara* is formed irregularly according to the rule *vācaṁyama-purandarau* (Pāṇini 6.3.69).

### Verse 50

*aśokas tāraṇas tāraḥ śūraḥ śaurir janeśvaraḥ \|*  
*anukūlaḥ śatāvartaḥ padmī padmanibhekṣaṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**337. Aśokaḥ: he relived the suffering of the earth.**  
**338. Tāraṇaḥ: he delivered everyone from fear.**  
**339. Tāraḥ: he delivered people from birth and old age.**  
**340. Śūraḥ: he manifested great prowess.**  
**341. Śauriḥ: he was born in Śura’s dynasty.**  
**342. Janeśvaraḥ: he comforted the people of Mathurā.**  
**343. Anukūlaḥ: he was affectionate to them.**  
**344. Śatāvartaḥ: he manifested a whirlpool of powers.**  
**345. Padmī: he held a lotus in his hand.**  
**346. Padma-nibhekṣaṇaḥ: he glanced towards Gokula.**

**Комментарий:**

He destroyed the suffering of the earth (*aśokaḥ*).

He delivered everyone from fear of thieves (*tāraṇaḥ*).

He delivered everyone from fear of birth and old age (*tāraḥ*).

He showed great power (*suraḥ*). A sura is one who fights against or defeats thieves etc.

He was born into the family of Śura (*śauriḥ*).

He pacified the people of Mathura (*janeśvaraḥ*). By his power he spread into their hearts.

He was situated favorably towards them (*anukūlaḥ*).

He was a whirlpool of great powers (*śatāvartaḥ*). *Āvarta* means whirling water or illusion. Or he had many devotees like Nārada who could bewilder the demons like Kaṁsa.

He held a play lotus at all times (*padmī*). Or he possessed a mass of lotuses.

From Mathurā he gazed towards Gokula which resembled a lotus (*padma-nibhekṣaṇaḥ*).

*sahasra-patraṁ kamala gokulākhyaṁ mahat padam*  
*tat-karṇikāraṁ tad dhāma tad anantāṁśa-saṁbhavam*

**The dhāma of Kṛṣṇa called Gokula is the supreme abode. It is the center (pericarp) of a lotus with a thousand petals and it is sustained by a portion of the spiritual energy of Baladeva called Ananta. Brahma-saṁhitā** [**5.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bs/2)

### Verse 51

*padmanābho ‘ravindākṣaḥ padma-garbhaḥ śarīra-bhṛt \|*  
*maharddhir ṛddho vṛddhātmā mahākṣo garuḍa-dhvajaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**347. Padma-nābhaḥ: his navel was shaped lika a lotus.**  
**348. Aravindākṣaḥ: he hadeyes like lotuses.**  
**349. Padma-garbhaḥ: he was situated in the devotees’ lotus hearts.**  
**350. Śarīra-bhṛt: he maintained the devotees’ bodies. 3**  
**51. Mahardhi: he was endowed with all powers and wealth.**  
**352. Ṛddhaḥ: he had an abundance of great qualities.**  
**353. Vṛddhātmā: he spread his form throughout the universe.**  
**354. Mahākṣaḥ: his senses were most excellent.**  
**355. Garuḍa-dhvajaḥ: he had a flag marked with Garuḍa.**

### Verse 52

*atulaḥ śarabho bhīmaḥ samaya-jño havir hariḥ \|*  
*sarva-lakṣaṇa-lakṣaṇyo lakṣmīvān samitiñjayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**356. Atulaḥ: he was incomparable.**  
**357. Śarabhaḥ: he crushed the demons.**  
**358. Bhīmaḥ: he instilled fear in them.**  
**359. Samaya-jñaḥ: he knew the proper time to award his devotees.**  
**360. Havir-hariḥ: he accepted offerings of sacrifice.**  
**361. Sarva-lakṣaṇa-lakṣaṇyaḥ: he was endowed with all auspicious marks and qualities.**  
**362. Lakṣmīvān: he had an abundance of excellent tastes and fragrances.**  
**363. Samitiñjayaḥ: he was victorious in battle.**

**Комментарий:**

He was incomparable (*atulaḥ). Na tasya pratimā asti*: he has no measure. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

He crushed (*śṛṇāti*) the demons (*śarabhaḥ*). This is formed according the rule *kṛr-śṛr-śali* (Uṇādi sutra 405).

He instilled fear in them (*bhīmaḥ*).

He knew when the sādhana of the devotee had matured and gave the result (*samaya-jñaḥ*).

As the antaryāmī of the devatās, he directly accepted the offerings in sacrifices (*havir-hariḥ*). The ending is according to sarva-dhatubhya in (Uṇādi sutra 560).

The Lord had characteristics (*lakṣaṇa*) arising from his qualities and his markings. His qualities such as red complexion or having limbs were nirguṇa.

*pañca-sukṣmaḥ pañca-dīrghāḥ sapta-raktaḥ, ṣaḍ unnatḥa*  
*tri-hrasva pṛthu gambhiro dvātrīśal lakṣaṇo mahān*

**In a great person five elements are fine, five are long, seven are red, six are raised, and three are short, broad and deep. Thus there are thirty-two characteristics. Sāmudrika (quoted in CC Ādi** [**14.15**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/14.15)**)**

There are markings on the hands and feet. The hand for instance has a wheel and lotus and the foot has a thunderbolt and flag. There are nineteen marks. These are famous in the Purāṇas. A person possessing those characteristics is understood to be a great person. The Lord was endowed with all those characteristics (*sarva-lakṣṇa-lakṣaṇyaḥ*).

He was eternally endowed with a wealth of fine tastes and fragrances (*lakṣmīvān*).

He was victorious in battle (*samitiṁjayaḥ*), such as in killing Tṛnāvarta.

### Verse 53

*vikṣaro rohito mārgo hetur damodaras sahaḥ \|*  
*mahī dharo mahā-bhāgo vegavān amitāśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**364. Vikṣaraḥ: his affection never decreased.**  
**365. RMhitaḥ: he becames angry at the demons.**  
**366. Mārgaḥ: he was sought by the devotees.**  
**367. Hetuḥ: he was the cause of their affectionate feelings.**  
**368. Damodaraḥ: he was tied around the waist with rope.**  
**369. Sahaḥ: he tolerated the scolding by his mother.**  
**370. Mahī: he had continual festivals.**  
**371. Dharaḥ: he preserved his pastimes.**  
**372. Mahā-bhāgaḥ: he shared in an excellent manner.**  
**373. Vegavān: he moved quickly while playing.**  
**374. Amitāśanaḥ: as Govardhana he ate unlimitedly when offered food.**

**Комментарий:**

His affection for the surrendered never dissipated (*vikṣaraḥ*).

He became angry when the demons attacked the devotees (*rohitaḥ*).

He was always sought by the devotees because he was ever fresh (*mārgaḥ*). *Mṛg* means to search. It belongs the caurādi class.

He was the cause of bhāva which, nourishing his pastimes, arose in the devotees (*hetuḥ*). The verb *hi* means to move. The ending is thus *tu*.

He was bound around the waist with a rope by Yaśodā *(dāmodaraḥ*).

*sa ca te naiva nāmnātra kṛṣṇo vai dāma-bandhanāt*  
*goṣṭhe dāmodara iti gopībhiḥ parigīyate*

**The gopīs in Vraja called Kṛṣṇa Dāmodara because he was tied with a rope. Hari-vaṁśa**

Controlled by prema, he tolerated being scolded by his mother (*sahaḥ*).

He had continual festivals celebrating his birth constellation etc. (*mahī*). Amara-koṣa says the *maha* means a festival or joy.

He preserved his childhood pastimes (*dharaḥ*).

He was excellent at sharing (*mahā-bhāgaḥ*).

He was quick when playing with the cowherd boys (*vegavān*).

He ate plentifully when the cowherds made offerings to Govardhana (*mahāśanaḥ*).

### Verse 54

*udbhavaḥ kṣobhaṇo devaḥ śrī-garbhaḥ parameśvaraḥ \|*  
*karaṇaṁ kāraṇaṁ kartā vikartā gahano guhaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**375. Udbhavaḥ: he caused the sons of Kuvera to be born as trees.**  
**376. Kṣobhaṇaḥ: he agitated the mind by his attractive qualities.**  
**377. Devaḥ: he played in all his actions.**  
**378. Śrī-garbhaḥ: he showed Yaśodā the universe within his stomach.**  
**379. Parameśvaraḥ: he was the master of the greatest Lakṣmī.**  
**380. Karaṇam: he assisted his devotees to attain him.**  
**381. Kāraṇam: he was the efficient and material cause of the universe.**  
**382. Kartā: he carried out primary creation.**  
**383. Vikartā: he carried out secondary creation by entering Brahmā.**  
**384. Gahanaḥ: he was difficult to understand even by the intelligent.**  
**385. Guhaḥ: he hid his greatness from the people of Vraja.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of him, the sons of Kuvera were born as trees (*udbhavaḥ)*.

He agitated the minds of men when they heard about his qualities (*kṣobhaṇaḥ*). Others say he agitates prakṛti at the time of creation.

He played when killing lions etc. (*devaḥ*).

He had within himself the wealth of the universe (*śrī-garbhaḥ*). When he opened his mouth after eating dirt, Yaśodā saw the universe within him.

He was the master of the supreme Lakṣmī of Gokula (*paremeśvaraḥ*).

He assisted in remarkable ways the activities of the devotees to attain him (*karaṇam*).

Through his three śaktis he is the material and efficient cause of the world (*kāraṇam*).

He creates the universe using pradhāna and other elements (*kartā*).

He carries out secondary creation (*visarga*) consisting of making the planets by being present within Brahmā (*vikartā*).

He obstructs (*gāhate*) the intelligence of the most intelligent (*gahanaḥ*). He is hard to understand. *Gāh* also means to stir up. Because of him, people became absorbed in other things. *Gahana* is formed according to bahulam anyatrāpi (Uṇādi sutra 281) and kṛcchra-gahanayoḥ kaṣaḥ (Pāniṇī 7.2.22).

He hid these glories from the people of Vraja (*guhaḥ*). This word is formed according to *igupadha-jñā-prīkarāh kaḥ* (Pāṇini 3.1.135). Even though they knew his powers, by the influence of sweetness, he made that greatness disappear, since he desired the taste of pastimes.

### Verse 55

*vyavasāyo vyavasthānaḥ saṁsthānaḥ sthāna-do dhruvaḥ \|*  
*pararddhiḥ paramaspaṣṭas tuṣṭaḥ puṣṭaḥ śubhekṣaṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**386. Vyavasāyaḥ: he was verified as the highest truth.**  
**387. Vyavasthānaḥ: all truths reside in him particularly.**  
**388. Sāṁsthānaḥ: all elements merge in him.**  
**389. Sthāna-daḥ: he gave a position to Yudhiṣtḥira.**  
**390. Dhruvaḥ: he followed after his devotees.**  
**391. Pararddhiḥ: he had the highest prosperity.**  
**392. Parama-spaṣṭaḥ: he was glorified as the highest by the Upaniṣads.**  
**393. Tuṣṭaḥ: he was full of bliss. 394. Puṣṭaḥ: he was endowed with natural enjoyable items.**  
**395. Śubhekṣaṇaḥ: he had a merciful, auspicious glance.**

**Комментарий:**

He was verified as the higesht truth (*vyavasāyaḥ*).

Kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam: Kṛṣṇa is the complete manifestation of Bhagavān. (SB [1.3.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.28))

All truths reside in him particularly (*vyavasthānaḥ*).

All elements merge into him (*saṁsthānaḥ*).

He gave a position to his worshipper Yudhiṣṭhira (*sthāna-daḥ*). The hundred and eight names mentions *yudhiṣthira-pratiṣṭhāṭa*: he established Yudhiṣthira in his position.

He sought out (*dhuvati*) his surrendered devotees (*dhruvaḥ*).

*nirapekṣaṁ munim śāntaṁ nirvairaṁ sama-darṣanam*  
*anuvrajāmy ahaṁ nityam puyeyety aṅghri-reṇubhiḥ*

**I always follow the footsteps of my pure devotees, who are free from all personal desire, are rapt in thought of my pastimes, are fixed in me, without any feelings of enmity, and are equal to all conditions of the world. Let me be purified by the dust from their feet! SB** [**11.14.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.16)

He possessed the highest prosperity (*paraṛddhiḥ*).

He was glorified as the highest prosperity by the Upaṇiṣads (*parama-spaṣṭaḥ*).

He was full of bliss (*tuṣṭaḥ*).

He was furnished with enjoyable items like fragrance related to his svarūpa (*puṣṭaḥ*).

He had eyes which mercifully glanced, causing auspiciousness (*subhekṣaṇaḥ*).

### Verse 56

*rāmo virāmo virajo mārgo neyo nayo ‘nayaḥ \|*  
*vīraḥ śaktimatāṁ śreṣṭho dharmo dharmavid uttamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**396. Ramaḥ: he gives joy to the yogīs.**  
**397. Virāmaḥ: he is the conclusion of everything.**  
**398. Virajo mārgaḥ: he has a pure method of attainment.**  
**399. Neyaḥ: he is fixed in friendship.**  
**400. Nayaḥ: he was brought close by friends.**  
**401. Anayaḥ: auspiciousness cannot exist without him.**  
**402. Vīraḥ: he became irritated with Śiśupāla.**  
**403. Śaktimatāṁ śreṣṭhaḥ: he was the best of persons having powers.**  
**404. Dharmaḥ: he supported all beings.**  
**405. Dharma-viduttamaḥ: he was the greatest of knowers of dharma.**

**Комментарий:**

The yogīs find pleasure in him (*rāmaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *halas ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.121)

He is the conclusion of everything (*virāmaḥ*). The same rule applies here.

He has a path or sādhana to attain him which is pure (*virajo margaḥ*). He is attained by chanting which does not harm others.

He is fixed in his friendship (*neyaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *erac* (Pāṇini 3.3.56).

*yajasvābhispitaṁ yajñaṁ mayi śreyasy avasthite*  
*niyuṅkṣa cāpi māṁ kṛtye sarvaṁ kartā smi te vacaḥ*

**Perform the sacrifice to me situated in the highest good. Engage me in some service. I will follow your words. Mahābhārata**

At the sacrifice he washed the feet.

Kṛṣṇaḥ pādāvanejane: Kṛṣṇa washed the feet. (SB [10.75.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/75.5))

He is brought close by his friends through obligation (*nayaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *erac* (Pāṇini 3.3.56).

*pāṇḍavānāṁ bhavān nātho bhavantaṁ āśritā vayam*

**You are the master of the Pāṇḍavas. We surrender to you. Mahābhārata**

There is no good luck without him (*anayaḥ*).

Being irritated, he cut off Śiśupāla’s head when it was indicated (*vīraḥ*). One of his names is *śiśupāla-śirac chettā*: he cut off Śiśupāla’s head.

He could do this because he was the best of persons possessing power (*śreṣṭhaḥ*).

He was the support of all people (*dharmaḥ*).

*ye ca veda-vido viprā ye cādhyātma-vido janāḥ*  
*te vadanti mahātmānaṁ kṛṣnaṁ dharmaṁ sanātanam*

**The knowers of the Vedas, the brāhmaṇas, knowers of brahman, speak of great Kṛṣṇa as the eternal dharma. Mahābhārata**

Manu and other knowers of dharma obtain their life from his conduct (*dharmavit uttamaḥ*).

### Verse 57

*vaikuṇṭhaḥ puruṣaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇa-daḥ praṇavaḥ pṛthuḥ \|*  
*hiraṇyagarbhaḥ śatru-ghno vyāpto vāyur adhokṣa-jaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**406. Vaikuṇṭhaḥ: he was born to Vikuṇṭha previously.**  
**407. Puruṣaḥ: he was the leading person.**  
**408. Prāṇaḥ: he was the life of the devotees.**  
**409. Prāṇa-daḥ: he purified the devotees’ senses.**  
**410. Pra-ṇavaḥ: he was ever youthful.**  
**411. Pṛthuḥ: he treated Arjuna as his very seḷf.**  
**412. Hiraṇyagarbhaḥ: he was perceived through knowledge.**  
**413. Śatru-ghnaḥ: he destroyed the devotees’ internal obstacles.**  
**414. Vyāptaḥ: he was present within the devotees.**  
**415. Vāyuḥ: he killed Jarāsandha.**  
**416. Adhokṣa-jaḥ: he was born again beneath the cart.**

**Комментарий:**

He was previously born as the child of Vikuṇṭhā, wife of Śubhra (*vaikuṇṭhaḥ*). This word is formed according to the rule *śivādibhyo ‘ṇ* (Pāṇini 4.1.112)

*patnī vikuṇṭhā śubhrasya tasyāṁ jātaḥ puraḥ pumān*

**He was previously born to Śubhra, in the womb of Vikuṇṭhā, his wife. (similar to SB** [**8.5.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-8/5.4)**)**

Because he contacted various elements like earth he is called Vaikuṇṭha (penetrating) according to Nārāyanīya.

He was the chief of all persons (*puruṣaḥ*). The word is derived from the root *pur* means to live and to go ahead.

He was like the very life of the devotee (*prāṇaḥ*).

*manomayaḥ prāṇa-śarīro bhārūpa*

**He is made of mind, his body is prāṇa. His form is light. Chāndogya Upaniṣad**

He purified the senses of the devotees (*prāṇa-daḥ*). *Dai* means to purify.

He was ever fresh (*pra-ṇavaḥ*) or he was filled with life.

*praṇamantīha yaṁ vedas tasmāt praṇava ucyate*

**He is called praṇava because the Vedas offer him respects.**

He spread (*prathayati)* his youth to Pārtha (Arjuna) making him equal to himself (*pṛthuḥ*). *Prath* means to reveal or to spread everywhere.

He was known (*gīryate*) by knowledge (*hiraṇya-garbhaḥ*).

*jñānaṁ jñeyaḥ jṇāna-gamyam*

**He is knowledge. He is the object of knowledge. He is the goal of knowledge. BG** [**13.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.18)

He destroyed the devotees’ enemies such as kāma (*śatru-ghnaḥ*). This is formed following the rule amanuṣya-kartṛke ca (Pāṇini 3.2.53).

He was situated in the devotees, who had affection (*vyāptaḥ*).

He injured (*vāti*) and killed Jarāsandha (*vāyuḥ*). *Vā* means to injure or pierce.

He was again born beneath the axle of the cart (*adhokṣajaḥ*). *Akṣa* means a wheel, a cart, dice, or something terrifying according to Śrīdhara.

*adho ‘nena śayānena śakaṭāntara cāriṇā*  
*rākṣasī nihatā raudrā śakuṇī-veśa-dhāriṇī*  
*pūtanā nāma ghorā sā mahākāyā mahābalā*  
*visa-dagdhaṁ stanaṁ kṣudrā prayacchantī janaṛdane*  
*dadṛśur nihatāṁ tatra rakṣasīṁ vana-gocarāḥ*  
*punar-jāto 'yam ity āhur uktas tasmād adhokṣajaḥ*

**An angry Rākṣasī, entering the cart, was killed as Kṛṣṇa slept beneath the cart’s axel. Fierce Pūtanā with great strength and huge body, dressing herself auspiciously, gave her poisonous breast to Kṛṣṇa. Seeing that she was killed, the cowherds said, “He has been born again.” Hari-vaṁśa**

Others say that it means “he who produces happiness for the senses.” *Akṣa* in the neuter means senses according to Śrīdhara.

### Verse 58

*ṛtuḥ sudarśanaḥ kālaḥ parameṣṭhī parigrahaḥ \|*  
*ugraḥ saṁvatsaro dakṣo viśrāmo viśva-dakṣiṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**417. Ṛtuḥ: he enters the minds of the devotees.**  
**418. Sudarśanaḥ: he is attractive just by his form.**  
**419. Kālaḥ: he destroys.**  
**420. Parameṣṭhī: he dwells in the spiritual sky.**  
**421. Parigrahaḥ: he is accepted as supreme.**  
**422. Ugraḥ: he mingled with the gopīs who accepted him.**  
**423. Saṁvatsaraḥ: he stayed with them at all times.**  
**424. Dakṣaḥ: he increased in beauty when with the gopīs.**  
**425. Viśrāmaḥ: he relieved the gopīs of their fatigue.**  
**426. Viśva-dakṣiṇaḥ: he gave even himself to these devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

He goes (*ṛcchati)* into the minds of his devotees with his various qualities (*ṛtuḥ*). This is formed according to *artes catur* (Uṇādi sutra 71).

He is attractive just by his form, even if one does not know his qualities (*sudarśanaḥ*).

*yad dharma-sūnor bata rājasūye*  
*nirīkṣya dṛk-svastyayanaṁ tri-lokaḥ*  
*kartsnyena cādyeha gataṁ vidhātur*  
*arvak-sṛtau kauśalam ity amanyata*

**At the** ***rājasūya*** **sacrifice of Yudhiṣṭhira, all the people of the universe, seeing the form of the Lord which created auspiciousness in all directions, considered that the skill of the creator in making the most beautiful objects in the recent universe was nullified completely at that time. SB** [**3.2.13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.13)

He causes destruction (*kālayati*). Thus he is called *kālaḥ*. *Kal* means to disprerse or harm and belongs to the *caurādi* group of words.

Having removed the burden of the earth, he remained in the spiritual sky (*parameṣṭhī*). This is formed according to the rule *parame kit* (Uṇādi sutra 453).

The root is sthā and becomes  sthī according to Pāṇini 8.3.97.

He was accepted as the supreme, while people rejected the devatās (*parigrahaḥ*) or he was accepted by the gopīs during the Horikā pastimes. This is mentioned in Manu-smṛti.

He mixed (ucyate) with these insistent (grahikā) gopīs (ugraḥ).

Uc means to mix with. This is formed according to the rule ṛgendrtvādi (Unādi 188).

He remained (*vasati)* with them constantly (*samvatsaraḥ*).

Meeting with the gopīs, he increased in beauty (*dakṣaḥ*). *Dakṣ* means to increase or to be quick.

He relieved them of fatigue (*viśrāmaḥ*). It is formed like *prajñā* and other words. He relieved the gopīs of fatigue caused by the pastimes.

He gave as dakṣina everything, even himself ( viśva-dakṣiṇaḥ ). Dakṣiṇaḥ  is formed according to the rule dru-rakṣibhyām inan (Uṇādi-sūtra 210)

*Dadāty atmānam apy aja:* the Lord gives even himself.

### Verse 59

*vistāraḥ sthāvaraḥ sthāṇuḥ pramāṇaṁ bījam avyayam \|*  
*artho ‘nartho mahākośo mahā-bhogo mahā-dhanaḥ\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**427. Vistāraḥ: he spread the path of affection.**  
**428. Sthāvaraḥ: he became peaceful.**  
**429. Sthāṇuḥ: he will establish respect for scriptures.**  
**430. Pramāṇam: he speaks the truth.**  
**431. Bījam avyayam: he is the eternal cause of manifesting everything.**  
**432. Arthaḥ: he is requested by the devotees to attain pure bhakti.**  
**433. Anarthaḥ: he is not requested by persons with no devotion.**  
**434. Mahā-kośaḥ: he has great storehouses.**  
**435. Mahā-bhMgaḥ: he has many types of wealth.**  
**436. Mahā-dhanaḥ: he is supremely wealthy.**

**Комментарий:**

He spread the path of affection (*vistāraḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *prathame vāva śabde* (Pāṇini 3.3.33).

After relieving earth of its burden he will remain at peace (*sthāvaḥ*).

At the end of Kali-yuga respect for scriptures will be again established (*sthānuḥ*). The ending is established *by stho ṇuḥ* (Uṇādi sutra 320).

*sthāpayitvā sa maryādāḥ svayam-bhu-vihitāḥ śubhāḥ*  
*vanaṁ puṇya-yaśaḥ karmā jarāvān saṁśrayiṣyati*

**Having established respect for scriptures with bring auspiciousness to the earth, having executed actions which spread his pure fame, he will retire to the forest as if growing old. Mahābhārata 3.189.2**

He speaks the truth (*pramāṇam*). *Pramāṇam* means a lasting contract, speaking the truth and proof according to Viśva.

Avyayaṁ bījam is one name. Bījam means he causes something to be born ( jāyate ) with a particular way. The vowel becomes long according to the rule anyesāṁ api (Pāṇini 6.3.137).

Or he enters (*vrajati*) the effects. This is classed as a *pṛṣodarādiḥ* formation.

He is requested by the desireless devotees for pure bhakti *(arthaḥ*).

He is not requested by persons having material desires for pure bhakti (*anarthaḥ*).

*catur-vidhā mama janā bhaktā eva hi te smṛtāḥ*  
*eṣām ekantinaḥ śreṣṭhās te vai vānanya-devatāḥ*  
*aham eva gatis teṣāṁ nirāśīḥ sarva-karmaṇām*  
*ye tu śiṣṭās trayo bhaktāḥ phala-kāmā hi te matāḥ*

**Four types of people are my devotees. Among them, those who worship only me are the best. They are devoted to me alone. I alone am their goal. They have no desire for activities at all. The other three types however have desire for results. Mahābhārata**

He has a great storehouse (*mahā-kośaḥ*) in which there are unlimited types of wealth.

In the storehouse he has all treasures (*mahā-bhāgaḥ*).

Thus he is greatly wealthy (*mahā-dhanaḥ*).

*kim alabhyaṁ bhagavati prasanne śrī-niketane*  
*tathāpi tat-parā rājan na hi vāñchanti kiñcana*

**My dear King, what is unattainable for one who has satisfied the Supreme Lord, the shelter of the goddess of fortune? Even so, those who are dedicated to his devotional service never want anything from him. SB** [**10.39.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/39.2)

### Verse 60

*anirviṇṇaḥ sthaviṣṭho bhūr dharma-yūpo mahā-makhaḥ \|*  
*nakṣatra-nemir nakṣatrī kṣamaḥ kṣāmaḥ samīhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**437. Anirviṇṇaḥ: he does not disregard the faithless.**  
**438. Sthaviṣṭhaḥ: he is fixed as the śiśumāra.**  
**439. Bhūḥ: he is the support of everything as the pole star.**  
**440. Dharma-yūpaḥ: he unites dharma with himself.**  
**441. Mahā-makhaḥ: he is the object of great sacrifices.**  
**442. Nakṣatra-nemiḥ: he makes the constellations rotate as their antaryāmī.**  
**443. Nakṣatrī: the constellations depend on him.**  
**444. Kṣamaḥ: he has power to control and support the constellations.**  
**445. Kṣāmaḥ: he has a subtle form when everything is destroyed.**  
**446. Samīhanaḥ: he is desired by the pious.**

**Комментарий:**

He shines by protecting countless jīvas who do not recognize him (*anirvinnaḥ*).

*tārāmayaṁ bhagavataḥ śiśumārākṛtiḥ prabho*

**You are the form of śiśumāra composed of stars. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

He is the support of everything by his connection to the pole star (*bhūḥ*). The word is formed according to the rule *bhuvaḥ saṁjñāntarayoḥ* (Pāṇini 3.2.179). *Puccha-deśe dhruvaḥ sthitaḥ*: Dhruva is situated at the base. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa)

The zodiac consisting of constellations, stars and planets like sun and moon is bound to the śiśumāra by wind. The pole star is situated as the tail and makes the zodiac rotate. Thus the pole star is the support of everything.

He unites *(yauti*) dharma with himself as the head of that form (*dharma-yūpaḥ*). *Pa* is added to *yu,* which is lengthened to yū.

Dharmo mūrdhānam āśritaḥ: dharma takes shelter of his head. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa)

He is the object of great sacrifices (*mahā-makhaḥ*). The sacrifices are undertaken with him as the object.

As the antaryāmī of the constellations bound to the śiśumāra, he makes them rotate (nakṣatra-nemiḥ). The word nemiḥ is formed according to the rule nayo miḥ (Uṇādi sutra 486).

*alāta-cakravad yānti vāta-cakreritāni tu*  
*yasmāj jyotiḥsi vahati pravahas tena saṁsmṛtaḥ*

**Since the wind blows in the zodiac because of the Lord, the stars move like a firebrand in a circle of wind. Visṇu Purāṇa**

He controls the existence of the constellations *(nakṣatrī*). Or some say he manifests the excellent constellations.

He has the power to support and control the constellations (*kṣamaḥ*).

He remains in a subtle form along with the essence of the constellations when there is a periodic destruction (*kṣāmaḥ*). *Kṣau* means to destroy. Thus it refers to final destruction. The stars and śiśumāra are all destroyed.

*yāvan mātra-pradeśe tu maitreyāvastitho dhruvaḥ*  
*kṣayam āyati tāvat tu bhūmer ābhūta-saṁplave*

**While the pole star remains, the earth is destroyed in the devastation. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

Pious people direct their desires to him (*samīhanaḥ*).

### Verse 61

*yajña ijyo mahejyaś ca kratuḥ satraṁ satāṁ gatiḥ \|*  
*sarvadarśī vimuktātmā sarva-jño jñānam uttamam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**447. Yajñaḥ: he was born as the son of Ruci.**  
**448. Ijyaḥ: he was to be worshipped.**  
**449. Mahejyaḥ: he was to be worshipped with the best items.**  
**450. Kratuḥ: he was worshipped by Vedic actions.**  
**451. Satram: he delivered the devotees.**  
**452. Satāṁ gatiḥ: he was attained by the devotees.**  
**453. Sarva-darśī: he saw all devotees simultaneously.**  
**454. Vimuktātmā: he was not touched by matter.**  
**455. Sarva-jñaḥ: he knew everything.**  
**456. Jñānam uttamam: his form was the highest, all pervading.**

**Комментарий:**

He appeared as Yajña, the son of Ruci, in the womb of Ākūtī (*yajñaḥ*).

*jāto rucer ajanayat suyamān suyajña*  
*ākūti-sūnur amarān atha dakṣiṇāyām*

**The Prajāpati first begot Suyajña, in the womb of his wife Ākūti, and then Suyajña begot demigods, headed by Suyama, in the womb of his wife Dakṣiṇā. SB** [**2.7.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/7.2)

This form was to be worshipped (*ījyaḥ*).

He should be worshipped with great respect, by sixteen items (*mahejyaḥ*). *Ijya* is formed according to *vrajayajor bhāve kvap* (Pāṇini 3.3.98).

He was worshipped by actions prescribed in the Vedas (*kratuḥ*). This formed according to *kṛñ kratuḥ* (Uṇādi sutra 77).

He delivered the devotees (*satram*). He also had houses and wealth in the form of his devotees. Some say it means he has great treasures or houses.

He was attained by the devotees (*satām gatiḥ).*

He could see all his devotees at the same time (*sarva-darśī*).

*darśana-dhyāna-saṁsparśair matsya-kūrma-vihaṅgamāḥ*  
*svāny apatyāni puṣṇanti tathāham api padmaja*

**The fish nourishes its offspring by seeing, the turtle by meditation, and the birds by touch. I nourish my devotees with similar affection, O Brahmā.** ***Padma Purāṇa***

He was not touched by matter (*vimuktaḥ).*

He knew the ātmās completely since he is in all ātmās (*sarva-jñāḥ*).

He had an all pervading form (*uttamam jñānam*).

### Verse 62

*suvrataḥ sumukhaḥ sūkṣmaḥ sughoṣaḥ sukha-daḥ suhṛt \|*  
*manoharo jita-krodho vīra-bāhur vidāraṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**457. Suvrataḥ: he is satisfied with vows offered with affection.**  
**458. Sumukhaḥ: he smiles out of bliss.**  
**459. Sūkṣmaḥ: his very form is joy.**  
**460. Sughoṣaḥ: he attracts even Brahmā by playing his flute.**  
**461. Sukha-daḥ: he gives happiness by that sound.**  
**462. Suhṛt: he does so with no selfish motive.**  
**463. Manoharaḥ: he attracts the devotee by his beautiful form.**  
**464. Jita-krodhaḥ: he controlled his anger when Kāliya attacked.**  
**465. Vīra-bāhuḥ: he had strong arms which agitated the deep water of Kāliya’s one.**  
**466. Vidāraṇaḥ: he tore apart Bakāsura.**

**Комментарий:**

Though very satisfied with a well-executed vow the Lord enjoys what is offered with affection. He is satisfied and stops vow when it is offered with affection (*suvrataḥ*). *Vratād bhojana-tan nivṛttyor*: after the vrata one should finish it and take food.

He is beautiful with a smile because of his bliss (*sumukhaḥ*).

He has a form of spiritual joy (*sūkṣmaḥ*).

The sound of his flute attracts even Brahmā by its splendor (*sughoṣa*ḥ).

*savanaśas tad upadhārya sureśāḥ*  
*śakra-śarva-parameṣṭhi-purogāḥ*  
*kavaya ānata-kandhara-cittāḥ*  
*kaśmalaṁ yayur aniścita-tattvāḥ*

**Brahmā, Śiva, Indra and other chief devatās listen repeatedly to the sound. Although they are the most learned authorities, with hearts in submission, they cannot ascertain the meaning and forget everything. SB** [**10.35.15**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.15)

He is called *veṇu-vādya-visṭaradaḥ* (he who distributes the sound of his flute) in the hundred and eight names.

He bestows happiness by the sound of his flute (*sukha-daḥ*).

He plays the flute without selfish motives, for others’ benefit (*suhṛt)*.

He attracts the devotees’ hearts by his beautiful form with three bends (*manoharaḥ*). He is called *tribhaṅgī* and *madhurākṛti* in the hundred and eight names.

He did not become angry at Kāliya (*ajita-krodhaḥ*). The wives of Kāliya say:

*krodho 'pi te 'nugraha eva sammataḥ*

**Your anger toward him is obviously to be understood as your mercy. SB** [**10.16.34**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/16.34)

*aroṣaṇo hy asau devo yasya bhāgo ’yam udyataḥ \|*  
*na sa śakyas tvayā draṣṭum asmābhir vā bṛhaspate \|*  
*yasya prasādaṁ kurute sa vai taṁ draṣṭum arhati \|\|*

**O Bṛhaspati, neither you nor we can see Viṣṇu, who has no anger, and to whom you offered a portion of the sacrifice. Whoever gets his mercy alone can see him. Mahābhārata**

He had strong arms, with which he agitated the deep waters of the pond (*vīra-bāhuḥ*).

He tore apart Bakāsura, huge as a mountain, as if the demon were a blade of grass (*vidāraṇaḥ*).

### Verse 63

*svāpanas sva-vaśo vyāpī naikātmā naika-karma-kṛt \|*  
*vatsaro vatsalo vatsī ratna-garbho dhaneśvaraḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**467. Svāpanaḥ: he put Balaṛāma to sleep.**  
**468. Sva-vaśaḥ: he acted independently to perform Govardhana pastimes.**  
**469. Vyāpī: he spread himself everywhere to accept offerings.**  
**470. Naikātmā: he was not one form.**  
**471. Naika-karma-kṛt: he performed various actions according to his devotees’ desires.**  
**472. Vatsaraḥ: he herded the calves.**  
**473. Vatsalaḥ: he accepted the calves.**  
**474. Vatsī: he had countless calves.**  
**475. Ratnagarbhaḥ: he was like a jewel born in the mine of Yaśodā.**  
**476. Dhaneśvaraḥ: he had a wealth of kāma-dhenus.**

**Комментарий:**

Controlled by his devotion, Kṛṣṇa put Balarāma to sleep (*svapanaḥ*).

*kvacit krīḍā-pariśrāntaṁ gopotsaṅgopabarhaṇam*  
*svayaṁ viśramayaty āryaṁ pāda-saṁvāhanādibhiḥ*

**When his elder brother, fatigued from playing, would lie down with his head upon the lap of a cowherd boy, Lord Kṛṣṇa would help him relax by personally massaging his feet and offering other services. SB** [**10.15.14**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/15.14)

He acted independently in order to hold the Govardhana festival (*sva-vaśaḥ*).

He was present at all the houses simultaneously to accept the offerings (*vyāpī*).

Thus he was not just one person (*naikātmā*).

*ekatvam ajahad eva bahu-rūpaḥ*

**Giving up one form he became many.**

*eko 'pi san bahudhā yo 'vabhāti*

**Though he is one, he manifests as many. Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad 1.19**

He performed a variety of actions following after the mood of his devotees (*naika-karma-kṛt*).

He led and called the calves (*vatsaraḥ*).

He accepted (*lāti*) the calves (*vatsalaḥ*). Or he desired that the calves be happy with himself. *La* with *vatsa* or *aṁṣa* means desire or strength. *Vatsa-bāḍha-cara* is one of the hundred and eight names.

He has unlimited excellent calves (*vatsī*).

He was born like a jewel, in the mine of Yaśodā’s womb (*ratna-garbhaḥ*).

He was the master of a wealth of kāma-dhenus (*dhaneśvāraḥ*).

### Verse 64

*dharma-gub dharma-kṛd dharmī sadasat kṣaram akṣaram \|*  
*avijñātā sahasrāṁśur vidhātā kṛta-lakṣaṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**477. Dharma-gup: he protected Vedic dharma in Dvārakā.**  
**478. Dharma-kṛt: he performed dharma there to set an example.**  
**479. Dharmī: he is dharma itself.**  
**480. Sat: he exists in all three phases of time.**  
**481. Asat: he never becomes exhausted.**  
**482. Kṣaram: he evades difficult circumstances.**  
**483. Akṣaram: he is praṇava.**  
**484. Avijñātā: he overlooks the faults of the devotee.**  
**485. Sahasrāṁśuḥ: he has knowledge of everything.**  
**486. Vidhātā: he eliminates faults in the devotee.**  
**487. Kṛta-lakṣaṇaḥ: he distinguishes the devotees by their exclusive devotion.**

**Комментарий:**

He is now described as the lord of Dvārakā. He protected dharma described in the Vedas (*dharma-gup*).

*dharma-saṁsthāpanārthāya sambhavāmi yuge yuge*

**I appeare yuga after yuga in order to establish dharma. BG** [**4.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.8)

He performed acts of dharma to teach others (dharma-kṛt).

*na me parthāsti kartavyaṁ triṣu lokeṣu kiñcana*  
*nānavāptam avāptavyaṁ varta eva ca karmaṇi*

**For me, O son of Pṛthā, there is nothing in the three worlds that has to be done. What results are to be obtained are not already unattained by me. But I also perform duties. BG** [**3.22**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.22)

His conduct according to dharma is described in Hari-vaṁśa. Because he is the source of dharma (*dharmī*), he does not commit adharma.

Yaḥ kāla-kāra guṇī sarva-vid yaḥ: he is the controller of time, possessor of great qualities, and knower of everything. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

He exists in all three phases of time (*sat*). *sad eva śaumya*: the Lord is eternal, O gentle one!

He never becomes fatigued (*asat*).

He escapes from difficult circumstances (*kṣaraḥ*).

He is the form of praṇava (*akṣaraḥ).*

Om iti ātmānaṁ dhyāyatha: you should meditate on the Lord saying om. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad)

He overlooks the faults of the surrendered soul *(avijñātā*).

*api cet sudurācāro bhajate mām ananya-bhāk*  
*sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ samyag vyavsthito hi saḥ*

**If the most sinful person worships me with no other desire than to please me, I still consider that person to be my devotee, since he has fixed himself completely in me. BG** [**9.30**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.30)

Others say *avijñāt*a means he knows without any obstruction.

He has countless types of knowledge (*sahasrāṁśuḥ*). He has knowledge of all things.

He extinguishes the faults of the surrendered soul (*vidhāṭa*).

*sva-pāda-mūlam bhajataḥ priyasya*  
*tyaktānya-bhāvasya hariḥ pareśaḥ*  
*vikarma yac cotpatitaṁ kathañcid*  
*dhunoti sarvaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭaḥ*

**One who has thus given up all other engagements and has taken full shelter at the lotus feet of Supreme Lord is very dear to the Lord. If such a surrendered soul accidentally commits some sinful activity, the Supreme Lord, great because of his affection for the devotee, seated within everyone’s heart, immediately takes away the reaction to such sin. SB** [**11.5.42**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.42)

How does dharma of the surrendered soul who commits sin differ from that of the person who conducts himself properly and how do the two persons differ? He can infer the difference (*kṛta-lakṣaṇam*). The devotee gives up other bhāvas and worships only Kṛṣṇa (*bhajate mām ananya-bhāk*).

### Verse 65

*gabhasti-nemiḥ sattva-sthaḥ siṁho bhūta-maheśvaraḥ \|*  
*ādidevo mahā-devo deveśo deva-bhṛd-guruḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**488. Gabhasti-nemiḥ: he has a cakra.**  
**489. Sattva-sthaḥ: he is situated in the devotees’ hearts.**  
**490. Simhaḥ: he is like a lion in protecting the devotees.**  
**491. Bhūta-maheśvaraḥ: he is the lord of the chief devatās.**  
**492. Ādi-devaḥ: he is accepted by the devatās and he combats the enemies of the devotees.**  
**493. Mahā-devaḥ: he is absorbed in playing.**  
**494. Deveśaḥ: he is fully capable in all play.**  
**495. Devabhṛd-guruḥ: he instructs Brahmā and Śiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who have surrendered are excluded from Yama’s punishments. The Lord has a circle of rays or cakra (*gabhasti-nemiḥ)* called Sudarśana. Out of fear of the cakra, the servants of Yama do not want to attack the devotees.

Do the devotees not worry? The Lord is situated in the hearts of the devotees (*sattva-sthaḥ*). The ending arises according to the rule *su-pi sthaḥ* (Pāṇini 3.2.4).

He will fight with the servants of Yama who do not know the Lord’s nature (*siṁhaḥ*). He is like a lion but not in the forest.

He is the lord of others like Yamarāja who are lords of the living entities (*maheśvara*ḥ).

He is accepted by Yamarāja (*ādi*) and he desires to conquer the enemies of the devotees (*devaḥ*). Yama says:

*vasati manasi yasya so ‘vyayaṭmā puruṣa-varasya na tasya dṛṣṭi-pāte*  
*tava gatir athavā mamāsti cakra-pratihata-vīrya-balasya so anya-lokyaḥ*

**The Lord resides in the mind of the best of men. He is not subject to my glance. His destination is another abode, belonging to you, endowed with the dazzling strength of the cakra. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

*prabhavati samyamane mamāpi viṣṇuh*

**Viṣṇu spreads his power to my abode. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

He is greatly absorbed in play (*devaḥ*) with the dependent devas, as if playing with a ball and other items (*mahā-devaḥ*).

*bālaḥ krīḍanakair yāvat krīḍate 'smābhir acyutaḥ*

**The child Kṛṣṇa played with us like toys. Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa**

He was fully capable according to the task (*deveśaḥ*).

He gives instructions (*guru*) according to qualification to Brahmā and Śiva (*deva-bhṛt*).

### Verse 66

*uttaro gopatir goptā jñāna-gamyaḥ purātanaḥ \|*  
*śarīra-bhūta-bhṛd-bhoktā kapīndro bhūri-dakṣiṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**496. Uttaraḥ: he is the best.**  
**497. Go-patiḥ: he is the controller of the sun.**  
**498. GMptā: he protects everyone.**  
**499. Jñāna-gamyaḥ: he is attained by knowledge or a combination of karma and jñana.**  
**500. Purātanaḥ: he exists in all bodies.**  
**501. Śarīra-bhūta-bhṛd-bhoktā: he supports Vṛndāvana and enjoys it.**  
**502. Kapīndraḥ: he is the master of the monkeys.**  
**503. Bhūri-dakṣiṇaḥ: he gives generously to the brāhmaṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

He is in the highest position of excellence, being endowed with unlimited, variegated śaktis *(uttaraḥ*). *Uttaraḥ* means an answer, up, north, and the best according to Viśva.

*viśvasmād indra uttaraḥ*

**The Lord is superior to others in the universe. Ṛg Veda**

He is the lord of the sun (*go-patiḥ*). Nānārtha-varga says *go* means a cow, the sun, or an ox.

He protects everyone (*goptā*).

He is attained by knowledge or knowledge combined with karma but not by karma alone (*jñāna-gamyaḥ*).

He spreads himself *(ātanoti*) in the bodies (*pura*) of all jīvas or existed previously (*purā*), since he is not influenced by time (*purātanaḥ*).

He supports Vṛndāvana, which arises from his body (*śarīra-bhūta-bhṛt*).

*pañca-yojanam evāsti vanaṁ me deha-rūpakam*

**The forest measuring five yojanas is my body. Gautamīya-tantra**

He eternally enjoys that forest (*bhoktā*). This is a compound with *śarīra-bhūta-bhṛt*.

He is the master of the monkeys, who participate in his pastimes (*kapīndraḥ*).

He gives many gives to the brāhmaṇas at the time of celebrating his birth constellation (*bhuri--dakṣiṇaḥ*).

### Verse 67

*somapo ‘mṛtapaḥ somaḥ purujit purusattamaḥ \|*  
*vinayo jayaḥ satya-sandho dāśārhas sātvatāṁ patiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**504. Somapaḥ: he protected Śiva.**  
**505. Amṛta-paḥ: he protected the devatās.**  
**506. SMmaḥ: he was endowed with beauty and fame.**  
**507. Purujit: he defeated powerful enemies.**  
**508. Purushottamaḥ: he was the best among powerful persons.**  
**509. Vinayaḥ: he was especially brought by the Yadus.**  
**510. Jayaḥ: he was subservient to them.**  
**511. Satya-sandhaḥ: he kept his promise to protect them.**  
**512. Dāśārhaḥ: he was the object of their charity.**  
**513. Sātvatāṁ patiḥ: he was the lord of the Yadus.**

**Комментарий:**

He protected Śiva (*soma-paḥ*).

He protected the devatās (*amṛta-paḥ*).

He was endowed with beauty or fame (*somaḥ*). *Umā* means the ātasi flower, Pārvatī, turmeric, fame or beauty according to Viśva.

He conquered many powerful enemies (*purujit*).

He was the best among all powerful persons such as Śiva (*puruṣottamaḥ*). During the destruction of Tripura, when Śiva and the devatās became prosperous, Śiva’s chariot fell to the ground. The Lord raised the chariot. This story is told in Hari-vaṁśa.

He was especially *(vi*) brought by the Yadus to be served by them (*vinayaḥ*).

He was subject to them (j*ayaḥ*).

He made promises which were true. He promised to protect them (*satya-sandhaḥ*).

He was the object of their charity (*dāśārhaḥ*).

*ataḥ pātrataraḥ ko 'nyas triṣu lokeṣu vidyate*  
*kṛṣṇāt kamala-patrākṣād deva-devāj janārdanāt*

**There is no one more worthy of charity than lotus eyed Kṛṣṇa, the head of the devatās, in the three worlds. Hari-vaṁśa**

Or he was born in the Dāśārha dynasty.

He protected the Yadus (*sātvatāṁ patiḥ*).

*śriyaḥ patir yajña-patiḥ prajā-patir*  
*dhiyāṁ patir loka-patir dharā-patiḥ*  
*patir gatiś cāndhaka-vṛṣṇi-sātvatāṁ*  
*prasīdatāṁ me bhagavān satāṁ patiḥ*

**He is the protector of prosperity, sacrifice, the citizens, the intelligent, the planets, and the earth. He is the protector and goal to be attained for the Andhakas, Vṛṣṇis and Yadus. May the Lord, master of the devotees, be pleased with me! SB** [**2.4.20**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/4.20)

Or those who take Kṛṣṇa, sad-brahma, as their master are called *satvata*. This becomes *sātvataḥ.* He is the master of these devotees. According to others, *sātvatām patiḥ* means the master of those who spread dharma.

### Verse 68

*jīvo vinayitā sākṣī mukundomita-vikramaḥ \|*  
*ambho-nidhir anantātmā mahodadhi-śayo ‘ntakaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**514. Jīvaḥ: he brought the devotees to life after separation.**  
**515. Vinayitā: he nourished them with care.**  
**516. Sākṣī: he appeared directly to them.**  
**517. Mukundaḥ: he smiled attractively like a kunda flower.**  
**518. Amita-vikramaḥ: he treated them as himself.**  
**519. Ambhonidhiḥ: he takes shelter of the Garbodaka Ocean.**  
**520. Anantātmā: he acts as Śeṣa’s antaryāmī.**  
**521. Mahodadhi-śayo ‘ntakaḥ: greatly attractive, he sleeps on the waters of devastation.**

**Комментарий:**

By showing himself, he brought to life the devotees who suffered because of separation (*jīvaḥ*).

He nourished them by attentive care, as if they were his sons (*vinayitā*).

*ānanda-mūrtir bhagavān haris trailokya-pūjitaḥ*  
*draṣṭuṁ na sahate devi kliṣṭān sva-paricārakān*

**Kṛṣṇa, the form of bliss, worshipped by the three worlds, cannot tolerate to see his followers suffering.**

*Nay* means to protect or nourish.

He became visible and followed them (*sākṣī*). Or he directly saw those who were suffering from separation.

He became visible and showed his face, smiling like a kunda flower (*mukundaḥ*). This word is formed like *pṛṣodara.*

He thought of them as his very self (*amita-vikṛamaḥ*).

Kaimutya (showing his greatness) is then used to express this: he takes shelter of the water below Pātāla to support the universe (*ambonidhiḥ*).

He becomes the antaryāmī of Śeṣa who resides on that water (*anantātmā*).

In his expansion he sleeps on the great ocean of devastation upon Śeṣa ( mahodadhi-śayaḥ). The word śayaḥ is formed according to the rule adhikaraṇe śete (Pāṇini 3.2.15).

He is very attractive there (*antakaḥ*). *Antaḥ* means the edge, the end, destruction svarūpa, and very attractive, according to Viśva. The two names combine as one name *mahodadiśayo ‘ntakaḥ*.

### Verse 69

*ajo mahārhaḥ svābhāvyo jitāmitraḥ pramodanaḥ \|*  
*ānando nandano nandaḥ satya-dharmā trivikramaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**522. Ajaḥ: Kapila did not have a material birth.**  
**523. Mahārhaḥ: he was worthy of great worship.**  
**524. Svābhāvyaḥ: he was regarded by his devotees as the supreme Lord.**  
**525. Jitāmitraḥ: he removed obstacles on the devotee’s path.**  
**526. Pramodanaḥ: he was joyful with his father.**  
**527. Ānandaḥ: he was blissful on teaching his mother.**  
**528. Nandanaḥ: he gave her joy by being her son.**  
**529. Nandaḥ: he was joyful to be her son.**  
**530. Satya-dharmā: he was naturally endowed with dharma and knowledge.**  
**531. Trivikramaḥ: he is the goal of the three Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Kapila is indicated with twelve names. He was not born like jīvas with a material body (*ajaḥ*). “Born from the seed of Kardama” means that he received strength of bhakti belonging to Kardama.

He should be worshipped grandly (*mahārhaḥ*).

He is thought of with conviction by his devotees as the Lord (*svābhāvyaḥ*).

He destroyed the unfavorable elements for the devotee such as kāma (*jitāmitraḥ*).

Seeing his pure devotee Kardama, Kapila wept with joy (*pramodanaḥ*).

Teaching his mother Devahūtī, he became blissful (*ānandaḥ*).

He gave her joy as her son (*nandanaḥ*).

He became joyful to be her son (*nandaḥ*).

He possessed real dharma and knowledge, since these were related to him (*satya-dharmaḥ*). The dharma of atheistic Kapila is inferior.

He is the goal (*kramaḥ*) of the three Vedas (*trivikramaḥ*). Śiva says:

*trir ity evaṁ tatra vedāḥ kīrtitāḥ muni-sattamaiḥ*  
*kramate tāṁs tathā sarvāṁs trivikrama iti smṛtaḥ*

**The three Vedas are glorified by the best of sages. The Lord surpasses (*****kramate*****) them. Thus he is called Trivikrama. Hari-vaṁśa**

### Verse 70

*maharṣiḥ kapilācāryaḥ kṛtajña medinī-patiḥ \|*  
*tripadas tri-daśādhyakṣo mahāśṛṅgaḥ kṛtānta-kṛt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**532. Maharṣiḥ Kapilācāryaḥ: Kapila revealed the Vedas and had a pale complexion.**  
**533. Kṛta-jñaḥ: he knew the activities of Aṁśumān.**  
**534. Medinī-patiḥ: as Varāha, he was the protector of the earth.**  
**535. Tripadaḥ: he was non-different from om.**  
**536. Tri-daśādhyakṣaḥ: he helped the devatās.**  
**537. Mahāśṛṅgaḥ: he held the earth on his tusk. 538. Kṛtānta-kṛt: he slew Hiraṇyākṣa.**

**Комментарий:**

He explained all the Vedas (mahaṛṣiḥ). He was pale in complexion (kapilaḥ). The words is formed according to the rule found in Unādi sutra 57.

He taught about the pure ātmā (*ācāryaḥ*). The words are a *karmadhāraya* compound *kapilācāryaḥ*. This is combined with the adjective *mahārṣiḥ* as one name.

He was aware of the submissive activities of the grandson of Sagara, Aṁsumān (*kṛta-jñaḥ*). One can see the details in the story of Kapila.

Varāha is described with twenty-five names.

Varāha was the protector of the earth (*medinī-patiḥ*).

He is expressed in the three syllables of om (*tripada*ḥ).

He acted as a friend when the devatās were in trouble (*tri-dāśādhyākṣaḥ*).

He held the earth on his tusk (*mahā-śṛṅgaḥ*). Nārāyaṇīya says that Varāha held the earth on one tusk. Or he was most powerful (the highest pinnacle). *Śṛṅga* means powerful or a peak according to Viśva.

He killed Hiraṇyākṣa who was like darkness (*kṛtānta-kṛt*).

### Verse 71

*mahā-varāho govindaḥ suṣeṇaḥ kanakāṅgadī \|*  
*guhyo gabhīro gahano guptaś cakra-gadā-dharaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**539. Mahā-varāhaḥ: Varāha was gigantic.**  
**540. GMvindaḥ: he observed the earth’s situation.**  
**541. Suṣeṇaḥ: he was surrounded by the devatās.**  
**542. Kanakāṅgadī: he wore golden ornaments.**  
**543. Guhyaḥ: his form could not be understood clearly.**  
**544. Gabhīraḥ: his intentions could not be understood.**  
**545. Gahanaḥ: he was impervious.**  
**546. Guptaḥ: he was well protected by his śaktis.**  
**547. Chakra-gadā-dharaḥ: he held a cakra and club.**

**Комментарий:**

He took the form of a boar as huge as a mountain (*mahā-varāhaḥ*).

He observed the earth (*govindaḥ*).

He has an army of devatās (*suṣeṇaḥ*) or a splendid, abode like Mathurā capable of destroying beginningless ignorance.

He wore armlets of gold and gems (*kanakāṅgadī*). This indicates a golden crown and other ornaments of gold as well.

Such a form was difficult to understand *(guhyaḥ*). The suffix *ya* indicates eternal nature.

He could not be entered into, or understood in depth (*gabhīraḥ*). *Ga* comes from *gam* (to go) and *bhī* indicates inside. This is because he had unlimited qualities.

Hiraṇyākṣa could not penetrate him because he was dense (*gahanaḥ*).

He could not be harmed because he was protected by his śaktis (*guptaḥ*).

He held a cakra and club (*cakra-gadā-dharaḥ*).

### Verse 72

*vedhāḥ svāṅgo’jitaḥ kṛṣṇo dṛḍhaḥ saṅkarṣaṇo’cyutaḥ \|*  
*varuṇo vāruṇo vṛukṣaḥ puṣkarākṣo mahāmanāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**548. Vedhāḥ: he did service for the devatās.**  
**549. Svāṅgaḥ: he made them understand about dharma.**  
**550. Ajitaḥ: he could not be defeated by Hiraṇyāksa.**  
**551. Kṛṣṇaḥ: he had a dark complexion.**  
**552. Dṛḍhaḥ: he was very strong.**  
**553. Saṅkarṣaṇo ‘cyutaḥ: he did not stop pulling Hiraṇyākṣa around.**  
**554. Varuṇaḥ: he chose who should attain him.**  
**555. Vāruṇaḥ: he was not far from the devotees.**  
**556. Vṛkṣaḥ: he accepted the earth as his wife.**  
**557. Puṣkarākṣaḥ: he penetrated the ocean.**  
**558. Mahāmanāḥ: he gave liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

He did (*vidadhāti*) activities for the devatās (*vedhāḥ*).

Varāha made his followers, the devatās (*svān*), understand (*iṅgayat*i) dharma (*svāṅgaḥ*). The root *iṅg* means to go.

He could not be defeated by Hiraṇyākṣaḥ (*ajitaḥ*).

Varāha had the complexion of a blue lotus (*kṛṣṇaḥ*).

He was extremely strong (*dṛdhaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *dṛdha-sthula-balayoḥ* (Pāṇini 7.2.21).

He dragged Hiraṇyākṣa around thoroughly (*saṅkarṣaṇaḥ*). He did not abandon this action (*acyutaḥ*). These are combined as one name. He did not stop dragging the demon around.

He chose that the devotees attain him (*varuṇaḥ*). This word is formed according to the rule *kṛ-vṛd-āribhyā unan* (Uṇādi sutra 336).

*yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyaḥ*

**Whoever is chosen by the Lord attains him. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad** [**3.2.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/3.2.4)

He was not far from the devotees (*varuṇānām*), who had chosen him as their master (*vāruṇaḥ*). Or he came out of the water.

He accepted (*vṛkṣate*) the earth as his wife (*vṛkṣaḥ*). Or he cut up (*vṛṣcati*) ignorance.

He penetrated (*akṣnot*) the ocean (*puṣkarāksaḥ*). *Puṣkara* means a drum, water, sky, the sun according to Amara-koṣa.

He was inclined to give liberation (*mahā-manāḥ*). One should see the story of Varāha.

### Verse 73

*bhagavān bhaga-hā nandī vana-mālī halāyudhaḥ \|*  
*ādityo jyotir ādityaḥ sahiṣṇur gati-sattamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**559. Bhagavān: he is full of all qualities.**  
**560. Bhaga-hā: he destroyed the wealth of the demons.**  
**561. Nandī: he had a father named Nanda.**  
**562. Vanamālī: he wore a forest garland.**  
**563. Halāyudhaḥ: he had also the form of Balarāma.**  
**564. Ādityaḥ: he was previously the son of Aditi as Vāmana.**  
**565. JyMtir-ādityaḥ: he was as brilliant as the sun.**  
**566. Sahiṣṇuḥ: he tolerated the offenses committed by devotees.**  
**567. Gati-sattamaḥ: he was most attractive when he moves about.**

**Комментарий:**

He is called Bhagavān.

*śuddhe mahā-vibhūty-ākhye para-brahmaṇi śabdate*  
*maitreya bhagavac-chabdaḥ sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇe*

**O Maitreya! The word** ***bhagavān*** **refers to the parabrahman who is pure, full of all powers, and is the cause of all causes. Viṣṇu Purāṇa.**

*saṁbharteti tathā bhartā bha-kārārtho dvayānvitaḥ*  
*netā gamayitā sraṣṭā ga-kārārthas tathā mune*  
*aiśvaryasya samagrasya vīryasya yaśasaḥ śriyaḥ*  
*jñāna-vairāgyayoś cāpi saṇṇāṁ bhaga itīṅganā*  
*vasanti tatra bhūtāni bhūtātmany akhilātmani*  
*sa ca bhūteṣv aśeṣeṣu va-kārārthas tato 'vyayaḥ*  
*jñāna-śakti-balaiśvarya- vīrya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ*  
*bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ*

**The syllable** ***bha*** **means the bestower and the maintainer. The syllable** ***ga*** **means the giver of prema, creator of qualities in the devotee and the cause of going to Vaikuṇṭha. The six** ***bhagas*** **are complete control, influence, good qualities, wealth, knowledge and renunciation. The syllable** ***va*** **means the indestructible Lord in whom all beings dwell and who dwells in all beings. The word** ***bhagavān*** **means that he is complete with power of mind (omniscience), power of senses, power of body, influence, control, and beauty, and is devoid of all inferior qualities. Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**6.5.79**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.5.79)

He destroyed the wealth (*bhaga*) of those who disrupted dharma (*bhaga-hā*).

He had an eternal father named Nanda (*nandī*). The hundred and eight names calls him *nanda-gopa-priyātmaja.*

He wore a forest garland at all times, offered by the gopīs (*vana-mālī*).

He had a form called Balarāma (*halayudhaḥ*).

He previously appeared as Vāmana, the son of Aditi (*ādityaḥ*).

This word is formed according to the rule *dity-adityāditya-patyuttarapadāṇṇyaḥ* (Pāṇini 4.2.85).

He was bright like the sun, the son of Āditi (*jyotir-ādityaḥ*).

Though bright like the sun, he was inclined to forgive the offenses of the surrendered (*sahiṣṇuḥ*).

He was most brilliant when he moved about (*gati-sattamaḥ*). Or he is the best among those who should be sought for shelter.

### Verse 74

*sudhanvā khaṇḍa-paraśur dāruṇo draviṇa-pradaḥ \|*  
*diva-spṛk sarva-dṛg-vyāso vācaspatir ayoni-jaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**568. Sudhanvā: as Paraśurāma he had a bow.**  
**569. Khaṇda-paraśuḥ: he held a battle-axe to destroy enemies.**  
**570. Dāruṇaḥ: he was harsh to the warriors.**  
**571. Draviṇa-pradaḥ: he gave wealth to the brāhmaṇas.**  
**572. Divah-spṛk: Vyāsa wrote about the spiritual realm.**  
**573. Sarva-dṛg-vyāsaḥ: he compiled the Vedas which present knowledge.**  
**574. Vācaspatiḥ: he manifested literary works like the Purāṇas.**  
**575. Ayonijaḥ: he was born from the Lord’s words.**

**Комментарий:**

Paraśurāma is indicated with four names. He had a glorious devotee as his bow (*su-dhanvā*).

He had an axe to cut down all the warriors (*khaṇḍa-paraśuḥ*).

He was merciless to the kṣatriyas (*dāruṇaḥ*).

At the sacrifice performed after destroying all the kṣatriyas, on the land he had conquered, he gave wealth to the brāhmaṇa priests (*draviṇa-pradaḥ*).

Vyāsa is indicated with ten names. He wrote about the spiritual realm factually (*diva-spṛk*).

He compiled the Vedas with four sections from which one attains knowledge (*sarva-dṛg vyāsaḥ*).

He manifested words in the form of Vedānta, itihāsa, and Purāṇa (*vācas-patiḥ*).

He was born from the words of the Lord, as an avatāra of eloquence (*ayoni-jaḥ*).

*atha bhuyo jagat-straṣtā bho-śabdenānunādayan*  
*sarasvatīm uccacāra tatra sārasvato ‘bhavad*

**The creator of the universe again spoke the word** ***bho*** **and released eloquence (Sarasvatī).** **Vyāsa appeared there. Mahābhārata 12.337.37**

### Verse 75

*tri-sāmā sāmagaḥ sāma nirvāṇaṁ bheṣajaṁ bhiṣak \|*  
*saṁnyāsa-kṛc chamaḥ śānto niṣṭhā śāntiḥ parāyaṇam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**576. Tri-sāmā: he manifested the three sāmaṣ.**  
**577. Sāma-gaḥ: he sang the sāmas, giving knowledge to his disciples.**  
**578. Sāma: he destroyed ignorance.**  
**579. Nirvāṇaṁ: he taught liberation.**  
**580. Bheṣajaṁ: he was the medicine for saṁsāra.**  
**581. Bhiṣak: he showed the final cause.**  
**582. Saṁnyāsa-kṛt: As Caitanya, he took sannyāsa.**  
**583. Samaḥ: he reflected on the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa.**  
**584. Sāntaḥ: he was detached.**  
**585. Niṣṭhā: he was fixed in kīrtana.**  
**586. Śāntiḥ: he pacified the opposition with kīrtana.**  
**587. Parāyaṇam: he was the refuge of persons of the highest bhāvas.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa manifested the three sāmās: veda, vrata and sama (*tri-sāmā*).

He sang all the sāmās with devotion (sāma-ga). This word is formed according to the rulegāpoṣtaka (Pāṇini 3.2.8).

This means he taught spiritual knowledge to his disciples.

He destroyed ignorance (*sāmaḥ*). The root is *so*, meaning to destroy. This has the ending *ma.*

Destroying ignorance, he bestowed liberation (*nirvāṇam*).

He is the medicine that destroys the disease of ignorance (*bheṣajam*).

He showed the final cause (*bhiṣak*).

Kṛṣṇa Caitanya is indicated with six names. He tooks sannyāsa (*sannyasa-kṛt*).

He reflected on the confidential pastimes of the Lord (*śamaḥ*). It is formed like the words of caurādi group.

He was detached from everything except Kṛṣṇa (*śāntaḥ*).

He was fixed in the sacrifice of chanting the Lord’s names (*niṣṭhā*).

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**The intelligent people worship the Lord, black in color, but shining with brilliance, who is accompanied by his associates, weapons, major and minor limbs, chiefly through chanting. SB** [**11.5.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.32)

He pacified the advaitins who were against bhakti by performing kīrtana (*śāntaḥ*).

He was the highest refuge for person of various bhāvas, up to mahābhāva (*parāyaṇam*).

### Verse 76

*śubhāṅgaḥ śāntidaḥ sraṣṭā kumudaḥ kuvaleśayaḥ \|*  
*go-hito go-patir goptā vṛṣa-bhākṣo vṛṣa-priyaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**588. Śubhāṅgaḥ: Kṛṣṇa had limbs attractive to the peopḷe of Vraja.**  
**589. Śānti-daḥ: he pacified their minds with his smile.**  
**590 Sraṣṭā: he created bliss for them.**  
**591. Ku-mudaḥ: he rejoiced in Vṛndāvana.**  
**592. Kuvaleśayaḥ: his hand held his flute.**  
**593. Go-hitaḥ: he benefited the cows.**  
**594. Go-patiḥ: he was the lord of the cows.**  
**595. Goptā: he protected the cows.**
