**596. Vṛṣa-bhākṣaḥ: he considered the bull to be a demon.**  
**597. Vṛṣa-priyaḥ: he was affectionate to the other bulls.**

**Комментарий:**

Again Svayam rūpa Kṛṣṇa is indicated. He had auspicious limbs--attractive to the people of Vraja (*śubhāṅgaḥ)*.

By his smile he pacified their minds more than peaceful persons can accomplish (*śanti-daḥ*).

He created a wealth of bliss for them (*sraṣṭā*).

He enjoyed on the earth (*ku*) of Vṛndāvana (*kumudaḥ*)

This word is formed according to the rule igupadha-jñā-prī-kṛiaḥ kaḥ (Pāṇini 3.1.135).

*vṛndāvanaṁ govardhanaṁ yamunā-pulināni ca*  
*vīkṣyāsīd uttamā prītī rāma-mādhavayor nṛpa*

**O King Parīkṣit, when Rāma and Kṛṣṇa saw Vṛndāvana, Govardhana, and the banks of the river Yamunā, they both enjoyed great pleasure. SB** [**10.11.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/11.36)

His hand held a flute—which existed (*valate*) on earth (*ku*) with its sweet, enchanting sound (*kuvaleśayaḥ*). One should see the description in Venu-gītā.

The word is formed according to the rule śaya-vaṣa-vāsiṣv akālāt (Pāṇini 6.2.18).

*Śaya* means hand according to Amara-koṣa.

He gave benefit to the cows (*go-hitaḥ*).

He was the lord of the cows (*go-patiḥ*).

He protected them (*goptā*).

He had his eyes (*akṣa*) on the bull demon (*vṛṣabha*) who had come to Vraja for committing violence (*vṛṣabhākṣaḥ*). He saw him as an enemy of Vraja.

Otherwise, his bulls were dear to him (*vṛṣa-priyaḥ*). The bull demon should be killed in order to protect his dear bulls.

### Verse 77

*anivartī nivṛttātmā saṁkṣeptā kṣemakṛc chivaḥ \|*  
*śrīvatsa-vakṣāḥ śrī-vāsaḥ śrī-patiḥ śrī-matāṁ varaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**598. Anivartī: he did not turn from his friends when the demon approached.**  
**599. Nivṛttātmā: he turned from his friends when he prepared to fight.**  
**600. Saṁkṣeptā: he killed the demon.**  
**601. Kṣema-kṛt śivaḥ: benefiting the people of Vraja, he then purified himself.**  
**602. Śrīvatsa-vakṣāḥ: he was marked with śrīvatsa.**  
**603. Śrī-vāsaḥ: he lingered on the beautiful slopes of Govardhana.**  
**604. Śrī-patiḥ: he protected beautiful Govardhana from Indra.**  
**605. Śrī-matāṁ varaḥ: he was selected as the best among his intelligent and eloquent friends.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa did not give up his friends in front of Vṛṣāsura (*anivartī*). Putting his arm on a friend’s shoulder, he ignored the demon.

When he faced the demon, he gave up his friend (*nivṛtātmā*). Putting everyone on one side, he prepared to fight with the demon.

Grabbing the demon’s horns, he killed him (*saṁkṣeptā*). *Vṛṣabhāsur-vidhvaṁsī* is one of the hundred and eight names.

He produced auspiciousness for Vraja (*kṣema-kṛt*). When the gopīs objected that he had committed a sin by killing a bull, he called the tīrthas such as Gaṅgā, and became pure by that water (*śivaḥ*). One should see the story of the killing of Vṛṣāsura and others in the Tenth Canto of Bhāgavatam. *Kṣema-kṛt* and *śiva* are combined as one name. Having killed Vṛṣāsura, he had no rival and rested peacefully (*śivaḥ*).

He has the śrivatsa mark on his chest. It is a whorl of white hair turning to the right. It is a unique mark of the supreme Lord.

*ātma-tulyaiḥ ṣoḍaśabhir vinā śrīvatsa-kaustubhau*

**Surrounding and serving Nārāyaṇa, were sixteen personal attendants, appearing exactly like him but without the mark of Śrīvatsa and the Kaustubha jewel. SB** [**6.9.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/9.29)

He was attached to staying on the beautiful ridges and plateaus of Govardhana (*śrī-vāsaḥ*).

He protected those beautiful slopes from Indra’s anger (*śrī-patiḥ*).

He was selected as the best among his friends who were intelligent or eloquent (*śrimatāṁ varaḥ*).

### Verse 78

*śrī-daḥ śrīśaḥ śrī-nivāsaḥ śrī-nidhiḥ śrī-vibhāvanaḥ \|*  
*śrī-dharaḥ śrī-karaḥ śreyaḥ śrīmān lMka-trayāśrayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**606. Śrī-daḥ: he nourished the ornamentation of his friends.**  
**607. Śrīśaḥ: he controlled the wealth of his devotees.**  
**608. Śrī-nivāsaḥ: he resided in Rādhā.**  
**609. Śrī-nidhiḥ: he placed Rādhā within himself.**  
**610. Śrī-vibhāvanaḥ: he perceived her by her great qualities.**  
**611. Śrī-dharaḥ: he possesses her eternally.**  
**612. Śrī-karaḥ: he embraced her.**  
**613. Śreyaḥ śrimān: he eternally possesses the highest śakti or śrī.**  
**614. LMka-trayāśrayaḥ: he is the shelter of the three worlds.**

**Комментарий:**

He nourished (*daḥ*) the dress and ornamentation of his friends (*śrī-daḥ*).

He controlled the wealth of his devotees (*śrīśaḥ*).

He lived continually, thinking of the great Lakṣmī of Gokula, the superintendent of all things there, who follows him whenever he appears in the world with his aṁṣas (*śrī-nivasaḥ*).

She was placed (*nidhiyate*) in him, like a cluster of gems in a sapphire case (*śrī-nidhiḥ*). She is called *śrī* because she takes shelter (*śrayaṇa*) of him.

He perceived (*vibhāvayati*) her by her wealth of qualities, like a stream of fragrance (*śrī-vibhāvaṇaḥ*).

He possessed her, like fire possesses heat (*śrī-dharaḥ*).

He placed his hands on her (*śṛi-karaḥ*). This means he embraces her. The six names starting with *śrī-nivasaḥ* indicate Rādhā.

The most excellent śakti called *śrī* exists with him eternally as his possession (*śreyaḥ śṛīmān*).

*dve pārśve candrāvalī rādhikā ceti*  
*yasyāṁśena lakṣmī-durgādikā-śaktiḥ iti*

**On Kṛṣṇa’s two sides are Candrāvalī and Rādhikā. Lakṣmī, Durgā and others exist. They are all his expansions. Puruṣa-bodhinī śruti.**

*rādhayā mādhavo devo mādhavenaiva rādhikā vibhrājante janeṣvā*

**Mādhava is revealed to the people through Rādhā and Rādhā is revealed through Mādhava. Ṛk-pariśiṣṭa**

*sattvaṁ tattvaṁ paratvaṁ ca tattva-trayam ahaṁ kila*  
*tri-tattva-rūpiṇī sā ca rādhikā mama vallabhā*  
*rakṛteḥ para evāhaṁ sāpi mac cakti-rūpiṇī*

**I am the three principles: sattva (effect), tattva (case) and paratva (spiritual energy). The form of these three is Rādhikā, my consort. I am beyond prakṛti and she is my śakti. Gautamīya-tantra**

*devī kṛṣṇamayī proktārādhikā para-devatā*  
*sarva-lakṣmīmayī sarva- kāntiḥ sammohinī parā*

**Dear Rādhā, filled with thoughts of Kṛṣṇa, is the supreme goddess. She is the embodiment of all women, possesses all beauty, and is the supreme bewilderer. Gautamīya-tantra**

With his eternal beauty and wealth, he is the shelter of the three worlds, starting with Gokula (*loka-trayāśrayaḥ*).

### Verse 79

*svakṣaḥ svaṅgaḥ śatānaṅdo naṅdir jyotir gaṇeśvaraḥ \|*  
*vijitātmā vidheyātmā satkīrtiś chinnasaṁśayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**615. Svakṣaḥ: he has glorious senses.**  
**616. Svaṅgaḥ: he has an attractive body.**  
**617. Śatānandaḥ: he gives Rādhā bliss in a hundred ways.**  
**618. Nandiḥ: he enjoys with her.**  
**619. JyMtir-gaṇeśvaraḥ: he has a host of shining servants.**  
**620. Vijitātmā: he is controlled by his devotees.**  
**621. Vidheyātmā: he is submissive to them.**  
**622. Sat-kīrtiḥ: he is famous for this.**  
**623. Chinna-saṁśayaḥ: he destroys the devotees’ doubts.**

**Комментарий:**

He has glorious (*su*) senses (*akṣaḥ*) because of enjoying an ocean of nectar consisting of beauty, fragrance and sweetness of Rādhā (*svakṣaḥ*).

He has an attractive body desired by Rādhā (*svaṅgaḥ*).

He gives her joy, with hundreds of his manifestations (*śatānandaḥ*).

*ciṇtāmaṇi-prakara-sadmasu kalpavṛkṣa-*  
*lakṣāvṛteṣu surabhīr abhipālayantam*  
*lakṣmī-sahasra-śata-sambhrama-sevyamānaṁ*  
*govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who affectionately tends the cows in stables whose walls are made of cintāmaṇi, surrounded by desire trees. He is eagerly served by countless** ***gopīs.*** **Brahma-saṁhitā**

He rejoices with her and other gopīs at all times in Vraja (*nandiḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *sarva-dhātubhya in* (Uṇādi sutra 560).

He is the master of a host of eternal servants shining with bodies equipped with umbrellas, cāmaras etc. (*jyotir-gaṇeśvaraḥ*).

He is dependent on authority because of his gentle qualities (*vijitātmā*).

He is submissive to his devotee in resting, sitting or going (*vidheyātmā*).

He is famous for this quality (*sat-kīrtiḥ*).

He destroys all doubts of his devotees on all subjects (*china-saṁśayaḥ*).

*tvad-anyaḥ saṁśayasyāsya chettā na by upapadyate*

**No one can be the destroyer of this doubt except you. BG** [**7.39**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.39)

### Verse 80

*udīrṇaḥ sarvataś cakṣur anīśaḥ śāśvata-sthiraḥ \|*  
*bhūśayo bhūṣaṇo bhūtir viśokaḥ śoka-nāśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**624. Udīrṇaḥ: by these qualities his position is exalted in the scriptures.**  
**625. Sarvataḥ-cakṣuḥ: he gazes on all devotees with affection.**  
**626. Anīśaḥ: he is controlled by his devotees.**  
**627. Śāśvata-sthiraḥ: he is steady in constantly associating with his devotees.**  
**628. Bhū-śayaḥ: he lay on the earth.**  
**629. Bhūṣaṇaḥ: he ornamented the earth with his footprints.**  
**630. Bhūtiḥ: he fulfilled the earth’s desires.**  
**631. Viśokaḥ: he destroyed the gopīs’ lamentation.**  
**632. Śoka-nāśanaḥ: he destroyed the lamentation of his parents.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of such qualities Kṛṣṇa is exalted in the scriptures (*udīrṇaḥ*).

He sees all the devotees with affection (*sarvataś cakṣuḥ*).

*darśana-dhyāna-saṁsparśair matsya-kūrma-vihaṅgamāḥ*  
*svāny apatyāni puṣṇanti tathāham api padmaja*

**The fish nourishes its offspring by seeing, the turtle by meditation, and the birds by touch. I nourish my devotees with similar affection, O Brahmā.** ***Padma Purāṇa***

In all conditions he depends on his devotees (*anīśaḥ*).

*aham bhaka-parādhīnaḥ*

**I am completely under the control of my devotees. SB** [**9.4.63**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.63)

He is steadfast at all times in the company of his devotees (*śāśvata-sthiraḥ*).

He rests on the ground of Vṛndāvana (*bhūśayaḥ*).

*kvacit pallava-talpeṣu niyuddha-śrama-karṣitaḥ*  
*vṛkṣa-mūlāśrayaḥ śete gopotsaṅgopabarhaṇaḥ*

**Sometimes Kṛṣṇa grew tired from fighting and lay down at the base of a tree, resting upon a bed made of soft twigs and buds and using the lap of a cowherd friend as his pillow. SB** [**10.15.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/15.16)

*Vṛndāvanāntaḥ sañcārī* is one of the hundred and eight names. Or he lay down on the ground in a bower, covered with a shawl, along with some gopī whom he has met privately.

He ornamented the earth with his footprints and decorated Rādhā with flower ornaments (*bhūṣaṇaḥ*).

Thus, he fulfilled the desires of the earth and Rādhā (*bhūtiḥ*). The word is formed according to the rule ktic ktau ca saṁjñāyām (Pāṇini 3.3.274).

He removed the lamentation of the gopīs when he promised to return after Akrūra took him to Mathurā (*aśokaḥ*).

He destroyed his parent’s lamentation (Vasudeva and Devakī) by the message of his coming there (*śoka-nāśana*).

### Verse 81

*arciṣmān arcitaḥ kuṁbho viśuddhātmā viśodhanaḥ \|*  
*aniruddho prati-rathaḥ pradyumno mita-vikramaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**633. Arciṣmān: he flamed with anger at Kaṁsa.**  
**634. Arcitaḥ: he was worshiped by the garland maker and Kubjā.**  
**635. Kumbhaḥ: he was desired by Kubjā.**  
**636. Viśuddhātmā: he did not trick her.**  
**637. ViśMdhanaḥ: he purified the elephant and its trainer by giving them liberation.**  
**638. Aniruddhaḥ: he could not be obstructed from entering the arena.**  
**639. Aprati-rathaḥ: he had no real opponent.**  
**640. Pradyumnaḥ: he had unlimited strength.**  
**641. Amita-vikramaḥ: he destroyed opponents of great prowess with ease.**

**Комментарий:**

He flamed with anger because of Kaṁsa’s disrespect for his parents (*arciṣmān*). *Arcis* means flaming or bright according to Nānārtha-varga.

He was welcomed by the garland maker and Kubjā (*arcitaḥ*).

He was desired by Kubjā, who was attracted to his form and qualities (*kumbhaḥ*).

Kubjā-kṛṣṇāmbara-dhara is one of the hundred and eight names. Or according to others, *kumbha* means he was full of qualities and youth.

He did not trick her, when he promised to come to Kubjā’s house in front of his friends (*viśuddhātmā*).

When he killed the elephant trainer and the elephant, he gave them liberation (*viśodhanaḥ*).

He could not be obstructed by the elephant at the gate to the arena (*aniruddhaḥ*).

He had no opposition (*prati-rathaḥ*).

This was because he had unlimited strength (*pradyumnaḥ*).

He destroyed the very strong opponents like Cānūra with ease (*amita-vikramaḥ*).

### Verse 82

*kālanemi-nihā vīraḥ śauriḥ śūra-janeśvaraḥ \|*  
*trilokātmā trilokeśaḥ keśavaḥ keśi-hā hariḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**642. Kālanemi-nihā: he killed Kaṁsa who was previously Kālanemi.**  
**643. Viraḥ: he dragged the body around.**  
**644. Śauriḥ: he was the son of Vasudeva.**  
**645. Śūra-janeśvaraḥ: he was accepted by the Śuras as their master.**  
**646. TrilMkātmā: he was adored by the three worlds.**  
**647. TrilMkeśaḥ: he minimized the karmas of the three worlds’ inhabitants.**  
**648. Keśavaḥ: his rays illuminated the planets.**  
**649. Keśi-hā: he killed Keśī. 650. Hariḥ: he was attractive to all.**

**Комментарий:**

He killed Kaṁsa who was previously Kālanemi (*kālameni-nihā*). The prefix *ni* indicates that Kṛṣṇa grabbed him by the hair and killed him along with his younger associates.

He moved Kaṁsa in a special way (*vi + īrayati*): he dragged him (*vīraḥ*).

*harir yathebhaṁ vicakarṣa so 'vyayaḥ*

**The Lord dragged him about as a lion drags an elephant. SB** [**10.44.38**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/44.38)

*Kaṁsāri* is mentioned in the hundred and eight names.

He did this because he was the son of Vasudeva (*śauriḥ*): he acted in this way because Kaṁsa had committed violence against his father.

Killing powerful Kaṁsa, he immediately made himself available to the members of the Śura dynasty (*śura-janeśvaraḥ*). The Śuras approved of him because of his power.

He became the object of prema for the three worlds because of helping them, by killing Kaṁsa (*trilokātmā*).

*hate kaṁse trayo lokāḥ prāpnuvan nirvṛttiṁ parām*

**When Kaṁsa was killed the three worlds became greatly blissful.**

He limited the karmas of inhabitants of the three worlds (*tri-lokeśaḥ*).

He possessed the combined rays of the sun and other luminous objects (*keśavaḥ*).

*sūryasya tapaso lokān agneḥ somasya cāpy uta*  
*aṁśavo ye prakāśante mama te keśa-saṁjñitāḥ \|*  
*sarvajñāḥ keśavaṁ tasmān mām āhur muni-sattama \|\|*

**My rays which light up the planets of the sun, fire and moon, are called** ***keśa.*** **The wise call me Keśava for this reason, O best of sages! Mahābhārata 12.328.43**

Or he is called Keśava because of his beautiful, black locks. The word is formed according to the rule keśād vo’nyatarasyām (Pāṇini 5.2.109).

He killed the demon called Keśi who had the form of a horse (*keśi-hā*).

On killing Keśī he appeared attractive to the devatās and cowherds (*hariḥ*).

The word is formed according to the rule ac ī (Uṇādi sūtra 581).

### Verse 83

*kāma-devaḥ kāma-pālaḥ kāmī kāntaḥ kṛtāgamaḥ \|*  
*anirdeśya-vapur viṣṇur vīro ‘nanto dhanañjayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**651. Kāma-devaḥ: he rejoiced greatly on killing Keśī.**  
**652. Kāma-pālaḥ: he fulfilled the desires of everyone by that killing.**  
**653. Kāmī: he desired to kill Kaṁsa.**  
**654. Kāntaḥ: he bound up the devatās with happiness.**  
**655. Kṛtāgamaḥ: having killed the demon, he came to his friends.**  
**656. Anirdeśya-vapuḥ: his body as parabrahman was difficult to undertand.**  
**657. Viṣṇuḥ: he pervaded the sky and earth.**  
**658. Vīraḥ: the words of Vedānta describe this aspect.**  
**659. Anantaḥ: he was not close to impure persons. etc.**  
**660. Dhananjayaḥ: he conquered Kaṁsa’s wealth and gave it to Ugrasena.**

### Verse 84

*brahmaṇyo brahma-kṛd brahmā brahma brahma-vivardhanaḥ \|*  
*brahma-vid brāhmaṇo brahmī brahma-jño brāhmaṇa-priyaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**661. Brahmaṇyaḥ: he served Garga in Mathurā.**  
**662. Brahma-kṛt: he performed austerities as a brahmacārī.**  
**663. Brahmā: he became Brahmā.**  
**664. Brahma: he is the supreme brahman.**  
**665. Brahma-vivardhanaḥ: he nourished the brāhmaṇas and their austerity.**  
**666. Brahma-vid: he learned the gāyatrī mantra.**  
**667. Brāhmaṇaḥ: he became brāhmaṇas to teach the Vedas.**  
**668. Brahmī: he was the regulator of all tattvas.**  
**669. Brahma-jñaḥ: he learned the Vedas.**  
**670. Brāhmaṇa-priyaḥ: he pleased Sāndīpani.**

**Комментарий:**

He gave benefit to the brāhmaṇa Garga ( brāhmaṇyaḥ ). The word is formed by the rule ugavādibhyo yat (Pāṇini 5.1.2)

He took Vedic initiation from Garga and served him (in Mathurā).

He performed austerities as a brahmacārī (*brahma-kṛt*). Brahma means brahman, austerities, the Vedas, Lord Brahmā and a brāhmaṇa according to Nānārtha-varga.

He also personally becomes Brahmā, the source of brāhmaṇas and their activities (*brahmā*).

*bhavet kvacin mahā-kalpe brahma jīvo py upāsanaiḥ*  
*kvacid atra mahāviṣṇur brahmatvaṁ pratipadyate \|\|*

**Sometimes a** ***jīva*** **becomes Brahmā in a** ***mahā-kalpa*** **by intense worship. Sometimes Garbhodakaśāyī becomes Brahmā in that planet. Padma Purāṇa**

Because he increases the puruṣas—in total and as individuals, and because the powers of his unlimited form and qualities expand, he is called brahman. This is the definition according to Nirukti. He is the final shelter of all existing things, the supreme entity to be known through Vedānta. Śiva says:

*bṛhatvād bṛṁhanatvāc ca tasmād brahmeti-śabditaḥ*

**He is called Brahman because he is huge and causes increase. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

He nourished the brāhmaṇas and their austerities (*brahma-vivardhaṇaḥ*).

He learned the gāyatrī mantra from Garga (*brahma-vit*).

*gargād yadu-kulācāryād gāyatrīṁ vratam āsthitau*

**Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma accepted gāyatrī from Gargamuni, the ācārya of the Yadu dynasty. SB** [**10.45.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/45.29)

He also appeared in the form of Dattatreya and others in gotras which taught the meaning of the Vedas *(bṛahmaṇaḥ*).

He eternally controls pradhāna and other tattvas *(brahmī*).

Going to Avantī he learned the Vedas in terms of words and meaning from Sāndīpani (*brahma-jñaḥ*).

He pleased the brāhmaṇa Sāndīpani, his guru, by delivering to him his dead son (*brāhmaṇa-priyaḥ*). These names describe Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma desiring to live in a gurukūla and going there.

### Verse 85

*mahā-kramo mahā-karmā mahā-tejā mahoragaḥ \|*  
*mahā-kratur mahā-yajvā mahā-yajño mahā-haviḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**671. Mahā-kramaḥ: he took great strides to escape from Kālayavana.**  
**672. Mahā-karmā: his activities were worthy of worship.**  
**673. Mahā-tejāḥ: he had great powers.**  
**674. Mahoragaḥ: he went with great affection to Mucukunda.**  
**675. Mahā-kratuḥ: sacrifice or worship directed to him is the greatest.**  
**676. Mahā-yajvā: he has the greatest worshippers.**  
**677. Mahā-yajñaḥ: japa of his name is the greatest sacrifice.**  
**678. Mahā-haviḥ: he has the greatest oblations or offerings.**

**Комментарий:**

Returning from Avantī to Mathurā and desiring to kill Kālayavana he took giant strides and entered Mucukunda’s cave (*mahā-kramaḥ*).

His activites are worthy of worship or praise (*mahā-karmā*).

He manifested intolerable influence or radiance (*mahā-tejā*).He thought of some arrangement for burning up Kālayavana so the Yadus could not blame him. Mucukunda says:

*kiṁsvit tejasvināṁ tejo bhagavān vā vibhāvasuḥ*

**Perhaps you are the potency of all potent beings. Or maybe you are the powerful god of fire. SB** [**10.51.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/51.28)

With a generous heart (*mahā urus=mahora*) he went to his devotee Mucukunda (*mahora-gaḥ*). The hundred and eight names describe Kṛṣṇa as *mucukunda-prasāda-kṛt*. Here the word *uras* (chest) means heart.

Without regard for caste, with no pretenses, he showed affection for his devotee. Similarly, as Rāmacandra he embraced Guha who belonged the Niṣāda caste and performed the funeral ties for the vulture Jaṭāyu. In Śabarī’s house he affectionately ate the fruit she offered. Sincere affection for the devotee is described in four names.

He accepts an offering of tulasī leaves, which is greater than other sacrifices, because it is easy to perform and gives eternal results (*mahā-kratuḥ*).

He has worshippers who are greater than other types of worshippers because they offering tulasī (*mahā-yajvā).*

He has the great sacrifice called japa (*mahā-yajñaḥ*). It is greater than other sacrifices because of the absence of violence etc.

Oblations to him are greater than other oblations (*havi*) because they are very pure--with oblations in the form of offering respects and study of scriptures (*mahā-haviḥ*).

*tulasī-dala-mātreṇa jalasya culukena vā*  
*vikrīṇite svam ātmānaṁ bhaktebhyo bhakta-vatsalaḥ*

**The Lord who is affectionate to the devotees sells himself to them just by their offering tulasī with a few drops of water. Viṣṇu-dharma**

*yāḥ kriyāḥ saṁprayuktāḥ syur ekānta-gata-buddhibhiḥ*  
*tāḥ sarvāḥ śirasā devaḥ pratigṛhṇāti vai svayam*  
*japyenaiva ca saṁśuddhyed brāhmaṇo nātra saṁśayaḥ*  
*kuryād anyān na vā kuryān maitro brāhmaṇa ucyate*

**The Lord accepts on his head all those actions offered by persons with unalloyed devotional minds. There is no doubt that the brāhmaṇ is purified by japa alone. Whether he does other actions or not, he is called a friend and brāhmaṇa. Mokṣa-dharma**

*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya mukta-saṅgaḥ paraṁ vrajet*

**The person free of attachment attains the Lord by chanting Kṛṣṇa’s name. SB** [**12.3.51**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/3.51)

*kṛṣṇa-praṇāmī na punar-bhavāya*

**The person offering** ***praṇāmas*** **to Kṛṣṇa does not take birth again. Nāradīya Purāṇa**

*yad etat sukṛtaṁ havyaṁ tena tuṣyanti devatāḥ*  
*namaskāreṇa haviṣā svādhyair api*

**The devatās are satisfied with oblations well executed. The Lord is satisfied with offering of respects, oblations and study. Mahābhārata**

Oblations refers to rice and barley offerings. Because the Lord’s worship is accomplished easily by using body mind and words, he is described in this way.

### Verse 86

*stavyaḥ stava-priyaḥ stotraṁ stutiḥ stotā raṇa-priyaḥ \|*  
*pūrṇaḥ pūrayitā puṇyaḥ puṇyakīrtir anāmayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**679. Stavyaḥ: he is to be praised.**  
**680. Stava-priyaḥ: he is pleased with chanting of his name.**  
**681. Stotraṁ: he is the cause of words of praise.**  
**682. Stutiḥ: he was praised by Dhruva.**  
**683. StMtā: he likes to praise the devotees.**  
**684. Raṇa-priyaḥ: he is fond of fighting demons.**  
**685. Pūrṇaḥ: he is full of all powers.**  
**686. Pūrayitā: he kills the demons through his devotees.**  
**687. Puṇyaḥ: he purifies the demons he kills.**  
**688. Puṇya-kīrtiḥ: his fame is purifying. 689. Anāmayaḥ: his fame cures the disease of material life.**

**Комментарий:**

He is worshipped thoroughly through employment of words. He is to be praised because of his eternal, unlimited, auspicious qualities (*stavyaḥ*).

He is pleased whem people chant his name (*stava-priyaḥ*).

He is the cause of words which praise him (*stotram*). This is shown clearly in the story of Dhruva. Dhruva praised the Lord when the Lord touched him with his conch.

He is praised (*stutiḥ*). The ending denotes that he is the cause of praise.

He likes to praise the devotee (*stotā*).

*vandito hi sa vandeta mānito mānayeta ca*  
*arcitaś cārayen nityaṁ pūjitaḥ, pratipūjayet*

**Though offered prayers, the Lord praises the devotee. Though respected, he offers them respects. Though worshipped he offers worship. Though adored he offers adoration. Mahābhārata 13.134.57**

He likes battles in which the demons are killed (*raṇa-priyaḥ*). He killed wicked demons and persons pretending to be kings. In the hundred and eight names he is called *duryodhana-kulāntaka* and *dānavendra-vināśanaḥ*.

How could he kill unlimited wicked persons? He was full of unlimited powers (*pūrṇaḥ*).

He did not kill all of them by himself, but through his associates to whom he gave his powers, he killed them (pūrayitā). He benefitted Bhīma, Arjuna and others by giving them his strength.

Killing them, he purified them (*puṇyaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *puñoyana (*Uṇādi sutra 696).

Like his form, his fame purified people (*puṇya-kīrtiḥ*).

His fame, like medicine, counteracted the sickness of saṁsāra (*anāmayaḥ*).

### Verse 87

*mano-javas tīrthakaro vasu-retā vasu-pradaḥ \|*  
*vasu-prado vāsudevo vasur vasumanā haviḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**690. Mano-javaḥ: he is quick to remove the suffering of saṁsāra.**  
**691. Tīrtha-karaḥ: he manifests scriptures to attain him.**  
**692. Vasu-retāḥ: he places jīvas in pradhāna.**  
**693. Vasu-pradaḥ: he protects the jīvas at the time of destruction.**  
**694. Vasu-pradaḥ: he gives wealth to the devotees.**  
**695. Vāsudevaḥ: he is expressed by words.**  
**696. Vasuḥ: he lives eternally in his eternal abode.**  
**697. Vasumanā: he remembered Bhīṣma.**  
**698. Haviḥ: he was called by Bhīṣma and was accepted as his object of vision.**

**Комментарий:**

Quickly he removes the disease of material life (*manojavaḥ*). *Kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya*: just by chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa one attains the Lord. (SB [12.3.51](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/3.51))

He manifests scriptures such as the Gītā by which one can attain him (*tīrtha-karaḥ*). *Tīrtha* means scripture and crossing over according to Śrīdhara. He will explain yoga, jñāna, saṅkhya and other methods. Some say it means he displays pastimes to allow one to enter into understanding him deeply.

He has small conscious particles which exist within pradhāna (*vasu-retā*). *Vasu* refers to pradhāna and other elements which remain (*vasati*) during the secondary creation. *Mama yonir mahad brahma*: material energy is the cause. (BG [14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.3))

When destruction takes place, he protects the jīvas thoroughly (*vasu-pradaḥ*). *Da* means to protect.

He rewards the devotees with gifts of wealth (*vasu-pradaḥ*). The name is repeated (with different meanings).

He dwells within words, as that which should be known (*vāsudevaḥ*). Śiva says:

*vasanād eva vāde ca vāsudeveti-śabditaḥ*

**The word** ***vāsudeva*** **means “he dwells in words.” Hari-vaṁśa**

He lives eternally in his eternal abode (*vasuḥ*).

He remembered Bhīṣma (*vasu-manāḥ*).

*śara-talpa-gato bhīṣmaḥ śāmyan iva hutāśanaḥ*  
*māṁ dhyāyati puruṣa-vyāghra tato me tad-gataṁ manaḥ*

**Bhīṣma, tiger among men, lying on a bed of arrows, like fire that had been pacified, meditated on me. Thus I meditate on him. Mahābhārata 13.153.5**

When Bhīṣma was about to leave his body, Kṛṣṇa was accepted (*hūyate*) by Bhīṣma as the object of his vision (*haviḥ*). *Hū* can mean to accept.

It also means to please with pleasing words. Thus Kṛṣṇa was pleased with Bhīṣma. *Bhīṣma-mukti-pradāyakaḥ* is one of the hundred and eight names. When Bhīṣma was liberated, another jīva was appointed in his position.

### Verse 88

*sad-gatiḥ sat-kṛtiḥ sattā sad-bhūtiḥ sat-parāyaṇaḥ \|*  
*śūraseno yadu-śreṣṭhaḥ san-nivāsaḥ suyāmunaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**699. Sad-gatiḥ: he was the goal of the devotees.**  
**700. Sat-kṛtiḥ: he performed glorious actions.**  
**701. Sattā: he was inclined to go to the Pāṇḍavas.**  
**702. Sad-bhūtiḥ: he acted as a servant of the devotees.**  
**703. Sat-parāyaṇaḥ: he was the eternal refuge.**  
**704. Śūra-senaḥ: he had courageous troops.**  
**705. Yadu-śreṣṭhaḥ: he was the king of the Yadus.**  
**706. Sannivāsaḥ: he possessed beautiful Dvārakā.**  
**707. Suyāmunaḥ: he possessed beautiful things near the Yamunā**

**Комментарий:**

He was the goal of devotees like Bhīṣma (*sad-gatiḥ*).

His actions were glorious (*sat-kṛtiḥ*). He acts as a companion at the devotee’s passing. Varāha says:

*yadi vātādi-dośeṇa mad-bhakto māṁ na ca smaret*  
*ahaṁ smarāmi mad-bhaktaṁ nayāmi paramāṁ gatim*

**If the devotee does not remember because of sickness, I remember my devotee and lead him to the supreme goal. Varāha Purāṇa**

He was inclined to go (*sattum*) to his devotees the Pāṇḍavas (*sattā*). The citizens of Dvārakā say:

*yarhy ambujakṣāpasasāra bho bhavān*  
*kurūn madhūn vātha suhṛd-didṛkṣayā*  
*tatrābda-koṭi-pratimaḥ kṣaṇo bhaved*  
*raviṁ vinākṣṇor iva nas tavācyuta \|\|*

**O lotus-eyed Lord! When you go to Hastināpura or Vraja to see your friends, one moment becomes like a trillion years for us, who belong to you, and who become like eyes without the sun. SB** [**1.11.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.9)

He became the messenger and doorkeeper for devotees (*sat*) like Yudhiṣṭhira, Ugrasena and Bali (*sad-bhūtiḥ*).

The supreme shelter exists eternally in his possession (*sat-parāyaṇaḥ*).

*sādhavo hṛdayaṁ mahyaṁ sādhūnāṁ hṛdayaṁ tv aham*  
*mad-anyat te na jānanti nāhaṁ tebhyo manāg api*

**The pure devotee is always within the core of my heart, and I am always in the heart of the pure devotee. My devotees do not know anything else but me and I do not know anything else but them. SB** [**9.4.68**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.68)

He possessed courageous troops (*śūra-senaḥ*).

He was respected as the king of the Yadus (*yadu-śreṣṭḥaḥ*). The two names t*ādavendra* and *yadudvaha* are found in the hundred and eight names.

He was in charge of very attractive Dvārakā (*san-nivāsaḥ*).

He is in possession of beautiful things near the Yamunā such as Bṛhad-vana (*suyāmunaḥ*).

The name is formed according to the rule adurabhavas ca (Pāṇini 4.2.70).

### Verse 89

*bhūtāvāso vāsudevaḥ sarvāsu nilayo ‘nalaḥ \|*  
*darpahā darpado ‘dṛpto durdharo ‘thāparājitaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**708. Bhūtāvāsaḥ: he was the residence of all beings.**  
**709. Vāsu-devaḥ: he inundated the Pāṇdavas with prema and played with them.**  
**710. Sarvāsu nilayaḥ: he was the shelter of the Pāṇḍavas and all beings.**  
**711. Analaḥ: he thought he could not do enough for his devotees.**  
**712. Darpa-hā: he destroyed the pride of Duryodhana.**  
**713. Darpa-daḥ: he gave the throne to Yudhiṣṭhira.**  
**714. Adṛptaḥ: he was not proud of his accomplishments.**  
**715. Durdharaḥ: he could not be imprisoned by the Kauravas.**  
**716. Aparājitaḥ: he remained undefeated.**

**Комментарий:**

All beings live in him, facing him (*bhūtāvasaḥ*). Śiva says:

*vasanti tvayi bhūtāni bhūtāvāsas tato bhavān*

**All beings live in you. Thus you are known as bhūta-vāsaḥ. Hari-vaṁśa**

He covers (*vaste*) the Pāṇḍavas and Yadus with prema and plays with them (*devaḥ*). This is a karmadhāraya compound.

He was thus the shelter of all of them (*sarvāsu nilayaḥ*).

He thinks he has not done enough for his devotee or is not inclined to forgive those who offend devotees (*an-alam*). Or he is the fire (*analam*) that destroys the oppressors of the devotees. Or he does not prevent (*al*—to prevent) persons from being qualified as devotees.

He destroyed the pride of Duryodhana and others (*darpa-hā*) and accomplished the rājasūya sacrifice of Yudhiṣṭhira (*darpa-daḥ*).

He was without pride in accompḷishing these feats (*adṛptaḥ*). Though he killed Jarāsandha and freed the imprisoned kings, he was not proud.

Acting as the messenger of the Pāṇḍdavas, he could not be caught or imprisoned by Duryodhana, his wicked ministers or his army of four parts (*durdharaḥ*).

*imaṁ hi pundarīkākṣaṁ jighṛkṣanty alpa-medhasaḥ*  
*paṭhenāgni-prajvalantaṁ yathā bālā yathā jaḍāḥ*

**On reading the letter, like children or fools the unintelligent Kauravas wanted to imprison the Lord, who burned like fire. Mahābhārata**

*Atha* indicates auspiciousness. They could not capture Kṛṣṇa or defeat him (*aparājitaḥ*).

### Verse 90

*viśva-mūrtir mahā-mūrtir dīpta-mūrtir amūrtimān \|*  
*aneka-mūrtir avyaktaḥ śata-mūrtiḥ śatānanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**717. Viśva-mūrtiḥ: he had all forms within himself.**  
**718. Mahā-mūrtiḥ: he displayed a huge form.**  
**719. Dīpta-mūrtiḥ: that form shone like the sun.**  
**720. Amūrtimān: he was composed of all the elements which have no form.**  
**721. Anekamūrtiḥ: he became many forms in Dvārakā.**  
**722. Avyaktaḥ: he could not be known even by Nārada.**  
**723. Śata-mūrtiḥ: he displayed many forms.**  
**724. Śatānanaḥ: he had hundreds of faces.**

**Комментарий:**

Why was he not defeated by so many persons? The next names explain this. He was all bodies such as devatās, Gandharvas and humans (*viśva-mūrtiḥ*).

Thus he has a huge form (*mahā-mūrtiḥ*). He could thus not be captured or defeated by the four parts of the army.

This form had the brilliance of many suns (*dīpta-mūrtiḥ*).

Not only was he the form of the universe and everything in it, but he was also without form (*amūrtimān*). All the material causes such as pradhāna and mahat-tattva which do not have particular forms (in the beginning) exist eternally as his body. *Yasyāvyaktaṁ śarīraṁ:* his body is not manifest. (Subala Upaniṣad) Because form is denied for subtle elements, he is called “without form.”

He was simultaneously present in 16,108 houses in Dvārakā (*aneka-mūrtiḥ*). He is called *ṣoḍaśa-śtrī-sahasreśaḥ* in the hundred and eight names.

How could he exist in all the houses? Even Nārada could not understand him in this aspect (*avyaktaḥ*).

*citraṁ bataitad ekena vapuṣā yugapat pṛthak*  
*gṛheṣu dvy-aṣṭa-sāhasraṁ striya eka udāvahat*

**It is astounding that single Kṛṣṇa expanded himself in sixteen thousand similar forms to marry sixteen thousand queens in their respective homes. SB** [**10.69.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/69.2)

Thus he has infinite forms (*śata-mūrtiḥ*) and infinite faces (*śatānanaḥ*).

### Verse 91

*eko naikaḥ savaḥ kaḥ kiṁ yat tat padam anuttamam \|*  
*loka-bandhur loka-nātho mādhavo bhakta-vatsalaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**725. Ekaḥ: he is one.**  
**726. Naikaḥ: he is simultaneously many.**  
**727. Savaḥ: he is one and many by his inconceivable śakti.**  
**728. Kaḥ: he goes to the devotee.**  
**729. Kim: he is the object of inquiry.**  
**730. Yat: he endeavors for the devotees’ affection.**  
**731. Tat: he increases the affection of those who approach him.**  
**732. Padam anuttamam: he is their highest shelter.**  
**733. Loka-bandhuḥ: he binds the devotee with prema.**  
**734. Loka-nāthah: he is their master.**  
**735. Mādhavaḥ: he destroys other types of knowledge.**  
**736. Bhakta-vatsalaḥ: he is affectionate to the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

Though he is many forms he is still one (*ekaḥ*). The root is *i*, to go, with the suffix *ka*.

But he is also many forms in reality (*naikaḥ*). The word *na* used in the compound maintains its form.

He must then be the shelter of duality. These forms cast aside (*syanti*) mistakes concerning duality and non-duality (*sā*). The formation is explained by the rule *anyeṣv api ḍṛśyate* (Pāṇini 3.2.101). At one time, by the acintya-śakti, he becomes many forms. This is the conclusion. He dwells (*vasati*) in devotees, not in the shelters (proponents) of dvaita and advaita (*savaḥ*).

The same rule applies here (Pāṇini 3.2.101).

He shines (*kanati*) in them or he goes to them, not the proponents of dvaita and advaita (*kaḥ*). *Ka* means to go or to shine.

The same rule applies here (Pāṇini 3.2.101)

He is to be inquired about by them (*kim*). This form indicates a question.

So ‘nveṣṭavyaḥ sa vijijñāsitavaḥ: he should be the object of inquiry. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [8.1.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/8.1.5))

He endeavors for their friendship (*yat -- yatate*).

He increases (*tanoti*) the affection of those who approach him (*tat*). The nasal is dropped.

He is their supreme shelter (*anuttamam padam*).

He binds the devotees with the ropes of prema (*loka-bandhuḥ*). The root is *bandh*--to tie. This is according to Uṇādi-sūtra 10 starting *śṛṛ-svṛ-snihi.*

The cause, the extraordinary relationship, is described. He is the master of these devotees (*loka-nathaḥ*). Opponents say that Śiva and others are masters of the peopḷe. Why do the devotees accept Kṛṣṇa as the lord of all people? He defeats (*dhunoti*) their knowledge (*mā*) by the Gītā and other scriptures about himself (*mādhavaḥ*). Because he is worshipped by Śiva and others as the controller of the worlds he is called *mahā-deva*. *Sarva-medhe mahatma*: Śiva offered sacrifice (to Viṣṇu) to become powerful. (Mahābhārta)

This is the Lord’s natural position.

*tam īśvarāṇāṁ paramāṁ maheśvaraṁ*  
*tam devatānāṁ paramaṁ ca daivatam*

**He is the controller, the highest the great lord, supreme among devas. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

He has this special relationship with devotees because he is affectionate to the devotees--he desires them at all times (*bhakta-vatsalaḥ*). If Śiva or others become angry with his devotees, he makes them fall down.

Vatsala is formed according to the rule vatsāṁsabhyām kāmavale (Pāṇini 5.2.98).

### Verse 92

*suvarṇa-varṇo hemāṅgo varāṅgaś candanāṅgadī \|*  
*vīrahā viṣamaḥ śūnyo ghṛtāśīr acalaś calaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**737. Suvarṇa-varṇaḥ: he had a complexion like gold.**  
**738. Hemāṅgaḥ: his residences were attractive like gold.**  
**739. Varāṅgaḥ: his limbs awere beautiful.**  
**740. Candanāṅgadī: he wore armlets which pleased the devotees.**  
**741. Vīra-hā: he destroyed the devotees’ obstacles.**  
**743. Viṣamaḥ: he had no equal.**  
**743. Śūnyaḥ: he destroyed the demons’ happiness.**  
**744. Ghṛtāśīḥ: he made the desires of the devotee come true.**  
**745. Acalaḥ: he was fixed in protecting the Pāṇḍavas.**  
**746. Calaḥ: he broke his promise and took up weapons.**

**Комментарий:**

He had a form like gold (*suvarṇa-varṇaḥ*).

*yadā paśya paśyate rukma-varṇaṁ*  
*kartāram īśam puruśaṁ brahma-yonim*  
*… nirañjanaḥ paramaṁ sāmyam upaiti*

**When the seer sees the golden person, the Lord, the creator, he, being pure, attains a form similar to the supreme. Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad** [**3.1.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/3.1.3)

He had places desirable as gold (*hemāṅgaḥ*).

He had beautiful limbs (*varāṅgaḥ*).

He had armlets which please the devotees’ hearts (*candanāṅgad*ī). Some say that the above four names indicate Kṛṣṇa Caitanya.

These qualities mentioned destroyed the heroes (*vīra*)--lust and other obstacles—for the devotees (*vīra-hā*).

He was distinguished from all others (*viṣamaḥ*).

*na tat samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate*

**The Lord has no equal or superior. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

Or the Lord is partial to the devotee (*viṣamaḥ*).

He decreased (*ūnayate*) the fortune (*śam*) of the demons (*śūnyaḥ*).

*śam ūnaṁ kurute viṣṇur adṛśyaḥ san paraḥ svayam*  
*tasmāc chūnya iti proktas todanāt tuccha ucyate*

**Unseen Viṣṇu, the supreme, decreases the fortune of the demons. Therefore he is called** ***śūnya*****. By that punishment, they become insignificant.**

Some say that the word means that he is free of faults (*śūnyaḥ*).

He illuminated the desires of the devotees (*ghṛtāśiḥ*). He made their desires come true. *Ghṛ* means to light up.

He was fixed in protecting the Pāṇḍavas (*acalaḥ*).

The devotee Bhīṣma promised to make Kṛṣṇa take up a weapon. Kṛṣṇa, breaking (*calati*) his vow not to take a weapon, made Bhīṣma’s promise come true and took up the chariot wheel (*calaḥ*).

### Verse 93

*amānī māna-do mānyo loka-svāmī tri-loka-dhṛk \|*  
*sumedhā medha-jo dhanyaḥ satya-medhā dharādharaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**747. Amānī: he is not proud.**  
**748. Māna-daḥ: he respects the devotees.**  
**749. Mānyaḥ: he is respected for destroying the enemies.**  
**750. Loka-svāmī: he is the master of all people.**  
**751. Triloka-dhṛk: he travels throughout the three worlds.**  
**752. Sumedhāḥ: he has glorious intelligence.**  
**753. Medha-jaḥ: he manifests intelligence when he is among the devotees.**  
**754. Dhanyaḥ: he has a wealth of devotees.**  
**755. Satya-medhāḥ: his identity as a cowherd is true.**  
**756. Dharā-dharaḥ: he lifted Govardhana.**

**Комментарий:**

He remains without pride in performing actions for his devotees (*amānī*). Though he is the supreme Lord, he becomes a door keeper, messenger or chariot driver.

*tat tasya kaiṅkaryam alaṁ bhṛtān no*  
*viglāpayaty aṅga yad ugrasenam*  
*tiṣṭhan niṣaṇṇaṁ parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇye*  
*nyabodhayad deva nidhārayeti*

**O Vidura! Kṛṣṇa’s acting as a servant of Ugrasena gives us servants great pain. Standing in from of Ugrasena sitting on the king’s throne he informed him, “O king of kings! Please establish me in your service.” SB** [**3.2.22**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.22)

*sārathya-pāraṣada-sevana-sakhya-dautya-*  
*vīrāsanānugamana-stavana-praṇāmān*  
*snigdheṣu pāṇḍuṣu jagat-praṇatiṁ ca viṣṇor*  
*bhaktiṁ karoti nṛ-patiś caraṇāravinde*

**Hearing through their praises that Kṛṣṇa acted as charioteer, follower, servant, friend and messenger of the dear Pāṇḍavas and that Viṣṇu was respected by the whole world, Parīkṣit expressed devotion to the lotus feet of the Lord. SB** [**1.16.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/16.16)

*kṛṣṇaḥ pādāvanejane*

**Kṛṣṇa washed everyone’s feet. SB** [**10.75.5**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/75.5)

The Lord respects the devotees (*māna-daḥ*).

He is respected because he crushes the enemies or is respected by the devotees when he acts a doorkeeper etc. (*mānyaḥ*).

Why does he do this? He supports all people (*loka-svāmī*).

He goes (*dharjati*) throughout the three worlds as the maintainer and protector (*triloka-dhṛj*). This is his natural quality. This is shown by his relationship with the devotees through kaimutya.

Though he is supreme, because of his quality of being controlled by the devotee, he has glorious intelligence (*sumedhā*). *Medh* means to meet.

His intelligence manifests when he associates with a devotee (*medha-jaḥ*).

He is fortunate in attaining the pure devotees as his wealth (*dhanyaḥ*).

Namo ‘kiñcana-vittāya: I offer respects to you who are the wealth of the impoverished. (SB [1.8.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.27))

His intelligence related to his identity among the cowherd devotees is real, not an imitation like an actor (*satya-medhā*).

*yadi vo 'sti mayi prītiḥ ślāghyo 'haṁ bhavatāṁ yadi*  
*tad ātma-bandhu-sadṛśī buddhir vaḥ kriyatāṁ mayi*  
*nāham evo na gandharvo na yakṣo na ca rākṣasaḥ*  
*ahaṁ vo bāndhavo jāto nātaś cintyam ato 'nyathā*

**If I am dear to you and to be praised by you, you should think of me as your friend. I am not a Gandharva or Yaksa or Rākṣāsa. I am born as your relative. Do not think otherwise. Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**5.13.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/5.13.12)

*tvaṁ no guruḥ pitṛvyaś ca ślāghyo bandhuś ca nityadā*  
*vayaṁ tu rakṣyāḥ poṣyāś ca anukampyāḥ prajā hi vaḥ*

**You are our guru, paternal uncle and praiseworthy friend, and we are like your sons, always dependent on your protection, sustenance and compassion. SB** [**10.48.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/48.29)

He performed actions while thinking himself a friend and relative.

He lifted Govardhana hill (*dharā-dharaḥ*).

### Verse 94

*tejo vṛṣo dyuti-dharaḥ sarva-śastra-bhṛtāṁ varaḥ \|*  
*pragraho nigraho vyagro naika-śṛṅgo gadāgrajaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**757. Tejo-vṛṣaḥ: he showered powers on his friends.**  
**758. Dyuti-dharaḥ: he possessed brilliant enthusiasm to protect them.**  
**759. Sarva-śastra-bhṛtāṁ varaḥ: he was the best among wielders of weapons.**  
**760. Pragrahaḥ: he was accepted by Arjuna as his charioteer.**  
**761. Nigrahaḥ: by his glance he destroyed the enemies.**  
**762. Vyagraḥ: he quickly vanquished them.**  
**763. Naika-śṛṅgaḥ: he used many means to accomplish this.**  
**764. Gadāgra-jaḥ: he had a younger brother Gada.**

**Комментарий:**

For protection of his friends he showered powers on Indra and others, which destroyed their suffering (*tejovṛṣaḥ*).

He possessed brilliance, characterized by enthusiasm to protect his friends (*dyuti-dharaḥ*).

He was the best among wielders of weapons such as Jarāsandha and Śālva (*sarva-śastra-bhrṭām varaḥ*). He entered among them and slew them.

He was accepted (pragrhyate) by Arjuna as his charioteer (pragṛhaḥ). This word is formed according to the rule graha-vṛ-ḍr- niścigamaś ca (Pāṇini 3.3.58).

Kṛṣṇa killed Arjuna’s enemies by his glance of time (*nigrahaḥ*).

Very quickly he destroyed Arjuna’s enemies (*vyagraḥ*). This is formed according to *ṛjendrāgravajra* (Uṇādi-sūtra 188).

He used many means to vanquish those many enemies (*anaika-śṛṅgaḥ*). These means are well known in the Mahābhārata. The root *śṛ* means to injure. This makes the word *śṛṅga.*

He appeared previous to his younger brother Gada (*gadāgra-jaḥ*).

### Verse 95

*catur-mūrtiś catur-bāhuś catur-vyūhaś catur-gatiḥ \|*  
*catur-ātmā catur-bhāvaś catur-vedavid ekapāt \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**765. Catur-mūrtiḥ: he had four forms as the sons of Dharma.**  
**766. Catur-bāhuḥ: he had four arms in the form of Bhīma, Arjuna, Sātyaki and Uddhava.**  
**767. Catur-vyūhaḥ: he is described in the four Vedas.**  
**768. Catur-gatiḥ: four types of devotees attain him.**  
**769. Catur-ātmā: he makes efforsts so that the devotees attain the four vargas.**  
**770. Catur-bhāvaḥ: he created the four vargas.**  
**771. Catur-veda-vid: he makes the four Vedas known to the devotees.**  
**772. Ekapāt: he possesses the material universes as one of his vibhūtis.**

**Комментарий:**

He has four forms as the sons of dharma: Nara, Nārāyaṇa, Hari and Kṛṣṇa (*catur-mūrtiḥ*).

He had four arms in the form of Bhīma, Arjuna, Sātyaki and Uddhava (*catur-bāhuḥ*).

He is arranged in the Vedas or understood (*vyūhyate*) to be the supreme Lord in the four Vedas (*catur-vyūhaḥ*).

The word is formed according to the rule halaś ca  (Pāṇini 3.3.121)

He is the shelter of the four types of devotees (artha, arthārthī, jijñasuḥ and jñānī) according to their bhāva (*catur-gatiḥ*). Or he has pastimes (*gatiḥ*) in his four abodes. Or he has a gait (*gatiḥ*) similar to the lion, tiger, elephant and bull.

He makes efforts for the four vargas (artha, dharma, kāma and mokṣa) to be obtained by bhakti (*catur-ātmā*).

He produces (*bhāvayati*) these four vargas for the worshipers (*catur-bhāvaḥ*).

He makes the four Vedas known to the devotees (*catur-veda-vit*).

He has one vibhūti which consists of the material world comprised of uncountable universes (*eka-pāt*).

Pādo ‘sya viśva-bhūtāni: the entities in the universe are one of his portions. (Puruṣa-sūkta)

*aṇḍānāṁ tu sahastrānāṁ sahastrāny ayutāni ca*  
*īḍṛśānāṁ tathā tatra koṭi koṭi śatāni ca*

**There are thousands upon thousands, millions, ten millions, and billions of universes. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

### Verse 96

*samāvarto nivṛttātmā durjayo dur-atikramaḥ \|*  
*durlabho durgamo durgo durāvāso durāri-hā \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**773. Samāvartaḥ: he repeatedly creates the universes.**  
**774. Nivṛtātmā: at the time of destruction he becomes disinterested in it.**  
**775. Durjayaḥ: he is conquered by samādhi, difficult to accomplish.**  
**776. Dur-atikramaḥ: he disregards painful punishments.**  
**777. Durlabhaḥ: he is attained by bhakti, hard to perfect.**  
**778. Durgamaḥ: he is understood by studying the Upaniṣads, though they are difficult to comprehend.**  
**779. Durgaḥ: he is sung in the sāmas, difficult to pronounce.**  
**780. Durāvāsaḥ: he lives in the heart by surrender, difficuilt to perfect.**  
**781. Durāri-hā: he destroys the obstacles for the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

Again and again he creates the universes (*samāvartaḥ*).

*anādir bhagavān kālo nānto’sya dvija vidyate \|*  
*avyucchinnās tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-saṁyamau \|\|*

**There is no end for the Lord and time which have no beginning. The two are continuous, and control creation, maintenance and destruction. Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**1.2.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.2.26)

At the time of destruction his mind is complete detached from the workings of the universe (*nivṛttātmā*).

*sva-sṛṣṭam idam āpīya śayānaṁ saha śaktibhiḥ*

**After Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa with drew the created universes along with the puruṣāvatāras, he remained inattentive to universal affairs as if sleeping. SB** [**10.87.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/87.12)

Some take the name as *anivṛtātmā*: he is involved with his devotees.

He is brought under control in the heart by samādhi, difficult to accomplish (*durjayaḥ*).

He does not care for punishment which gives pain (*dur-atikramaḥ*).

He is attained by bhakti, which is difficult to perfect (*durlabhaḥ*).

*janmāntara-sahasreṣu tapo-dāna-samādhibhiḥ*  
*narāṇāṁ kṣīna-pāpānāṁ kṛṣṇe bhaktiḥ prajāyate*

**Bhakti to Kṛṣṇa destroys the sins which are destroyed by austerity, charity and samādhi practiced for thousands of births. Yoga-vāśiṣṭha**

He is understood by studying the Upaniṣads which are difficult to comprehend (*durgamaḥ*).

He is glorified in the sāma verses which are difficult to comprehend (*durgaḥ*).

He lives in the heart by surrender, which is difficult to perfect (*durāvāsaḥ*). That type of worship is too difficult.

That is explained: he counteracts or destroys the evil enemies in the form of lust etc. (durāri-hā). The method is not too difficult because he assists the devotee.

### Verse 97

*śubhāṅgo loka-sāraṅgaḥ sutantus tantu-vardhanaḥ \|*  
*indra-karmā mahā-karmā kṛta-karmā kṛtāgamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**782. Śubhāṅgaḥ: he recognizes auspicious elements in bhakti.**  
**783. LMka-sāraṅgaḥ: he praises the qualities of his devotees.**  
**784. Sutantuḥ: he traps the devotees with his attractive qualities.**  
**785. Tantu-vardhanaḥ: he increases the net of attraction by appearing in various avatāras.**  
**786. Indra-karmā: he performed actions for Indra.**  
**787. Mahā-karmā: he performed great actions on his behalf.**  
**788. Kṛta-karmā: he performed other astounding pastimes.**  
**789. Kṛtāgamaḥ: after performing his pastimes, he returned to Dvārakā.**

**Комментарий:**

He recognizes auspicious elements such as words of the guru, faith, advice given in dreams etc. (*śubhāṅgaḥ*). Because of the Lord’s assistance the worship is not too difficult.

He glorifies the qualities of the devotees (*loka-sārāṅgaḥ*).

He has a splendid net of qualities and actions attractive to the devotee facing him (*su-tantuḥ*).

He increases those attractive features by appearing as attractive avatāras (*tantu-vardhaṇaḥ*).

He performed activities for Indra (*indra-karmā*). This is further described.

He performed great actions like killing Bhaumāsura, returning the jeweled mountain, the umbrella and Aditi’s earrings (*mahā-karmā*).

He performed actions like taking sixteen thousand princesses, taking the pārijāta tree and defeating all the devatās, or defeating Śiva, cutting off Bāṇāsura’s arms, and rescuing Aniruddha and Uṣā (*kṛta-karmā*).

Performing all these actions he returned to Dvārakā (*kṛtāgamaḥ*).

### Verse 98

*udbhavaḥ sundaraḥ sundo ratna-nābhaḥ sulMcanaḥ \|*  
*arko vājasanaḥ śṛṅgī jayantaḥ sarva-vijjayī \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**790. Udbhavaḥ: he attained fame there for his excellent actions.**  
**791. Sundaraḥ: he was attractive because all limbs were properly placed.**  
**792. Sundaḥ: he melted the devotees by his merciful glance.**  
**793. Ratna-nābhaḥ: he had a navel red like a ruby.**  
**794. SulMcanaḥ: he had beautiful eyes.**  
**795. Arkaḥ: he was praised.**  
**796. Vāja-sanaḥ: he gave food.**  
**797. Śṛṅgī: he blew his horn.**  
**798. Jayantaḥ: he defeated the boys in wrestling.**  
**799. Sarva-vij-jayī: he had excellence, by having learned sages as his company.**

**Комментарий:**

He attained fame because of his outstanding actions or he attained the parijāta and the princesses (*udbhavaḥ*). *Bhū* (*bhava*) indicates attaining.

His limbs were properly placed (*sundaraḥ*).

He nicely moistened or made soft ( su undati) those who had taken shelter of him because of his merciful glance (sundaḥ). It is formed according to the ruleprṣodarādīni (Pāṇini 6.3.109).

He had a navel which shone red like a ruby (*ratna-nābhaḥ*).

He had eyes which were beautiful because of their length and redness (*sulocanaḥ*).

He was praised (*arkaḥ*). *Ark* means to praise.

He supplied (*sanoti*) food (*vāja-sanaḥ*).

Annado vasu-dānaḥ: the Lord is the giver of food and treasure. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad)

As a cowherd, he made music using a buffalo horn (*śṛṅgī*).

*prabodhayan śṛṅga-ravena*

**He alerted the boys with the sound of the horn. SB** [**10.12.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/12.1)

He defeated the boys in wrestling or debating (*jayantaḥ*).

He had eternal excellence by having around him persons of knowledge (*sarva-vit*) like Vaśiṣṭḥa and Vāmadeva (*sarva-vij-jayī*).

### Verse 99

*suvarṇa-bindur akṣobhyaḥ sarva-vāg īśvareśvaraḥ \|*  
*mahā-hrado mahā-garto mahā-bhūto mahā-nidhiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**800. Suvarṇa-binduḥ: he had limbs as attractive as gold.**  
**801. Akṣobhyaḥ: he could not be agitated by false smiles.**  
**802. Sarva-vāg: he is indicated by all the Vedas.**  
**803. Iśvareśvaraḥ: he is the controller of Brahmā and Śiva.**  
**804. Mahā-hradaḥ: he destroys all suffering and gives relief like a lake.**  
**805. Mahā-gartaḥ: all the universes rest at one place on his body.**  
**806. Mahā-bhūtaḥ: he gives rise to the primary elements.**  
**807. Mahā-nidhiḥ: he gives the great treasure of himself to the devotee.**

**Комментарий:**

He has limbs attractive like gold (*suvarṇa-binduḥ*). *Bindu* means limb according to Medinī. Or he has a golden dot on his forehead.

He could not be agitated by false smiles of women devoid of prema (*akṣobhyaḥ*). Suta says:

*yasyendriyaṁ vimathituṁ kuhakair na śekuḥ*

**The queens could not agitate the senses of the Lord. SB** [**1.11.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.36)

He is indicated by all the words of the Vedas (*sarva-vāk).*

This is because he is the controller of Brahmā, Śiva and others (*īśvareśvaraḥ*).

*tam īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaram*

**He is the supreme lord of all controllers. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

*svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas tryadhīśaḥ*

**Kṛṣṇa is the lord of all kinds of threes and is independently supreme by achievement of all kinds of fortune. SB** [**3.2.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.21)

He destroys the suffering of person suffering in the material world and persons desiring liberation, like a huge lake (*mahā-hradaḥ*). He is called *saṁsāra-vairī* (enemy of saṁsāra) in the hundred and eight names.

Millions of univereses are placed at just one place on his body (*mahā-gartaḥ*).

*ihaikasthaṁ jagat kṛtsnaṁ paśyādya sa carācaram*  
*mama dehe guḍākeśa yac cānyad draṣṭum icchasi*

**Now, in this place, see in one part of my body this whole universe with its moving and non-moving entities, O conqueror of sleep, and see whatever else you desire to see. BG** [**11.7**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/11.7)

He gives rise to the five primary elements (*mahā-bhūtaḥ*).

He gives himself the greatest treasure (*mahā-nidhiḥ*) to the devotee

*visṛjati hṛdayaṁ na yasya sākṣād*  
*dharir avaśābhihito 'py aghaugha-nāśāḥ*  
*praṇaya-rasanayā dhṛtāṅghri-padmaḥ*  
*sa bhavati bhāgavata-pradhāna uktaḥ*

**He who binds the lotus feet of the Lord by ropes of love, and whose heart the Lord, destroyer of heaps of sin, does not leave, even if beckoned accidentally, is called the best of devotees. SB** [**11.2.55**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.55)

### Verse 100

*kumudaḥ kundaraḥ kundaḥ parjanyaḥ pāvano ‘nilaḥ \|*  
*amṛtāśo ‘mṛtavapuḥ sarvajñaḥ sarvato mukhaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**808. Kumudaḥ: he rejoices at any place in order to promote bhakti.**  
**809. Kundaraḥ: he gives treasures to those desiring them.**  
**810. Kundaḥ: he wears a kunda (jasmine) garland.**  
**811. Parjanyaḥ: he showers sweetness on the devotee.**  
**812. Pāvanaḥ: he purifies them of any accidental sin.**  
**813. Nilaḥ: he completely accepts the unalloyed devotees.**  
**814. Amṛtāśaḥ: he makes the devotee experience sweetness.**  
**815. Amṛta-vapuḥ: he has an eternal body.**  
**816. Sarva-jñaḥ: he knows the devotees’ situations.**  
**817. Sarvato mukhaḥ: he faces the devotees wherever they may be.**

**Комментарий:**

He rejoices (*mudah*) at a suitable place (*ku*) in order to promote bhakti (*kumudaḥ*). This is formed by the rule *igupadhāṭh kit* (Uṇādi sutra 526).

*visṛjati hṛdayaṁ na yasya sākṣād*  
*dharir avaśābhihito 'py aghaugha-nāśaḥ*  
*praṇaya-rasanayā dhṛtāṅghri-padmaḥ*  
*sa bhavati bhāgavata-pradhāna uktaḥ*

**He who binds the lotus feet of the Lord by ropes of love, and whose heart the Lord, destroyer of heaps of sin, does not leave, even if beckoned accidentally, is called the best of devotees. SB** [**11.2.55**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.55)

He gives (*rāti*) treasure (*kunda*) to those who desire it (*kundaraḥ*). This indicates treasures like mahāpadma, mahānidhi etc.

He has kunda flowers (*kundaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *arṣ ādibho ‘c* (Pāṇini 5.2.127).

*kunda-dāma-kṛta-kaustubha-veśḥ*

**He wore the Kaustubha with a garland of kunda flowers. SB** [**10.35.20**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.20)

Or he arranges (*dadāt*i) for the devotees a place (*kum*) in their hearts where they can understand the truth concerning him.

*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te*

**I give the intelligence by which they come to me. BG** [**10.10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.10)

In that place in the heart, he showers the sweetness of his form like a raincloud (*parjanyaḥ*). *Pṛṣ* means to sprinkle.

He purifies that place in the heart if by chance it becomes contaminated by sin (*pavanaḥ*).

*svapāda-mūlaṁ bhajataḥ priyasya*  
*tyaktānya-bhāvasya hariḥ pareśaḥ*  
*vikarma yac cotpatitaṁ kathañcit*  
*dhunoti sarvaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭaḥ*

**One who has thus given up all other engagements and has taken full shelter at the lotus feet of Supreme Lord is very dear to the Lord. If such a surrendered soul accidentally commits some sinful activity, the Supreme Lord, great because of his affection for the devotee, seated within everyone’s heart, immediately takes away the reaction to such sin. SB** [**11.5.42**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.42)

He completely (*nitaram*) accepts (*lāti*) the unalloyed devotees (*nilaḥ*). Or he gave to Ugrasena the land (*ila*) obtained (*nijita*) after killing Kaṁsa. *Ila* means earth or mountain according to Halāyudha. Or he gave up sleep because of his desire for Rukmiṇī. *Il* also means to sleep or to move.

*tathāham api tac-citto nidrāṁ ca na labhe niśi*

**Just as Rukmiṇī's mind is fixed on me, my mind is fixed on her. I can't even sleep at night. SB** [**10.53.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/53.2)

He makes the devotee enjoy (*āśayati*) liberation--a heart filled with sweetness (*amṛtāśaḥ*).

He has a body which is eternal (*amṛta-vapuḥ*).

He knows all the situations of the devotees (*sarva-jñaḥ*).

*darṣana-dhyāna-saṁsparśair matsya-kūrma-vihaṅgamāḥ*  
*svāny apatayāni puṣṇanti tathāham api padmaja*

**The fish nourishes its offspring by seeing, the turtle by meditation, and the birds by touch. I nourish my devotees with similar affection, O Brahmā. Padma Purāṇa**

*nirapekṣaṁ muniṁ śāntaṁ nirvairaṁ sama-darśanam*  
*anuvrajāmy ahaṁ nityaṁ pūyeyety aṅghri-reṇubhiḥ*

**I always follow the footsteps of my pure devotees, who are free from all personal desire, are rapt in thought of my pastimes, are fixed in me, without any feelings of enmity, and are equal to all conditions of the world. Let me be purified by the dust from their feet! SB** [**11.14.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.16)

He faces the devotees, situating himself in all directions (*sarvato mukhaḥ*).

### Verse 101

*sulabhaḥ suvrataḥ siddhaḥ śatrujic chatru-tāpanaḥ \|*  
*nyagrodho ‘dumbaro ‘śvatthaś cāṇūrāndhra-niṣūdanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**818. Sulabhaḥ: he was easily attained by Śrutadeva and Bahulāśva in their houses.**  
**819. Suvrataḥ: he was the object of glorious festivals.**  
**820. Siddhaḥ: he was present in his deity form.**  
**821. Śatru-jit: he destroyed all obstacles for Śrutadeva.**  
**822. Śatru-tāpanaḥ: he destroyed all obstacles for Bahulāśva.**  
**823. Nyagrodhaḥ: he threw the demons to hell with contempt.**  
**824. Udumbaraḥ: he existed above the material realm.**  
**825. Aśvatthaḥ: he remained the controller of the temporary material realm.**  
**826. Cāṇūrāndhra-niṣūdanaḥ: he killed Cāṇura.**

**Комментарий:**

Now his stay in Mithilā is described. He was attained by Śrutadeva and Bahulāśva at their houses (*sulabhaḥ*).

He was commemorated in special vratas with great festivals like janmāṣtamī (*suvrataḥ*). He became visible to them along with Vyāsa and others since they could not go to Dvārakā, since he wanted to receive their worship.

Why did the two not go to Dvārakā? He was eternally situated in his deity form (*siddhaḥ*). Experiencing the deity to be Kṛṣṇa directly, they did not go to Dvārakā. He resided in their houses at all times as the deity form, since he was controlled by his devotees, and removed unfavorable elements to their bhakti.

Thus situated in the heart, he destroyed the enemies of Śrutadeva, who was a brahmarṣi, such as kāma and other obstacles (*śatru-jit*).

He destroyed the enemies of the rājarṣi Bahulāśva—whatever was an obstacle to his ruling the kingdom (*śatru-tāpanaḥ*).

Treating the enemies with contempt (*nyak*) he threw to hell (*rodha*) the opponents of his devotees (*nyagrodhaḥ*). This follows words of the pacādi group.

He exists (*vara*) above the material realm, outstanding (*udgata*) with all excellent qualities (*udumvaraḥ*). Or he has a supreme realm (*varaḥ*) in the sky (*uḍu*). This is formed according to the rule *prṣodarādīni* (Pāṇini 6.3.109). This means he brings the devotee to his eternal realm.

He eternally controls the material world which does not (*a*) endure with a permanent form (*śvasṭa*). It is temporary (*aśvatthaḥ*). He is eternally its controller.

It is not difficult for the controller of all moving and nonmoving beings to destroy the devotees’ enemies and to give happiness to his devotees. An example is then given. He killed Cāṇura who came from Andhra (*cāṇurāndhra-nisūdanaḥ*). As he destroyed the enemies and gave bliss to the devotees of Mathurā, he did the same for these two devotees.

### Verse 102

*sahasrārciḥ sapta-jihvaḥ saptaidhāḥ sapta-vāhanaḥ \|*  
*amūrtir anagho ‘cintyo bhaya-kṛd bhayanāśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**827. Sahasrārciḥ: he is the cause of the thousand rays existing in the sun.**  
**828. Sapta-jihvaḥ: he is the cause of the seven tongues of fire.**  
**829. Saptaidhāḥ: he burns the seven worlds like kindling.**  
**830. Sapta-vāhanaḥ: he maintains the seven worlds.**  
**831. Amūrtiḥ: his form is non-different from his self.**  
**832. Anaghaḥ: he is the purest.**  
**833. Acintyaḥ: he is inconceivable.**  
**834. Bhaya-kṛt: he arranges for speculators to receive a fearful birth.**  
**835. Bhaya-nāśanaḥ: he destroys fear for those who defeat speculation.**

**Комментарий:**

It is easy for his servants to accomplish these tasks, what to speak of the Lord. By him a thousand rays exist in the sun (*sahasrārciḥ*). *Yena sūryas tapati tejaseddhaḥ*: because of the Lord, the sun, bright with light, shines. (Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa 3.12.9)

*yad āditya-gataṁ tejo jagad bhāsayate 'khilam*  
*yac candramasi yac cāgnau tat tejo viddhi māmakam*

**Know that the light of the sun, moon and fire, which illuminates this universe, is my light. BG** [**15.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.12)

The seven tongues of fire starting with Kālī and Karālīmānajava exist because of him (*sapta-jihvaḥ*).

Because of him the seven worlds burn like wood fuel (*saptedhāḥ*). His form of Saṅkarṣaṇa is the fire of destruction.

*pātāla-talam ārabhya saṅkarṣaṇa-mukhānalaḥ*  
*dahann ūrdhva-śikho viśvag vardhate vāyuneritaḥ*

**Beginning from Pātālaloka, a fire grows, emanating from the mouth of Saṅkarṣaṇa. Its flames shoot upward, driven by great winds, and it scorches everything in all directions. SB** [**11.3.10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/3.10)

He supports the seven worlds, giving them prosperity (*sapta-vāhanaḥ*). This means that as Viṣṇu he is the maintainer of the universe.

His form is indicated in the next names. His form is not different from his svarūpa (*amūrtiḥ*). His form is himself (not a body separate from the self).

*Ātmetyevopāsīta:* one should meditate on the Lord who is ātmā. (Śatapatha-brāhmaṇa)

*satya-jñānānantaṇanda-matraika-rasa-mūrtayaḥ*

**The forms all had eternal, unlimited forms, full of knowledge and bliss, which were one form. SB** [**10.13.54**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/13.54)

*arūpavad eva tat pradhānatvād*

**The Lord does not possess a form since his form is his very self. Brahma-sūtra** [**3.2.14**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.2.14)

*samāna evaṁ cābhedāt*

**Though there are differences because of the Lord's features, the Lord is still one entity, because his features are non-different from himself. Brahma-sūtra** [**3.3.20**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.3.20)

He is the purest (*anaghaḥ*).

Pavitrāṇāṁ pavitraṁ yaḥ: he is the purest of all pure things. (Mahābhārata 13.80.1)

How can he who is knowledge and bliss have a form? He is inconceivable (*acintyaḥ*). He is beyond logical explanation and is understood only by scriptures.

Naiṣā tarkeṇa matir āpaneyā: one cannot conceive of the Lord by logic. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad)

*takrāpratiṣthānāt*

**The Lord is not established by logic. Brahma-sūtra** [**2.1.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/2.1.11)

If one maintains that the Lord can be understood by logic, the Lord puts one into a fear situation such as being born as a jackal (*bhaya-kṛt*).

*ānvīkṣikīṁ tarka-vidyām anurakto nirarthikām*  
*tasyaiva phala-nirvṛttiḥ sṛgālatvaṁ mama dvija*

**If a person is attached to useless argumentation to understand the Lord, he gains no result. I make this person to be born as a jackal. Mahābhārata 12.173.45**

He destroys fear for those who oppose such theories (*bhaya-nāśaṇaḥ*).

### Verse 103

*aṇur bṛhat-kṛśaḥ sthūlo guṇa-bhṛn nirguṇo mahān \|*  
*adhṛtas sva-dhṛtaḥ svāsyaḥ prāgvaṁśo vaṁśa-vardhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**836. Aṇuḥ: he is very small, accompanying the jīva.**  
**837. Bṛhat: he is huge, bigger than the universe.**  
**838. Kṛśaḥ: he is thin. 839. Sthūlaḥ: he is massive.**  
**840. Guṇa-bhṛt: he supports the material guṇas.**  
**841. Nirguṇaḥ: he is not touched by the guṇas.**  
**842. Mahān: he is to be worshipped because of this.**  
**843. Adhṛtaḥ: he is not supported by anything.**  
**844. Sva-dhṛtaḥ: he supports himself.**  
**845. Svāsyaḥ: he has a beautiful face.**  
**846. Prāgvaṁśaḥ: his family members are eternally liberated.**  
**847. Vaṁśa-vardhanaḥ: he increased his family members.**

**Комментарий:**

Six names explain the Lord’s inconceivable śakti. He accompanies the jīva on the suṣumna path (*aṇuḥ*).

He cannot fit into the universe (*bṛhat*).

*sa bhūmiṁ viśvato vrṭvā atyatiṣṭhad daṣāṅgulam*

**He spread out the earth everywhere and remained in the heart. Puruṣa-sūkta**

He is so thin that he has no problem to enter even stone (*kṛśaḥ*).

He is solid, as the form of millions of universe (*sthulaḥ*).

He supports the material guṇas such as sattva (*guṇa-bhṛt*).

He is not contaminated by the guṇas he supports (*aguṇaḥ*).

*etad īśānam īśasya prakṛti-stho 'pi tad-guṇaiḥ na yujyate sadātmasthair yathā buddhis tad-āśrayāḥ*

**This is the power of the Lord: though he is situated in prakṛti, his is not affected by the guṇas which are situated in him, just as the intelligence of the devotee remembering the Lord is not affected by the guṇas. SB** [**1.11.39**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.39)

Because he has this inconceivable nature he is to be worshipped by all beings (*mahān*).

No one needs to supports the Lord (*adhṛtaḥ*).

*mat-sthāni sarva-bhūtāni na cāhaṁ teśv avasthitaḥ*

**All beings are in me but I am not in them. BG** [**9.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.4)

He is supported by his own great powers (*sva-dhṛtaḥ*).

*sa bhagavaḥ kasmin pratiṣṭhitaḥ sva mahimni*

**Dear sir, in what is the supreme being situated? He is situated in his own glory. Chāndogya Upaniṣad** [**7.24.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/7.24.1)

He has a face (*āsyaḥ*) more beautiful (*su*) than the moon or a lotus (*svāsyaḥ*). Or his face is decorated with all the Vedas. Or he is suitable to dwell (*āsati*) in the devotees (*sveṣu*), who take him as their very life.

The members of his dynasty such as Pradyumna were previously perfect or nitya-siddha (*prāg-vaṁśaḥ*).

He increased the dynasty of the Yadus (*vaṁśa-vardhanaḥ*). The main family members were five hundred and sixty million.

### Verse 104

*bhāra-bhṛt kathito yogī yogīśaḥ sarva-kāma-daḥ \|*  
*āśramaḥ śramaṇaḥ, kṣāmaḥ suparṇo vāyu-vāhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**848. Bhāra-bhṛt: he maintains the devotee’s body.**  
**849. Kathitaḥ: this quality is described in the scriptures.**  
**850. Yogī: he is endowed with the quality of having his desires fulfilled.**  
**851. Yogīśaḥ: he gives powers to yogīs.**  
**852. Sarva-kāma-daḥ: he fulfills the material desires of a fallen yogī.**  
**853. Āśramaḥ: he makes the yogī take birth in a family of devotees.**  
**854. Śramaṇaḥ: he makes the yogī practice in that situation.**  
**855. Kṣāmaḥ: he gives ability to the yogī to progress.**  
**856. Suparṇaḥ: he has beautiful tulasī leaves on his feet.**  
**857. Vāyu-vāhanaḥ: he quickly carried Uparicari Vasu back to his position on Garuḍa.**

**Комментарий:**

The next names describe how the Lord is ready to nourish the devotee. He carries the wealth of maintaining the devotee’s body (*bhāra-bhṛt*).

Bhartā san bhriyamāno vibhāti: the maintainer, the Lord, is served by the devotees. Taittirīya Āraṇyaka 3.14.1

*darśana-dhyāna-saṁsparśair matsya-kūrma-vihaṅgamāḥ*  
*svāny apatyāni puṣṇanti tathāham api padmaja*

**The fish nourishes its offspring by seeing, the turtle by meditation, and the birds by touch. I nourish my devotees with similar affection, O Brahmā. Padma Purāṇa**

He is described in this way (*kathitaḥ*).

How can one person maintain unlimited devotees? He is endowed (*yogī*) with the quality of having all his desires manifested eternally. *Satya-saṇkalpa* is one of the hundred and eight names. He maintains them just by his will.

He is the master of yogīs like the Kumāras (*yogīśaḥ*). He gives them their powers.

Some names describe his ability to support the devotees by destroying material desires or impressions. He gives all desires in places like Svarga to fallen yogīs because they desire enjoyment (*sarva-kāma-daḥ*).

After enjoying in Svarga he makes the yogī take birth in a devotee’s house and has them make efforts in that shelter (*āśramaḥ*).

*prāpya puṇya-kṛtān lokān uśitvā śāśvatīḥ samaḥ*  
*śucīnāṁ śrīmatāṁ gehe yoga-bhraṣṭo 'bhijāyate*

**After attaining the worlds of the pious and living there for long years, the fallen** ***yogī*** **is born in the house of the religious and wealthy. BG** [**6.41**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.41)

*athavā yoginām eva kule bhavati dhīmatām*

**Or, if he has practiced a long time, he is born in the family of skillful yogīs. BG** [**6.42**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.42)

He makes the yogī exert himself (*śramaṇaḥ*) or practice yoga because he has previous impressions of Svarga and birth which have not been destroyed.

*tatra tam buddhi-saṁyogaṁ labhate paurva-dehikam*

**On taking such a birth, he revives the divine consciousness of his previous life. BG** [**6.43**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.43)

Those who have fallen from yoga can attain the Lord by practicing yoga because of the Lord’s assistance. He makes them qualified (*kṣāmaḥ*). This word is formed according to the rule *halaś ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.121).

*na hi kalyāṇa-kṛt kaścid durgatiṁ tāta gacchati*

**One who has done the auspicious work of yoga is not deprived of the benefits, O my son. BG** [**6.40**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.40)

He has attractive tulasī leaves (at his feet), not gold (*suparṇaḥ*). One can see the story of Viṣṇu dāsa and the Chola king, in Padma Purāṇa.

The Lord carried Uparicara-vasu, a great devotee cursed by a great sage to come to earth, to his previous position as Uparicara, using Garuḍa who flew like the wind (*vāyu-vāhanaḥ*). This story is famous in Nārāyanīya.

### Verse 105

*dhanur-dharo dhanur vedo daṇḍo damayitā damaḥ \|*  
*aparājitas sarva-saho niyantā niyamo yamaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**858. Dhanur-dharaḥ: he held a bow at Lakṣmaṇā’s svayamvara.**  
**859. Dhanur-vedaḥ: he had knowledge of how to pierce the fish.**  
**860. Daṅḍaḥ: he punished the enemies through Arjuna.**  
**861. Damayitā: he tamed seven bulls at Nagnajitā’s svayaṁvara.**  
**862. Adamaḥ: he was the only tamer.**  
**863. Aparājitaḥ: he could not be defeated by Rukmi’s troops.**  
**864. Sarva-sahaḥ: he tolerated all of Rukmi’s insults.**  
**865. Niyantā: he restrained Rukmi.**  
**866. Niyamaḥ: he restrained Jāmbavān. 867. Yamaḥ: he gave up fighting with Jāmbavān.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa as the husband of Lakṣmaṇā is described in the following names. He held a bow to pierce the fish at Lakṣmaṇā’s svayaṁvara (*dhanur-dharaḥ*). Only he could pierce the fish.

He had knowledge (*veda*) of the fish to be pierced by the arrow from the bow (*dhanur-vedaḥ*).

*matsyābhāsaṁ jale vīkṣya jñātvā ca tad-avasthitim*  
*pārtho yatto 'sṛjad bāṇaṁ nācchinat paspṛśe param*  
*rājanyeṣu nivṛtteṣu bhagna-māneṣu māniṣu*  
*bhagavān dhanur ādāya sajyaṁ kṛtvātha līlayā*  
*tasmin sandhāya viśikhaṁ matsyaṁ vīkṣya sakṛj jale*  
*chittveṣuṇāpātayat taṁ sūrye cābhijiti sthite*

**Then Arjuna looked at the reflection of the fish in the water and determined its position. When he carefully shot his arrow at it, however, he did not pierce the target but merely grazed it. After all the arrogant kings had given up, their pride broken, the Supreme Lord picked up the bow, easily strung it and then fixed his arrow upon it. As the sun stood in the constellation Abhijit, he looked at the fish in the water only once and then pierced it with the arrow, knocking it to the ground. SB** [**10.83.24-26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/83.24)

He punished the enemies through Arjuna (*daṇḍaḥ*).

He tamed seven bulls at Nagnajitā’s svayaṁvara (*damayitā*).

Except for him there was no other tamer (*adamaḥ*).

He is described as the husband of Rukminī. He could not be defeated by Rukmi’s troops (*aparājitaḥ*).

He tolerated all of Rukmi’s insulting words (*sarva-sahaḥ*).

He subdued Rukmi who used harsh words, by tying him up and cutting his hair (*niyantā*).

He is described as the husband of Jāmbavatī. He restrained (*niyacchati*) Jāmbavān (*niyamaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *yamaḥ sam-upa-ni-viṣu ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.63).

Bringing Jāmbavatī close to him, pleased with Jāmbavān who ha.

### Verse 106

*sattvavān sāttvikaḥ satyaḥ satya-dharma-parāyaṇaḥ \|*  
*abhiprāyaḥ priyārhorhaḥ priya-kṛt priti-vardhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**868. Satva-vān: he had determination to obtain Satyābhāmā.**  
**869. Sāttvikaḥ: he had strength of intellect, body and prāṇas.**  
**870. Satyaḥ: he had Satyabhāmā as the object of his love.**  
**871. Satya-dharma-parāyaṇaḥ: he was the shelter of prema with a vow of love for his wife.**  
**872. Abhiprāyaḥ: he is attained by all types of people.**  
**873. Priyārhaḥ: he honors the devotees.**  
**874. Arhaḥ: he is honored by those devotees.**  
**875. Priya-kṛt: he shows affection to them.**  
**876. Priti-vardhanaḥ: his affection for them increases.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa as the husband of Satyabhāmā is described. He had great determination to attain Satyabhāmā (*sattvavān*). *Sattva* can mean the guṇa sattva, a piśāca, strength, matter, nature, ātmā, determination, the heart, prāṇas or living beings, according to Viśva.

He was qualified with strength of intellect, strength of body and strength of prāṇas (*sāttvikaḥ*). The ending is formed according to *tad arhati* (Pāṇini 5.1.63).

“Having defamed me, Satrājit has brought the jewel, but is drowning in an ocean of fear and shame to offer it to me. To obtain forgiveness for his offenses he offers his daughter Satyabhāmā, endowed with extraordinary qualities and beauty along with the gem.” In this way he showed his strength of intellect etc.

He had Satyabhāmā as the object of his love (satyaḥ). The word is formed by the rule arśa ādibho ‘c (Pāṇini 5.2.127).

He was the shelter of dharma free from cheating-- prema with exclusive vow of love for his wife (*satya-dharma-parāyaṇaḥ*). *Satya* means a vow and truth according to Nānārtha-varga. He took that vow as his very life. *Satyabhāmā-rataḥ* is one of the hundred and eight names.

He is attained (*preyate*) everywhere (*abhitaḥ*) because of his nature of being attracted to the devotee and his soothing nature, like the moon (*abhiprāyaḥ*).

Not desiring Lakṣmī, he honors his devotees who know his svarūpa and who offer love to him with pure devotion (*priyārhaḥ*).

He is respected by the devotees with all their hearts, who give up everything else (*arhaḥ*). This follows the pacādi group of words in its formation.

He has affection to them because of their devotion (*priya-kṛt*).

Disregarding Lakṣmī, his affection for them increases (*prīti-vardhanaḥ*).

*caturvidhā bhajante māṁ janāḥ sukṛtino 'rjuna*  
*arto jijñāsur arthārthī jñānī ca bhāratarśabha*  
*teśāṁ jñānī nitya-yukta ekabhaktir viśiśyate*  
*priyo hi jñānino 'tyartham ahaṁ sa ca mama priyaḥ*

**Four types of religious persons worship me, O Arjuna: the sufferer, the inquirer, the enjoyer and the jñānī. Of these four types, the jñānī, who is constantly engaged in thinking of me, who is practicing pure bhakti, is the best. This jñānī loves only me, and I love only him. BG** [**16.17**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/16.17)

He is affectionate towards the arta and others because of his compassionate nature. This attraction to the devotees while ignoring Lakṣmī is well known in the Bhāgavatam and other scriptures.

### Verse 107

*vihāyasa-gatir jyMtiḥ surucir huta-bhug vibhuḥ \|*  
*ravir virocanaḥ sūryaḥ savitā ravi-locanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**877. Vihāyasa-gatiḥ: he takes the devotee to the spiritual sky.**  
**878. Jyotiḥ: he arranges the path of light.**  
**879. Suruciḥ: he arranges the path of the day.**  
**880. Hutabhuk vibhuḥ: he arranges the path of the śukla-pakṣa.**  
**881. Raviḥ: he arranges the uttarāyaṇa path.**  
**882. VirMcanaḥ: he arranges the path of the saṁvatsara.**  
**883. Sūryaḥ: he arranges the path of the wind.**  
**884. Savitā: he arranges the path of the sun. 885. Ravi-lMcanaḥ: he arranges the path of the moon.**

**Комментарий:**

Twelve names describe how he is the direct cause for the devotees’ attaining his lotus feet.

He allows the devotee to attain the destination in the spiritual sky (*vihāyasa-gatiḥ*). The formation follows the rule *pṛṣodarādīni* (Pāṇini 6.3.109)

How do they attain him as the goal? Leaving by the suṣumna the devotee attains the fire. This attainment of the path of light is arranged by him. Thus he is called light (*jyotiḥ*). *Ja* means light or fire.

He manifests the glorious day (*suruciḥ*). This is formed by the rule *igupadhāṭh kit* (Uṇādi sutra 526). Because of him, the devotee goes by the path of the day.

In him the moon is full (*hūta-bhuk-vibhuḥ*). He is like the śukla-pakṣa because the night is lit up when the śukla-pakṣa arrives. Devadatta is called a lion because of the similarity of bravery. By his mercy the devotee goes on the path of the śukla-pakṣa.

Because of his mercy the devotees goes (ravate) by the uttarāyaṇa path (raviḥ). The root ru  means to go. The word is formed using the rule aca ī (Uṇādi sūtra 581)
