He makes the devotee shine (*virocayati*) as he goes on the path of the saṁvatsara (*virocanaḥ*). By his mercy the devotee goes on the saṁvatsara path.

Leading his devotee, he makes the wind blow ( sūryaḥ ). The word is formed according to rājasūya-sūrya  
(Pāṇini 3.1.114).

By his mercy the devotee goes by the wind path.

He produces (*sūte*) the devotee’s attainment of the sun (*savitā*). By his mercy the devotee goes by the path of the sun.

He shows (*locayati*) the moon by the light of the sun (*ravi-locanaḥ*). By his mercy the devotee goes by the path of the moon.

### Verse 108

*ananto hutabhug-bhMktā sukhado naikajo ‘grajaḥ \|*  
*anirviṇṇaḥ sadāmarṣī lokādhiṣṭhānam adbhutaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**886. Anantaḥ: he does not impede the devotee’s progress on the path.**  
**887. Hutabhuk-bhoktā: he nourishes the devotee while he is on the path with offerings of nectar**  
**888. Sukha-daḥ: he awards the devotee a spiritual body.**  
**889. Naika-daḥ: he gives many objects to him.**  
**890. Agrajaḥ: he manifests himself in front of the devotee.**  
**891. Anirviṇṇaḥ: he is never lax in helping those who have not achieved perfection to attain him.**  
**892. Sadāmarṣī: he considers the situation of the person who has not completed the sādhana.**  
**893. LMkādhiṣṭhānam: he is the eternal shelter of these devotees.**  
**894. Adbhutaḥ: he astonishes the devotee with his ever new form, qualities and activities.**

**Комментарий:**

He does not bind the devotee to stagnation on the path or stop (*antati*) the devotee’s progress. He makes the devotee go on the path of lightning (*anantaḥ*).

He nourishes the devotee going to his abode with oblations, transformations of nectar from the moon, through Varuṇa, Indra, and the prajāpatis along with the associates of the Lord, after going by the path of lightning (*hūta-bhug-bhoktā*).

Though the Lord is the various causes on this path, this does not agree with

tesām ahaṁ samudhartā mṛtu-saṁsāra-sāgarāt: I quickly deliver the devotee from the path of death. (BG [12.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.7))

All these factors mentioned above are dependent on this factor. Led by an associate of the Lord, the devotee, who has had his subtle body destroyed, is given a body of bliss of an associate (*sukhadaḥ*). Or the Lord gives a blissful form (*sukha-daḥ*) to the renounced, distressed devotee who has come without regard for the path of arcirādi.

*nayāmi paramaṁ sthānam arcir ādigataṁ vinā*  
*garuḍa-skandham āropya yatheccham anivāritaḥ*

**Putting him on Garuḍa’s shoulder, by my will, without resistance, I lead the devotee to the highest place, without having to go by the path of arcirādi. Varāha Purāṇa**

One should consider the body of the devotee to be composed of knowledge and bliss, like the Lord’s body, produced by his will.

*yatra tv asya sarvam ātmaivābhūt tat kena kaṁ paśyet*

**When all the senses of the liberated jīva become filled with the Lord's śakti, how does he see and what does he see: he sees the Lord by senses endowed with the Lord's śakti. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad** [**4.5.15**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.5.15)

*vasanti yatra puruṣāḥ sarve vaikuṇṭha-mūrtayaḥ*  
*ye 'nimitta-nimittena dharmeṇārādhayan harim*

**In that place all men reside with forms like the Lord and they worship the Lord with unmotivated service. SB** [**3.15.14**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/15.14)

He does not give one object (*naikadaḥ*) to the devotee but rather gives everything to him, even himself.

Endowed with all wealth and powers, he appears (*jaḥ*) in front (*agra*) of that devotee (*agra-jaḥ*). He never separates himself from the devotee.

The twelve devatās bringing the worshipper on the path of the Lord are established by the Lord.

*arcir-ādinā tat-prathiteḥ*

**Persons in knowledge start the ascension with light since that is understood in scriptures. Brahma-sūtra** [**4.3.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.3.1)

By light, day, waxing fortnight, northern course of the sun, year, air, sun, moon, lightning, water, Indra-loka and Prajāpati-loka, the liberated person goes to the abode of the Lord. This is the path called arcirādi.

With great effort, many of his devotees attain that rare abode. But the Lord is never lax in helping those who have not perfected their sādhana to attain his feet (*anirviṇṇaḥ).*

The Lord also considers (*āmarṣī*) benefiting other devotees (*sat*), who have not completed their sādhana (*sadāmarṣī*).

He gives eternal shelter to his devotees who have come close to him (*lokādhiṣṭhānam*).

He astonishes the devotee at every moment with his ever fresh qualities, forms and pastimes (*adbhutaḥ*). This is derived from the root *bhū* (to be) using the rule *ādibhuvo* *ḍutac* (Uṇādī sūtra 682).

### Verse 109

*sanāt sanātana-tamaḥ kapilaḥ kapir avyayaḥ \|*  
*svasti-daḥ svasti-kṛt svasti svasti-bhuk svasti-dakṣiṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**895. Sanāt: he accepts the offerings of the devotee.**  
**896. Sanātana-tamaḥ: he appears attractively to the nitya-mukta.**  
**897. Kapilaḥ: he desires those devotee.**  
**898. Kapiḥ: he accepts the water offered.**  
**899. Avyayaḥ: he does not leave the devotee.**  
**900. Svastidaḥ: he gives auspiciousness.**  
**901. Svasti-kṛt: he destroys the auspiciousness of the non-devotee.**  
**902. Svasti: he is auspiciousness personified.**  
**903. Svasti-bhuk: he nourishes the devotee’s auspiciousness.**  
**904. Svasti-dakṣiṇaḥ: he gives the auspicious dhāma to his worshipper as a reward.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord accepts (*atti*) what is offered (*sana*), such as fragrance or food (*sanāt*).

He appears most excellent (*tamaḥ*) to the nitya-muktas (*sanātana-tamaḥ*). Thought they are eternally with the Lord, they see him as if he were ever fresh.

He desires (*kāmayate*) them (*kapilaḥ*). This word is formed from the root *kam* according to the rule *kameḥ paśca* (Uṇādi sutra 55).

He drinks (*pibati*) the water *(ka*) offered in his service (*kapī*). The word is formed using the rule *ato lopaḥ* (Pāṇini 6.4.48)

He does not leave (*vyeti*) their association (*avyayaḥ*).

*Svasti* is a combination of *su* and *asti.* It means auspicious. This is generally treated as an indeclinable. He gives auspiciousness (*svasti-daḥ*).

He removes (*kṛntati*) auspiciousness from the non-devotees (*svasti-kṛt*).

He is auspiciousness personified (*svasti*).

He nourishes (*bhunakti*) the auspiciousness of those who remember him (*svasti-bhuk*). Or he experiences auspiciousness personally.

He gives his eternal abode and other things *(svasti*) as the reward (*dakṣiṇā*) to persons who perform sacrifices in the form of action of bhakti (*svasti-dakṣiṇaḥ*).

### Verse 110

*araudraḥ kunḍalī cakrī vikramy ūrjita-śāsanaḥ \|*  
*śabdātigaḥ śabda-sahaḥ śiśiraḥ śarvarī-karaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**905. Araudraḥ: he is blissful.**  
**906. Kunḍalī: he wears makara earrings.**  
**907. Cakrī: he has unlimited troops.**  
**908. Vikramī: he has power to defeat unlimited demons.**  
**909. Ūrjita-śāsanaḥ: he must be obeyed even by Brahmā and Śiva.**  
**910. Śabdātigaḥ: he cannot be fully described by words.**  
**911. Śabda-sahaḥ: he heard the cry of Draupadī.**  
**912. Śiśiraḥ: he took away the Pāṇḍavas’ suffering.**  
**913. Śarvarī-karaḥ: he took a piece of spinach in his hand.**

**Комментарий:**

He is full of bliss, a form of happiness (*araudraḥ*). *Aroṣaṇo hy asau devaḥ*: the Lord is devoid of anger. (Mahābhārata 12.323.15)

He wears makara shapped earrings (*kuṇḍalī*). This indicates that he wears a crown and bracelets as well.

He has unlimited troops (*cakṛī*).

He has power to destroy innumerable wicked demons (*vikramī*).

His orders cannot be disregarded by Brahmā and Śiva (*ūrjita-śāsaṇaḥ*).

He goes beyond words (*śabdātigaḥ*). The formation is according to the rule *anyeṣv api dṛśyate* (Pāṇini 3.2.110)

His unlimited qualities cannot be fully described by Ananta or Sarasvatī.

Names describe how he took away the suffering of the Pāṇḍavas and the problem caused by Durvāsā. He heard the words of Draupadī (*śabda-sahaḥ*). The word is formed according to the rule *sañjñāyāṁ bhṛtṛ* (Pāṇini 3.2.46).

He is called *kṛsṇāvyasana-karṣakaḥ* (he removed the suffering of Daurpadī) in the hundred and eight names.

He took away their suffering just by glancing at them (*śiśiraḥ*).

He took in his hand a bit of spinach to satisfy the appetite of Durvāsā (*śarvarī-karaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *śṛṇoteḥ hrasvaśca* (Uṇādi sutra 126).

He satisfied the universe by eating the piece of spinach. He thus protected them from the fearful anger of Durvāsā.

### Verse 111

*akrūraḥ peśalo dakṣo dakṣiṇaḥ kṣamiṇāṁ varaḥ \|*  
*vidvattamo vīta-bhayaḥ puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**915. Akrūraḥ: he did not harm Durvāsā for his offense.**  
**915. Peśalaḥ: he skillfully solved the problem for both sides.**  
**916. Dakṣaḥ: he came quickly to the Pāṇḍavas.**  
**917. Dakṣiṇaḥ: he curtailed Duryodhana’s efforts.**  
**918. Kṣamiṇāṁ varaḥ: he was most tolerant.**  
**919. Vidvattamaḥ: he was most skilful at removing danger for Gajendra.**  
**920. Vīta-bhayaḥ: he delivered Gajendra from fear.**  
**921. Puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ: he destroys sin of persons who hear or relate this story.**

**Комментарий:**

Because he is kind to brāhmaṇas, Kṛṣṇa did not harm Durvāsā, though Durvāsā had harassed the Pāṇḍavas (*akrūraḥ*). The word is formed according to the *rule krṭeccaḥ krū ca* (Uṇādi 181)

He was skillful or attractive by performing an action to resolve the problem for both parties (*peśalaḥ). Peśala* means attractive or skillful according to Viśva.

He came to the Pāṇḍavas quickly, simply by being remembered (*dakṣaḥ*).

He curtailed (*damśati*) the efforts of Duryodhana (*dakṣiṇaḥ*). The ending follows the words of the *raudhādi* class of words.

He forgave the offense of Durvāsā (*kṣamiṇāṁ varaḥ*).

The deliverance of Gajendra is next indicated. He was most skillful in eliminating the danger to Gajendra, who had surrendered to him (*vidvattamaḥ*).

He destroyed Gajendra’s fear of the crocodile (*vīta-bhayaḥ*). He pulled the elephant and the crocodile out of the water and pierced the crocodile. Thus he made Gajendra fearless.

*grāha-grastaṁ gajendraṁ ca tam grāhaṁ ca jalāśayāt*  
*ujjahārāprameyātmā tarasā madhusūdanaḥ*

**The unlimited Lord quickly took the crocodile and Gajendra, who was caught by the crocodile, from the water. Vāmana Purāṇa 58.62**

He destroys sins of persons who hear about how he delivered Gajendra (*puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ*).

*śrutenāhi kuruśreṣṭha smṛtena kathitena vā*  
*gajendra-mokṣaṇānenaiva sadyaḥ pāpāt pramucyate*

**One is immediately freed of sin by hearing, remembering or relating the story of the deliverance of Gajendra. Vāmana Purāṇa**

### Verse 112

*uttāraṇo duṣkṛti-hā puṇyo duḥsvapna-nāśanaḥ \|*  
*vīrahā rakṣaṇas santo jīvanaḥ paryavasthitaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**922. Uttāraṇaḥ: he lifted Gajendra from the water.**  
**923. Duṣkṛti-hā: he killed the evil crocodile.**  
**924. Puṇyaḥ: he purifies others by this story.**  
**925. Duḥsvapna-nāśanaḥ: he removes the effects of bad dreams by this story.**  
**926. Vīra-hā: he killed the strong crocodile.**  
**927. Rakṣaṇaḥ: he protected Gajendra just by his touch.**  
**928. Santaḥ: he bestowed auspiciousness.**  
**929. Jīvanaḥ: he brought the crocodile to life.**  
**930. Paryavasthitaḥ: he was situated close to Gajendra.**

**Комментарий:**

He lifted Gajendra from the water (*uttaraṇaḥ*).

He killed the wicked crocodile (*duṣkṛti-hā*).

*sthala-sthaṁ dārayām āsa grāhaṁ cakreṇa mādhavaḥ*  
*mocayām āsa nāgendraṁ pāśebhyaḥ śaraṇāgatam*

**The Lord pierced the crocodile which now on the land with his cakra. He liberated the surrendered elephant from material bondage. Vāmana Purāṇa**

He purifies persons even today by the story of Gajendra’s deliverance (*puṇyaḥ*).

He destroys the faults in bad dreams by remembrance of Gajendra, the Lord and the water body (*duḥsvapna-nāśanaḥ*).

*tad ahaṁ śrotum icchāmi nṛṇāṁ duḥsvapna-nāśanam*  
*duḥsvapna-darṣanaṁ ghoram āvekṣya bharatarśabha*  
*prayātaḥ kiṁ japej jāpyaṁ vibudhaḥ kim anusmaret*  
*ye māṁ tvāṁ ca saraś caiva grahasya ca vimokṣaṇam*  
*ye smariṣyanti manujāḥ prayatāḥ sthira-buddhayaḥ*  
*duḥsvapno naśyate teśāṁ susvapnaś ca bhaviśyati*

**I desire to hear about the destruction of bad dreams for men. O best of the Bharatas! What should the intelligent person chant or remember with care on seeing terrible dreams? Intelligent people who remember with care me, you, the water body and the liberation of the crocodile will destroy bad dreams and sleep peacefully. Viṣṇu Dharma**

He killed the strong crocodile (vīra-hā).

*sthala-sthaṁ dārayām āsa grāhaṁ cakreṇa mādhavaḥ*

**He killed the crocodile situated on the land. Vāmana Purāṇa**

He protected Gajendra just by beginning to touch him (*rakṣaṇaḥ*).

*evam uktvā maharaja gajendraṁ madhusūdanaḥ*  
*sparśayām āsa hastena gajaṁ gandharvam eva ca*

**O king! Saying this, the Lord touched Gajendra and the crocodile (who was now a Gandharva) with his hand. Vāmana Purāṇa**

He gave (*tanoti*) auspiciousness (*sa*ṁ) to Gajendra who had surrendered (*santaḥ*). This follows the rule *anyeṣv api dṛṣyate* (Pāṇini 3.2.101)

He brought the crocodile to life as a Gandharva (*jīvanaḥ*).

*sa hi devala-śāpena hūhūr gandharva-sattamaḥ*  
*grāhatvam agamat kṛṣṇād vadhaṁ prāpya divaṁ gataḥ*

**The Gandharva named Hūhū became a crocodile because of Devala’s curse. On being killed by the Lord he went to the sky. Viṣṇu Dharma**

Because of affection for his devotee, he remained moving around Gajendra (*pary-avasthitaḥ*).

*bhaktiṁ tasya tu sañcintya nāgasyāmogha-saṁstavāt*  
*prītimān bhagavān rājan śrutvā cakra-gadādharaḥ*  
*ārūhya garuḍaṁ viṣṇur ājagāma surottamaḥ*  
*sannidhyaṁ kalpayām āsa tasmin sarasi loka-dhṛk*

**Thinking of his bhakti, the Lord, best of the devas, seer of all planets, pleased with his effective prayer, holding his cakra and club, mounted Garuḍa and came to the lake. There he gave Gajendra his association. Viṣṇu Dharma**

### Verse 113

*ananta-rūpo ‘nanta-śrīr jita-manyur bhayāpahaḥ \|*  
*catur-aśro gabhīrātmā vidiśo vyādiśo ‘diśaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**931. Ananta-rūpaḥ: Kṛṣṇa had unlimited forms.**  
**932. Ananta-śrīḥ: he had unlimited powers and wealth.**  
**933. Jita-manyuḥ: he did not become angry at Brahmā.**  
**934. Bhayāpahaḥ: he gave Brahmā fearlessness.**  
**935. Catura-śraḥ: he acted as an inferior to his mother and friends.**  
**936. Gabhirātmā: his intentions could be not understood by Brahmā.**  
**937. Avidiśaḥ: he did not give specific instructions to Brahmā.**  
**938. Vyādiśaḥ: he gives instructions according to qualification.**  
**939. Adiśaḥ: he is not given instructions by anyone else.**

**Комментарий:**

The brahma-mohana pastime is described. Kṛṣṇa had unlimited forms (*ananta-rūpaḥ*).

*yāvad vatsaka-vatsapālaka-vapur yāvat karāṅghry-ādikaṁ*  
*yāvad yaṣṭi-viśāṇa-veṇu-dala-śig yāvad vibhūśāmbaram*  
*yāvac chīla-guṇa-guṇābhidhākṛti-vayo yāvad vihārādikaṁ*  
*sarvaṁ viṣṇumayaṁ giro ‘ṅga-vad ajaḥ sarva-svarūpo babhau*

**Though unborn, Kṛṣṇa, because he is the embodiment of all forms, different and non-different from them like words being inseparable from their endings, he expanded himself into the exact number of missing cowherd boys and calves, with their exact bodily features, their particular types of hands, legs and other limbs, their sticks, bugles , flutes, ornamental flower petals, their lunch bags, their particular types of dress and ornaments placed in various ways, their names, ages and forms, and their special activities and characteristics, which were all non-different from him. SB** [**10.13.19**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/13.19)

The calves were also beyond counting.

*kṛṣṇa-vatsair asaṅkhyātair yūthī-kṛtya sva-vatsakān*  
*cārayanto 'rbha-līlābhir vijahrus tatra tatra ha*

**Along with the cowherd boys and their own groups of calves, Kṛṣṇa came out with an unlimited number of calves assembled. Then all the boys began to sport in the forest in a greatly playful spirit. SB** [**10.12.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/12.3)

He had unlimited powers (*ananta-śrīḥ*) since he was served by all wealth.

He did not become angry with Brahmā though he had a good reason (*jita-manyuḥ*).

Though Brahmā had offended him when he should have praised him, he gave him fearlessness (*bhayāpahaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *āśiṣi han* (Pāniṇi 3.2.49).

He acted as an inferior with friends and his mothers (*catura-sraḥ*).

His intentions could not be understood by Brahmā (*gabhīrātmā*).

He did not instruct Brahmā with particular details (*avidiśaḥ*).

This is formed according to the rule *igupadha-jñā-prī-karaḥ kaḥ* (Pāṇini 3.1.135)

He was displeased with Brahmā who was the knower of all the Vedas because Brahmā has separated him from his friends. He did not utter a word but by indications said “Go from here.”

This is because he teaches various (*vividha*) topics (*vyādiśaḥ*). It is proper for him to give instructions to the qualified person.

This is because no one gives instructions to him (*adiśaḥ*). He is the supreme Lord.

*tam īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaraṁ*  
*tam devatānāṁ paramaṁ ca daivatam*  
*patiṁ patīnāṁ paramaṁ parastād*  
*vidāma devaṁ bhuvaneśam īḍyam*

**We know the worshipable Lord, ruler of the world, the great Lord, supreme among controllers, supreme devatā among devatās, the master of masters, greater than the great. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad**

*svayaṁ tv asāmyātiśayas tryadhīśaḥ*  
*svarājya-lakṣmyāpta-samasta-kāmaḥ*  
*baliṁ haradbhiś cira-lokapālaiḥ*  
*kirīṭa-koṭīḍita-pāda-pīṭhaḥ*

**No one is equal to him or superior to him. He is master of the three energies and three Viṣṇus. All his desires are fulfilled by Lakṣmī and his rulership over his devotees, pastimes and qualities. His feet are praised by the sound by ten million crowns of the devatās offering respects while presenting gifts. SB** [**3.2.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.21)

### Verse 114

*anādir bhūr bhuvo lakṣmīs suvīro rucirāṅgadaḥ \|*  
*janano jana-janmādir bhīmo bhīma-parākramaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**940. Anādiḥ: he was not recognized as the Lord by Brahmā.**  
**941. Bhūḥ: he was merciful, though insulted by Brahmā.**  
**942. Bhuvo lakṣmīḥ: he ornamented the ground with his footprints.**  
**943. Suvīraḥ: he had courageous friends.**  
**944. Ruchirāṅgadaḥ: he made his friends’ limbs beautiful by his embrace.**  
**945. Jananaḥ: he manifested the spring rāsa dance.**  
**946. Jana-janmādiḥ: his birth and other pastimes are eternal.**  
**947. Bhimaḥ: he was ferocious in front of Śaṅkhacūḍa.**  
**948. Bhima-parākramaḥ: he attacked him ferociously.**

**Комментарий:**

He was not recognized (*ādiyate*) by Brahmā and other devatās (*anādiḥ*). They took him as a dependent.

He was (*bhūḥ*) merciful, though insulted by Brahmā. This is formed according to the rule *bhuvaḥ saṁjñanatarayoḥ* (Pāṇini 3.2.179).

The ground of Vraja was ornamented by his feet (*bhuvo lakṣmīḥ*).

He had friends such as Śrīdāma and Subhadra who were as courageous as himself (*suvīraḥ*).

He made their limbs beautiful (*rucirāṅga-daḥ*), when he embraced them.

The killing of Śaṅkhacūḍa is described. Surrounded by his gopīs he produced (*janayati*) the spring rāsa dance festival near Govardhana along with Balarāma and his gopīs (*jananaḥ*).

Producing a pastime usually indicates it is not eternal. However, his birth and other things with a beginning are without a beginning (*ajana-janmādiḥ*).

eko devo nitya-līlānurakto bhakta-vyāpī bhakta-hṛdy antarātmā

The one Lord is engaged in his eternal pastimes. He is also within the heart, spread in all his devotees. Puruṣa-bodhinī śruti

*janma karma ca me divyam*

**My birth and activities are all spiritual. BG** [**4.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.9)

The pastime exists in other manifestations. In this particular manifestation it makes its appearance (at a certain time). The eternal nature of the pastimes is explained elsewhere.

He became ferocious (*bhīmaḥ*) in front of Śaṅkhacūḍa, who had taken away the gopīs. Selecting Balarāma to protect the gopīs, he pursued the demon, killed him, and brought the Syamantaka jewel.

He was a ferocious attacker (bhīma-parākramaḥ).

### Verse 115

*ādhāra-nilayo ‘dhātā puṣpahāsaḥ prajāgaraḥ \|*  
*ūrdhvagas satpathācāraḥ prāṇadaḥ praṇavaḥ paṇaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**949. Ādhāra-nilayaḥ: he was the shelter for gopīs who were endowed with all qualities.**  
**950. Dhātā: he drank the nectar of their sweetness.**  
**951. Puṣpa-hāsaḥ: he smiled attractively as jasmine flowers.**  
**952. Prajāgaraḥ: he stayed awake all night during the rāsa dance.**  
**953. Ūrdhva-gaḥ: he had beauty which defeated Cupid.**  
**954. Satpathācāraḥ: he acted with pure love.**  
**955. Prāṇa-daḥ: he gave his very self to the gopīs.**  
**956. Pra-navaḥ: he was praised for his conduct and love.**  
**957. Paṇaḥ: he negotiated with the gopīs.**

**Комментарий:**

He was the shelter of the gopīs, who were endowed with all good qualities (*ādhāra-nilayaḥ*). Or it indicates Kṛṣṇa as the enjoyer of the rāsa dance. He was the complete shelter of the dear gopīs, the abode of all good qualities such as beauty, grace, music, dancing and poetry, who had been called by his flute in the autumn full moon.

He was the drinker of nectar in the form of their words, skills, smiles and glances (*dhātā). Dhātā* is derived from the root *dhet*, which means to drink. *Dhātā* means a person who drinks.

He smiled in a way suitable to the pastimes of love with the gopīs (*puṣpa-hāsaḥ*).

*udāra-hāsa-dvija-kunda-dīdhiti vyarocataināṅka ivoḍubhir vṛtaḥ*

**His smile revealed his shining rows of teeth, white like jasmine buds. He increased in beauty, like the moon surrounded by stars. SB** [**10.29.43**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.43)

He stayed up with the gopīs all night, which became a night of Brahmā (*pra-jāgarāh*). Without staying awake for all that time he would not be able to experience the variety of qualities of billions of gopīs.

*brahma-rātra upāvṛtte vāsudevānumoditāḥ*  
*anicchantyo yayur gopyaḥ sva-gṛhān bhagavat-priyāḥ*

**As Brahma’ night ended, Kṛṣṇa advised the** ***gopīs*** **to return to their homes. Although they did not wish to do so, the Lord’s beloved consorts complied with his command. SB** [**10.33.38**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.38)

His splendid beauty defeated Cupid (*ūrdhva-gaḥ*).

*cakāsa gopī-pariśad-gato 'rcitas*  
*trailokya-lakṣmyaika-padaṁ vapur dadhat*

**He revealed in front of them a manifestation which gave exclusive shelter to the wealth of all types of beauty of all objects existing until his appearance in all the three worlds and even Mahāvaikuṇṭha. SB** [**10.32.14**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/32.14)

He acted on the path of love without no motives (*sat-pathācaraḥ*). The gopīs had no motives in their love for him.

“Why would you give up us gopīs who take you as our very life, who are completely absorbed in you, and who have given up relatives to make you happy?” Their questions to him increased his prema. They asked:

*bhajato 'nubhajanty eka eka etad-viparyayam*  
*nobhayāṁś ca bhajanty eka etan no brūhi sādhu bhoḥ*

**Some people reciprocate the affection only of those who are affectionate toward them, while others show affection even to those who are indifferent or inimical. And yet others will not show affection toward anyone. Dear Kṛṣṇa, please properly explain this matter to us. SB** [**10.32.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/32.16)

His increased love is shown in the following:

*nāhaṁ tu sakhyo bhajato 'pi jantūn*  
*bhajāmy amīṣām anuvṛtti-vṛttaye*  
*yathādhano labdha-dhane vinaṣṭe*  
*tac-cintayānyan nibhṛto na veda*

**But the reason I do not immediately reciprocate the affection of living beings even when they worship Me, O gopīs, is that I want to intensify their loving devotion. They then become like a poor man who has gained some wealth and then lost it, and who thus becomes so anxious about it that he can think of nothing else. SB** [**10.32.20**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/32.20)

*evaṁ mad-arthojjhita-loka-veda*  
*svānām hi vo mayy anuvṛttaye ’balāḥ*  
*mayāparokṣaṁ bhajatā tirohitaṁ*  
*māsūyituṁ mārhatha tat priyaṁ priyāḥ*

**My dear girls, understanding that simply for my sake you had rejected the authority of worldly opinion, of the Vedas and of your relatives, I acted as I did only to increase your attachment to me. SB** [**10.32.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/32.21)

He concluded his answer by saying that he was indebted to them:

*na pāraye ’haṁ niravadya-saṁyujāṁ*  
*sva-sādhu-kṛtyaṁ vibudhāyuṣāpi vaḥ*  
*yā mābhajan durjara-geha-śṛṅkhalāḥ*  
*saṁvṛścya tad vaḥ pratiyātu sādhunā*

**I am not able to repay my debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā. Your connection with me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation. SB** [**10.32.22**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/32.22)

You have worshipped me, cutting off all ties. I cannot respond properly to you. He gave to them his very self, the cause of his life (*prāṇa-daḥ*). This means he was completely submissive to them.

He was praised (*nūyate*) for his affection, conduct and wealth (*pra-navaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *ṛdor ap* (Pāṇini 3.3.57).

In this way he negotiated and performed pastimes (*paṇati*) with the gopīs (*paṇaḥ*).

### Verse 116

*pramāṇaṁ prāṇa-nilayaḥ prāṇa-bhṛt prāṇa-jīvanaḥ \|*  
*tattvaṁ tattvavid ekātmā janma-mṛtyu-jarātigaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**958. Pramāṇaṁ: he was the authority on love.**  
**959. Prāṇa-nilayaḥ: he made the gopīs disappear in him like birds in a nest.**  
**960. Prāṇa-bhṛt: he maintained the gopīs.**  
**961. Prāṇa-jīvanaḥ: he maintained his life because of them.**  
**962. Tattvaṁ: his form, abode and activities were all spiritual**  
**963. Tattvavid: he is eternally endowed with his spiritual svarūpa.**  
**964. Ekātmā: he is the chief entity.**  
**965. Janma-mṛtyu-jarātigaḥ: he is beyond birth and death.**

**Комментарий:**

By these actions he was the authority on conduct on the path of love (*pramāṇam*).

He causes the gopīs who lived by the relationship of sweet affection to him to disappear in him like birds in the nest (*prāṇa-nilayaḥ*). The word is formed according to the rule *pūṁṣi sañjñāyāṁ dha prāyena* (Pāṇini 3.3.118).

He maintained the gopīs who lived in a relationship with him by madhurya-rasa (*prāṇa-bhṛt*).

He was alive because of the gopīs (*prāṇa-jīvanaḥ*).

These intimate activities of the Lord with women were not māyā. *Tattva* refers to one’s svarūpa, paramātmā, and a musical instrument according to Viśva. His activities were not māyā because the Lord’s abode, powers, associates, actions and objects related to him arise from his spiritual svarūpa-śakti (*tattvam*).

He is eternally endowed (*vindati*) with his spiritual svarūpa (*tattva-vit*).

He is the essential existent entity (*ekātmā*). He is the chief ātmā among all ātmās or puruṣottamaḥ, the supreme Lord. *Eka* means chief, other or sole entity according to Amara-koṣa.

He is thus beyond all six transformations such as birth death and old age (*janma-mṛtu-jarātigaḥ*).

### Verse 117

*bhūr-bhuvaḥ-svas tarus tāraḥ sa pitā pra-pitā mahaḥ \|*  
*yajño yajña-patir yajvā yajñāṅgo yajña-vāhanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**966. Bhūr-bhuvaḥ-svas-taruḥ: as a tree, he gives shade to all beings.**  
**967. Tāraḥ: he is the cause of pure liberation.**  
**968. Pitā: he generates all beings.**  
**969. Prapitā: he nourishes them.**  
**970. Mahaḥ: he is full of splendor.**  
**971. Yajñaḥ: he assists the devotee in his worship.**  
**972. Yajña-patiḥ: he protects the worship.**  
**973. Yajvā: he accomplishes the worship.**  
**974. Yajñāngaḥ: he is aware of the worship.**  
**975. Yajña-vāhanaḥ: he instills faith in the worship.**

**Комментарий:**

The supreme majesty of Kṛṣṇa, who is the enjoyer or the rāsa dance, is described. He provides shade for living for all entities in the three worlds (*bhūr-bhuvaḥ-svar-taruḥ*).

But he is also celebrated as the giver of pure liberation (*tāraḥ*). *Tāra* means pure, crossing over and a pure pearl according to Viśva.

He is the origin of all beings (*savitā*) and their nourisher (*prapitā*).

He is splendorous (*mahaḥ*). *Mahas* means festival or bright according to Nānartha-varga.

He is described as the establisher of the path of bhakti with six names.

He assists in the devotee in his worship (*yajñaḥ*).

He protects the devotee’s worship (*yajña-patiḥ*).

He accomplishes the worship by entering into the devotee (*yajvā*). This word is formed according to the rule *su-yajor ṅvanip* (Pāṇini 3.2.103).

He gives the devotee instructions on that worship (*yajñāṅgaḥ*).

He instills faith in the worship so the devotee can perform it (*yajña-vāhanaḥ*).

### Verse 118

*yajña-bhṛd yajña-kṛd yajñī yajña-bhug yajña-sādhanaḥ \|*  
*yajñānta-kṛd yajña-guhyam annam annāda eva ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**976. Yajña-bhṛt: he supports the sacrifice.**  
**977. Yajña-kṛt: he performs sacrifice to teach others.**  
**978. Yajñi: he is the sacrifice. 979. Yajña-bhuk: he experiences the sacrifice.**  
**980. Yajña-sādhanaḥ: he assists the sacrifice by giving knowledge.**  
**981. Yajñānta-kṛt: he gives the results of the sacrifice.**  
**982. Yajña-guhyam: he is the object of the sacrifice.**  
**983. Annam: he gives enjoyment in the spiritual world.**  
**984. Annādaḥ: he feeds the devotee.**

**Комментарий:**

He supports or nourishes the worship by making the imperfect parts perfect (*yajña-bhṛt*).

He establishes the path of niṣkāma-karma. This is explained in six names. He performs worship to teach the people (*yajña-kṛt*). This is famous in the story of bringing back the son of the brāhmaṇa as told in Hari-vaṁśa.

He is the sacrifice, since it is the means to attain him (*yajñ*ī).

He experiences the sacrifice (*yajña-bhuk*).

He assists the sacrifice by giving knowledge (*yajña-sādhanaḥ*).

He brings certain results to the sacrifice (*yajñānta-kṛt*).

He is the chief object in performing sacrifice (*yajña-guhyam*).

Taste and fragrance related to him in the spiritual world, which are obtained by sacrifice, are to be enjoyed (*annam*).

By his will he becomes the food and makes the devotee enjoy it (*annadaḥ*). The word *eva* indicates that no one else does this. The word *ca* indicates all types of objects to be enjoyed.

### Verse 119

*ātma-yoniḥ svayaṁ-jāto vaikhānaḥ sāma-gāyanaḥ \|*  
*devakī-nandanaḥ sraṣṭā kṣitīśaḥ papa-nāśanaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**985. Ātma-yoniḥ: he had the devotees associate with him.**  
**986. Svayaṁ-jātaḥ: he was born by his own will.**  
**987. Vaikhānaḥ: he uprooted the sorrows of his devotees.**  
**988. Sāma-gāyanaḥ: he was the object of singers of the Sāma.**  
**989. Devakī-nandanaḥ: he was the son of Devakī and Yaśodā.**  
**990. Sraṣṭā: he made the earth thornless.**  
**991. Kṣitīśaḥ: he protected the earth.**  
**992. Pāpa-nāśanaḥ: he destroyed all karmas and gave liberation even to demons.**

**Комментарий:**

The devotee associates (*yauti*) with the Lord (*ātma*), enjoying his company (*ātma-yoniḥ*).

*so ‘śnute sarvān kāmān saha brahmaṇā vipaścitā*

**He enjoys all pleasures with the Lord. Taittirīya Upaniṣad** [**2.1.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.1.1)

The manifestation of Mathurā maṇḍala is described. He was born or appeared, full of qualities and parts, by his will alone (*svayaṁ jātaḥ*). This indicates svayam bhagavān Kṛṣṇa.

*sva-śānta-rūpeśv itaraiḥ sva-rūpair*  
*abhyardyamāneśv anukampitātmā*  
*parāvareśo mahad-aṁśa-yukto*  
*vy ajo 'pi jāto bhagavān yathāgniḥ*

**Being compassionate in mind, when his devotees are afflicted by the demons, S*****v*****ayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa, though not having material birth, appears within this world along with all his Viṣṇu expansions, since he is the lord of all forms of God and** ***devatās.***  **SB** [**3.2.15**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.15)

Having appeared, he uprooted the sorrow of his devotees (*vikhānaḥ*). This is formed according to the rule *khano gha ca* (Pāṇini 3.3.125). The word *vaikhaṇa* is formed according to the rule *prajyādibhyaṣca* (Pāṇini 5.4.38). He destroyed all the wicked demons who posed as kings.

He was the shelter of singers of the Sāma (*sāma-gāyanaḥ*). He was the object of their praise.

Where did he take birth? He was the son of Devakī (*devakī-nandanaḥ*). Devakī is the wife of Nanda and the wife of Vasudeva. He was born to both of them. This is well known in the Purāṇas.

*dve nāmnī nanda-bharyāyā yaśodā devakīti ca*

**Nanda’s wife had two names: Yaśodā and Devakī. Ādi Purāṇa**

This is indicated by Śukadeva as well.

*niśīthe tamau udbhūte jāyamāne janardane*  
*devakyāṁ deva-rūpiṇyāṁ viṣṇuḥ sarva-guhā-śayaḥ*  
*āvirāsīd yathā prācyāṁ diśīndur iva puśkalaḥ*

**Then the Lord, complete with all his expansions, who is situated in the core of everyone’s heart, responding to the request of his devotees, appeared from Devakī, non-different from the Lord, in the dense darkness of night, like the full moon rising on the eastern horizon. SB** [**10.3.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.8)

*yaśodā nanda-patnī ca jātaṁ param abudhyata*  
*na tal-liṅgaṁ pariśrāntā nidrayāpagata-smṛtiḥ*

**She understood that the Lord (*****param*****) had been born from her womb. She did not understand the marks of Vasudeva coming (*****tal-liṅgam*****) because she was tired. SB** [**10.3.53**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/3.53)

This is also understood from phrases like adṛśyatānujā viṣṇoḥ: the younger sister of the Lord disappeared (SB [10.4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/4.9)) and nandas tvātmaje: when Kṛṣnā appeared as his son, Nanda was overjoyed. (SB [10.5.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/5.1)) and gopikā-sūta :he was the son of the cowherd woman. (SB [10.9.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.21))

Taking a secondary meaning as in upaguhya ātmajām: Devakī embraced her girl child (which was not actually her child by birth (SB s[\[4.7\] sb-10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/4.7)) is not suitable. The two merged together as one (in Yaśodā’s house). This one person went to Mathurā later and again returned. That is the clear meaning without doubt. The names *yaśodā-vatsala* and *devakī-nandana* are found in the hundred and eight names to indicate he was the son of both mothers.

He made the earth thornless *(sraṣṭā*).

He protected the earth by relieving her of her burden (*kśitīśaḥ*).

He removed all karmas (*pāpam*) of listeners by having them hear about his pure actions and gave liberation to demons he killed after removing their ignorance (*papa-nāśanaḥ*).

### Verse 120

*śaṅkhabhṛn nandakī cakrī śārṅgadhanvā gadādharaḥ \|*  
*rathāṅgapāṇir akṣobhyaḥ sarva-praharaṇāyudhaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**993. Śaṅkhabhṛt: he holds a conch, which produces the five elements including water.**  
**994. Nandakī: he holds a sword known as Nandaka, which presides over knowledge.**  
**995. Cakri: he holds a wheel known as Sudarshana, which presides over fire element.**  
**996. Śārṅga-dhanvā: he holds the Sarnga bow, which presides over action.**  
**997. Gadādharaḥ: he holds a club known Kaumodaki, which presides over the air element.**  
**998. Rathāṅga-pāṇiḥ: he held a chariot wheel in his hand.**  
**999. Akṣobhyaḥ: he could not be disturbed after giving up weapons.**  
**1000. Sarva-praharaṇā-yudhaḥ: all weapons are his weapons.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa as the maintainer and protector of everyone is indicated. He holds the conch named Pāñcajanya, representing water among the five elements (*śaṅkha-bhṛt*).

He has a sword called Nandaka composed of vijñāna (*nandakī*).

He has a cakra called sudarśana represented fire tattva (*cakrī*).

He has a bow named Śārṅga representing kriyā-tattva (action). The word is formed according to the rule *dhanusaś ca* (Pāṇini 5.4.132)

He holds the club named Kaumodakī, representing vāyu-tattva or air element.

Because his nature to protect the devotee is most praiseworthy it is again mentioned. He held the chariot wheel to protect Arjuna from Bhīṣma (*rathāṅga-pāṇiḥ*).

He could not be disturbed or overpowered though he later gave up all weapons (*akṣobhyaḥ*).

This is because he possesses everyone’s weapons (*sarva-praharaṇāyudhaḥ*) since he is the antaryāmī of them all.

Sarvāntaryāmī: he dwells within all beings. Tejobindhu Upaniṣad.

*Om* at the end indicates auspiciousness.

Though there are proofs for each of the names, these have not been supplied for fear of enlarging the work.

### Verse 121

*itīdaṁ kīrtanīyasya keśavasya mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*nāmnāṁ sahasraṁ divyānām aśeṣeṇa prakīrtitam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus the thousand spiritual names of the great soul Keśava, Kṛṣṇa, who should be glorified, have been sung completely.**

**Комментарий:**

I promised to recite the thousand names of Viṣṇu. Now there is a conclusion. The name of the killer of Keśī, (keśavaḥ), or Kṛṣṇa, should be chanted (kīrtanīyasya).

*yasmāt tvayaiva duṣṭātmā hataḥ keśī janārdana*  
*tasmāt keśava-nāmnā tvaṁ loke geyo bhaviśyasi*

**O Janārdana! Because you killed the wicked Keśī, you will be glorified by the name Keśava in this world. Brahma Purāṇa**

The spiritual (*divynānām*) names of the great soul Viṣṇu have been recited by me, Bhīṣma, completely (*aśeṣeṇa*).

### Verse 122

*ya idaṁ śṛṇuyānnityaṁ yaś cāpi parikīrtayet \|*  
*nāśubhaṁ prāpnuyāt kiñcit so ‘mutreha ca mānavaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The human being who hears or sings daily these names will never meet inauspiciousness in this life or the next.**

**Комментарий:**

The secondary results which produce material auspiciousness are described in three verses.

*Idam* means this stotra which has been approved by all the teachers of the Veda such as Nārada, Devasthāna, Vātsya and Sumantu, by all the brahmārṣī who see all higher and lower elements, and by Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa himself, the supreme Lord, seated among millions of listeners (as Bhīsma spoke). Whatever person, including a woman or śūdra, daily hears this stotra, and whatever brāḥmaṇa recites and hears it, will not suffer inauspiciousness of the three miseries in this life and falling to hell in the next life.

### Verse 123

*vedāntago brāhmaṇaḥ syāt kṣatriyo vijayī bhavet \|*  
*vaiśyo dhana-samṛddhaḥ syāt śūdras sukham avāpnuyāt \|\|*

The brāhmaṇa will get knowledge, the kṣatriya will get victory, the vaiśya will get wealth, and the śūdra will get happiness.

The brāhmaṇa will go to the conclusion (*anta*) of the Vedas. (He will understand the conclusion.) The śūdra is dependent. By hearing, with the appearance of knowledge of the truth, devoid of dependence on others, he will become happy.

### Verse 124

*dharmārthī prāpnuyād dharmam arthārthī cārtham āpnuyāt \|*  
*kāmān avāpnuyāt kāmī prajārthī cāpnuyāt prajām \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He who seeks dharma will attain dharma. He who seeks wealth will attain wealth. He who seeks pleasure will attain pleasure. He who seeks children will attain children.

*Kāmān* refers to prescribed sense objects such as taste and form.

### Verse 125

*bhaktimān yaḥ sadotthāya śucis tad gata-mānasaḥ \|*  
*sahasraṁ vāsudevasya nāmnām etat prakīrtayet \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

One should immediately rise from bed and, with devotion, with pure body, with mind absorbed in the Lord, chant the thousand names of Kṛṣṇa.

Three more verses describe material results. The person should be fixed in service to a genuine guru (*bhaktimān*). He should be pure (*śuciḥ*), having taken a bath etc. His mind should be absorbed in the supreme Lord, Vāsudeva, the son of Vasudeva (*tad-gata-mānasaḥ*). He should chant the thousand names, or hear them, according to his qualification.

### Verse 126

*yaśaḥ prāpnMti vipulaṁ yāti prādhānyam eva ca \|*  
*acalāṁ śriyam āpnMti śreyaḥ prāpnMty anuttamam \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He will attain fame and great prominence. He will attain permanent wealth and incomparable superiority.

The results for vaiśyas and śūdras are described in one verse.

### Verse 127

*na bhayaṁ kvacid āpnMti vīryaṁ tejaś ca vindati \|*  
*bhavaty arogo dyutimān bala-rūpa-guṇānvitaḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He will not have fear, and will attain courage and power. He will not have sickness, will shine and be endowed with strength, beauty and good qualities.

The results for the kṣatriya are described in one verse. He will not have fear, and will attain courage (*vīryam*) and power (*tejas*).

### Verse 128

*rogārto mucyate rogād baddho mucyeta bandhanāt \|*  
*bhayān mucyeta bhītas tu mucyetāpanna āpadaḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The person suffering from sickness will be freed from sickness. The bound up person will be freed of bondage. The fearful person will be free of fear. The person in danger will be free of dangers.

General results for all people are described in two verses. He will be freed from chains etc. (*bandhanāt*) and from fear of enemies or wild animals. The person afflicted by dangers (*āpannaḥ*) will be free of dangers (*āpadaḥ*).

### Verse 129

*durgāṇy atitaraty āśu puruṣaḥ puruṣottamam \|*  
*stuvan nāma-sahasreṇa nityaṁ bhakti-samanvitaḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He who with devotion constantly praises the supreme Lord by the thousand names will quickly surpass the worst sinful acts.

*Durgānī* means the most terrible sins.

### Verse 130

*vāsudevāśrayo martyo vāsudeva-parāyaṇaḥ \|*  
*sarva-pāpa-viśuddhātmā yāti brahma sanātanam \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The man who takes shelter of Vāsudeva, who surrenders to him, purified of all sin, will attain the eternal brahman called Kṛṣṇa.

The chief result is now described in two verses. Having given up all sins (*sarva-pāpa-viśuddhātmā*), becoming an exclusive devotee of Kṛṣṇa, the person, devoid of all faults, having a spiritual form, will attain Kṛṣṇa (*brahma sanātanam*).

### Verse 131

*na vāsudeva-bhaktānām aśubhaṁ vidyate kvacit \|*  
*janma-mṛtyu-jarā-vyādhi-bhayaṁ naivMpajāyate \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The devotees of Kṛṣṇa have no obstacles to attaining the results and are free of fear from birth, death, old age and disease.

There are no obstacles in sādhana, while reciting the names. For the devotees of Kṛṣṇa there are no defects in the result—destroying suffering of this life--by not reciting perfectly. Fear from birth, death, old age and disease does not occur, since the recitation destroys all faults and certainly produces liberation.

### Verse 132

*imaṁ stavam adhīyānaḥ śraddhā-bhakti-samanvitaḥ \|*  
*yujyetātmā-sukha-kṣānti-śrī-dhṛti-smṛti-kīrtibhiḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He who recites these names with faith and respect becomes endowed with realization of the self, tolerance, a wealth of renunciation, determination, meditation and fame.

Two verses confirm the previous statements. One should chant with firm faith (*śraddhā*) and respect (*bhakti*). One becomes endowed with realization of the self, with tolerance, with a wealth of renunciation (*śri*), with determination having absence of desire (*dhṛti*), with meditation on Vāsudeva (*smṛti*) and with fame (*kīrtibhih*).

### Verse 133

*na krodho na ca mātsaryaṁ na lobho nāśubhā matiḥ \|*  
*bhavanti kṛta-puṇyānāṁ bhaktānāṁ puruṣottame \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

For the devotee of the Lord who has recited the thousand names, anger, envy, greed and sinful mentality do not arise.

As an elephant flees when a lion roars, by recitation of the names, anger and other dispositions flee. The persons who have recited the names (*kṛta-puṇyānām*) will not have their minds afflicted by sin (*aśubha-matiḥ*). *Puṇya* means what is approved or beautiful according to Amara-koṣa.

### Verse 134

*dyaus sa-candrārka-nakṣatrā khaṁ diśM bhūr mahodadhiḥ \|*  
*vāsudevasya vīryeṇa vidhṛtāni mahātmanaḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The sky with the moon, sun and stars, antarikṣa, the directions, the earth and the oceans are held up by the power of the supremely great Vāsudeva.

The power of the Lord who is praised in the thousand names is described in three verses*. Vīryeṇa* means “by the power of the Lord, which is actually his will.” Thus he is described with his quality of greatness as his svarūpa (*mahātmanaḥ*). The word *vibhu* describes this quality.

### Verse 135

*sa-surāsura gandharvaṁ sa-yakṣoraga-rākṣasam \|*  
*jagad vaśe vartatedaṁ kṛṣṇasya sa-carācaram \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The universe with devas, demons, Gandharvas, Yakṣas, snakes and Rākṣasas, with all moving and non-moving beings, exists under the power of Kṛṣṇa.

*Vartatedam* should actually be *vartate idam.* The sandhi is poetic license.

### Verse 136

*indriyāṇi mano buddhiḥ sattvaṁ tejo balaṁ dhṛtiḥ \|*  
*vāsudevātmakāny āhuḥ kṣetraṁ kṣetrajña eva ca \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

The senses, mind, intelligence, courage, power of senses, power of body, determination, the body and the ātmā are Vāsudeva alone.

The senses etc. are said to none other than Vāsudeva (*vāsudevātmakān*i). This means that Vāsudeva is the establisher of these things.

### Verse 137

*sarvāgamānām ācāraḥ prathamaṁ parikalypate \|*  
*ācāra-prabhavo dharmo dharmasya prabhur acyutaḥ \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

Proper conduct was first established in the scriptures. Dharma rooted in proper conduct was then established. The protector of dharma is Kṛṣṇa.

Do you say that Kṛṣṇa, my maternal cousin, is these material activities? First among all things to appear, proper conduct was established. This refers to bathing, sandhyā worship etc. they were first to be established.

Dharma, rooted in proper conduct (*ācāra-prabhavaḥ*), was established. Dharma refers to agni-hotra, darśapaurṇamāsa sacrifices etc. The protector (*prabhuḥ*) of dharma is Kṛṣṇa (*acyuta*ḥ). He performs actions like a human to establish dharma. He does not perform actions to gain results of dharma.

### Verse 138

*ṛṣayaḥ pitaro devā mahābhūtāni dhātavaḥ \|*  
*jaṅgamājaṅgamaṁ cedaṁ jagan nārāyaṇodbhavam \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

All the sages, Pitṛs, devatās, gross elements, dhātus of the body, all moving and non-mvoing entities in the universe arose from Nārāyaṇa.

In order to show the low quality of the persons opposed to Kṛṣṇa, and their lack of worship of a guru, Kṛṣṇa is shown to be the father and guru of the universe in two verses.

The universe arose from Nārāyaṇa as the nimitta (efficient cause) and the upādāna (material cause). This is shown in Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad.

### Verse 139

*yogo jñānaṁ tathā sāṁkhyaṁ vidyāḥ śilpādi-karma ca \|*  
*vedāś śāstrāṇi vijñānam etat sarvaṁ janārdanāt \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

Yoga, jñāna, sānkhya, mantras, crafts, the Vedas, explanatory works, and understanding the Vedas arose from Kṛṣṇa.

*Yoga* means aṣṭāṅa-yoga related to Viṣṇu. *Jñānam* means the sādhana in jñāna such as *sama* controlling the senses. *Sāṅkhyam* refers to distinguishing the cit from the acit. *Vidyāḥ* refers to secret mantras. *Śilpādi-karma* refers to painting etc. *Vedāḥ* means Ṛg and other Vedas. *Śāstrāṇi* means works which explain the Vedas, such as mīmāṁsa-sūtras. *Vijñānam* means understanding the meaning. All these arose from Kṛṣṇa (*janārdanāt*), the son of Devakī.

### Verse 140

*eko viṣṇur mahadbhūtaṁ pṛthag bhūtāny anekaśaḥ \|*  
*trīn–lMkān vyāpya bhūtātmā bhuṅkte viśva-bhug avyayaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The supreme Lord, Viṣṇu, pervades prakṛti, the separate elements, the three worlds, and enjoys what is offered to him. The eternal being, he protects everyone and is unchanging in his position, qualities and nature.**

**Комментарий:**

One verse describes how the Lord pervades everything. The cause of everything (mahat), which existed before everything (bhūtam) is pradhāna (prakṛti). The separate elements (pṛthag bhūtāni anekaśaḥ) are mahat-tattva, earth, water, fire, air and ether. He made the three worlds and pervades them. He is one (ekaḥ), the supreme Viṣṇu, your maternal cousin. He enjoys what is offered by his devotee (bhuṅkte) and no one else.

*patraṁ puśpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati*  
*tad ahaṁ bhakty-upahṛtam aṣnāmi prayatātmanaḥ*

**If one offers me with love and devotion a leaf, a flower, fruit or water, I will accept it. BG** [**9.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.26)

He is the ātmā who has always existed (*bhūtātmā*). Not only does he pervade everything but he is the protector of all the lords of the universe (*viśva-bhuk*). His position, qualities and nature are unchanging (*avyayaḥ*).

### Verse 141

*imaṁ stavaṁ bhagavato viṣṇor vyāsena kīrtitam \|*  
*paṭhed ya icchet puruṣaḥ śreyaḥ prāptuṁ sukhāni ca \|\|*

**Комментарий:**

He who desires the highest good and happiness should chant these verses concerning the supreme lord Viṣṇu (Kṛṣṇa), praised by Vyāsa.

The person who desires happiness and fortune should recite this work (*stavam*), since that happiness can be easily attained. Desire is the nature of the human being. Śruti says:

*kāmaḥ saṅkalpo vikalpo vicikitsā śraddhā dhṛtir adhṛtir hrīr dhīr bhīr ity etat sarvaṁ mana eva*

**Desire, determination, indecision, doubt, faith, lack of faith, memory, forgetfulness shame, reflection and fear are all the mind. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad** [**1.5.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/1.5.3)

Desire etc. are forms of the mind. By reciting the verses, the desires will be fulfilled since these verses are glorified by Vyāsa. It is indicated that the verses are the best choice.

### Verse 142

*viśveśvaram ajaṁ devaṁ jagataḥ prabhavāpyayam \|*  
*bhajanti ye puṣkarākṣaṁ na te yānti parābhavam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Those who worship Kṛṣṇa, the lord of the universe, unborn, the cause of creation and destruction of the universe, surpass the pains of saṁsāra.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who worship Kṛṣṇa by reciting or hearing these verses do not experience the pains of saṁsāra (*parābhavam*). They go to Kṛṣṇa’s planet and reside there eternally in bliss. They are given liberation and a spiritual body.

*tasyod iti nāma sa eṣa sarvebhyaḥ pāpmābhyah udita udeti hā vai sarvebhyaḥ pāpmābhyo ya evaṁ veda*

**He is called Ut. He has risen above all sins. He who knows this rises above all sins. Chāndogya Upaniṣad** [**1.6.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/1.6.6)

*oṁ āsya jānanto nāma cid vivaktan mahas te viṣṇo su-matiṁ bhajāmahe*

**You, who know that the Lord’s name oṁ is spiritual, should chant it. Ṛg Veda 1.156.3**

*madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ*  
*sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam*  
*sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā*  
*bhṛguvara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma*

**The name of Kṛṣṇa is sweeter than the sweetest, the most auspicious of all things auspicious, the highest fruit in the tree of all the Vedas, and is composed entirely of pure consciousness. O best of Bhṛgu’s dynasty! Heard once with faith or in negligence, it can deliver any human being. Prabhāsa-khaṇḍa, Padma Purāṇa**

*nāma cintāmaṇī kṛṣṇas caitanya rasa vigrahaḥ*  
*pūrṇah śuddho nitya-mukto ‘bhinnatvāt nāma-nāminoḥ*

**The name of the Lord fulfills all desires like cintāmaṇi. It is the very form of Kṛṣṇa. It is full of consciousness and** ***rasa.*** **It is** **complete, pure, and eternally liberated. This is because of the non-difference of the name and Kṛṣṇa. Padma Purāṇa**

In these glorifications of the name, one should remember the faults of interpreting the meaning.

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇeṣu nāma-māhātmya-vāciṣu*  
*ye ‘rthavāda iti bruyas teṣāṁ na niraya-ksayaḥ*

**The persons who interpret the names of the Lord has no cessation of hell. Jaimini-saṁhitā**

*arthavādaṁ harer nāmni sad bhāvayati yo naraḥ*  
*sa pāpiṣṭho manuṣyānāṁ niraye patati sphuṭam*

**The person who interprets the name of the lord is the worst sinner and clearly falls to hell. Since the names are most merciful, chanting the names destroys the thirty-two sevā aparādhas and the ten nāma aparādhas. Kātyāyana-saṁhiṭa**

*tam api deva-varaṁ karuṇākaraṁ sthāvara-jaṅgama-mukti-karām param*  
*aticaranty aparādha-parā janā ya iha tān vapati dhruva nāma hi*

**The merciful name of the Lord, which liberates moving and non-moving beings, cuts down those who offend. Padma Purāṇa**

*jāta-nāmāparādhe tu pramadena kathañcana*  
*sadā saṅkīrtayen nāma tad-eka-śaraṇo bhavet*

**If, somehow or other, one accidentally commits offenses while chanting the holy name, he should constantly chant the holy name. He should fully surrender to the name. Padma Purāṇa**

*nāmāparādha-yuktānāṁ nāmāny eva haranty agham*  
*aviśrānti-prayuktāni tāny evārtha-karāṇi ca*

**The holy name removes the sins, even of those who chant offensively, provided they chant constantly. The holy name gives all results. Padma Purāṇa**

Thus those who desire results, who are very intelligent, should chant without offense.

*aparādha-vimukto hi nāmni yatnaṁ samācara*  
*nāmnaiva tava devarṣe sarvaṁ setsyati nānyat*

**One should with care avoid offenses. O Nārada! One will attain perfection by the name and by no other means. Padma Purāṇa**

May this commentary written by Vidyabhūṣaṇa, “explaining the sweet meaning of the names,” be relished by the devotees, by their mercy, along with the human goals inherent in the names.

Man will attain the highest good by worshipping what person?

What frees one from bondage?

Man will attain the highest good by glorifying the actions and qualities of what person?

Yoga-sūtras of Patañjali.

It should probably by *śyāma-vṛṣabho.*

This refers tothe eye brows; nostrils; eyes; ears; the lips; nipples; elbows; wrists; knees; testicles; loins; hands; feet and thighs.

tataś ca viṣṇunāmāsi dhātor vyāpteś ca darśanāt is an alternative version.

śipayo raśmayo matāḥ teṣu praveśād viśveṣaḥ śipiviṣṭa iho ‘cyate

Perhaps this should be *dadāmy aṭmānam ātmanā* (SB 11.27.26).

Viṣṇu-dāsa made offerings of tulasī leaves and the king made offerings of gold and jewels. The Lord favoured Viṣṇu-dasa, and disguised as a beggar, stole his offerings of rice. When Viṣṇu-dāsa discovered the thief, the Lord revealed himself and took Viṣṇu-dāsa to Vaikuṇṭha, neglecting the king.
